  and method elements, and the sites step 3's flags decline.
Departures from Maglev that TRust's representation forces: the receiver check is a kind tag and
IC_PLAIN, not a map; representation is per value, not per elements kind, so a load checks each
word at its use and a store keeps the kind bits; bounds are on the initialized length (Warp's), not
an Array's length; the prototype and HAS_FAR are read on the cold paths, where V8's map implies
them; the protector is checked at run time until dependencies exist; feedback has no maps, modes or
typed-array kinds, so the receiver set picks the form and a kind-tag dispatch serves mixed or empty
records; the barrier is the owner's card and a large block's dirty bit (JSC's owner barrier, Warp's
whole-cell edge); a typed array's validity holds per effect epoch; growth and copy-on-write are pure
calls with no collection at the call (Warp); an index of -0 is index 0 (Warp, Maglev's tagged path);
an int32 element is f64 bits, so an int32 store is SCVTF and STR D and an int32 use of a load LDR D
and FJCVTZS. Whether the kind tag and IC_PLAIN can change once JavaScript sees an object decides
whether the receiver check is a fact across effects (a value fact) or per effect epoch; the step
verifies it in the code first. Measured at step 5: whether the first stage's in-line paths should
record (JSC's baseline writes its ArrayProfile at every access; TRust's in-line hits record nothing,
so a typed array's kind is known only from the VM's runs and the misses). Tests, under the tier
eager and with exits forced: holes and reads past used with and without the protector, an index
setter installed on Array.prototype mid-loop, Array subclasses, HAS_FAR arrays, appends across
capacity and from the empty block, copy-on-write literals, frozen and sealed arrays, -0, 2^31 and
fractional keys, NaN and -0 into int32 arrays, references into NUMBERS arrays and into arrays over
64 words under GC stress and TRUST_JS_GC_VERIFY, typed arrays detached or shrunk between calls,
each typed kind's conversions; difftest's corpus seeded with the same.
Step 2 landed (9d408d92..9d74051a, 2026-11-09). The library's suites pass with the tier off and on
eager (3,040 each), the tier's tests off and on after the rebase, Test262's Array, TypedArray,
TypedArrayConstructors, assignment, compound-assignment, update, property-accessor and arguments
tests (6,630) in the tier's four configurations with no failure, 18 element tests under GC stress
and verification, and on x86-64 the type-check and the feedback and element tests (208); the
difftest corpus is not yet seeded (carried to the stage's gates). Choices and departures: the kind
proof (tag and IC_PLAIN) is a value fact under a contract the ElemKind doc names, whose one
exception, an embedder's indexed-property install on an object JS holds, leaves the words empty
and meets cold paths that test IC_PLAIN again (a test covers it); TRust's typed arrays share one
kind tag, so a single-kind site also checks the view's kind byte, which V8's per-kind maps imply;
a single-kind typed read past the length reads undefined in line (Maglev's kHandleOOB), a stub or
mixed site's and every typed store's past it take the generic access; typed arrays with no native
kind (BigInt, Float16, shared) take it too, as Maglev's; a site with an empty record branches on
the kind tag instead of staying an effect; the element record keeps a typed array's kinds in its
free bits (feedback-schema.txt). Open: the loop prepass is conservative, so loop-invariant
receivers are checked each iteration; a typed array's length is an accessor left an effect, so
an `i < t.length` loop starts an epoch each iteration (Maglev's LoadTypedArrayLength); UpdateElem
seldom sees int32 records (the VM's fast path records nothing); out-of-bounds typed stores are
generic.

Step 3, the exit profile and reoptimization (design, 2026-11-09; input
target/p3/stage2/reopt-input.txt, which cites the code and the exemplars line by line). Its input
came before step 2's; the two are independent and land in the order they are done. Stage 1 counts
every exit but forced ones toward one total per code and at 100 falls back for good: nothing
records which sites failed, and nothing compiles the chunk with the tier again (code2 is set once
and, settled from birth, has no checkpoint). Point 12 on TRust's code:
- Counting. Each exit record counts its exits, and the code's exit table holds its limit (100 << R)
  and whether it was discarded. An exit that is not forced adds to its record and the total; at the
  limit the code is discarded before the VM run, where the fallback is now. Activations still
  running a discarded code finish in it, and their exits count without triggering anything again
  (JSC returns early, dfg/DFGOperations.cpp:6664-6672).
- The profile. At the discard every record with a count adds its reason as a flag at its key, as
  JSC's tallyFrequentExitSites (bytecode/CodeBlock.cpp:3094-3120). The key is the baseline site the
  second stage's record map names for the exit's pc, on that first-stage chunk and marked whether
  the site was spliced, as JSC keys a site at the exit's semantic origin with its inline kind
  (bytecode/DFGExitProfile.cpp:44-75, DFGExitProfile.h:45-145), so any later compile of the site
  finds it: code2 again, another caller's splice, the callee's own second stage, stage 3's
  inlining. A pc the map names nothing for (guards, calls, block starts, throws) keys on code2. The
  profile lives on the chunk, made with its first flag; R, the function's count of discards, lives
  on its first stage, as JSC keeps it on the baseline CodeBlock (CodeBlock.cpp:2478-2483).
- The decline. The snapshot gathers each pc's flags (code2's own and its baseline site's, a named or
  call flag only from a site of the same inline kind, a value flag from any, JSC's split), and the
  builder declines what a flag names: NotNumber takes the generic operation and NotInt32 the f64
  path; Overflow f64 arithmetic even on proven int32s (JSC's makeSafe,
  dfg/DFGByteCodeParser.cpp:1371-1376); WrongShape checks the site's shapes again where its ways
  grew since the discarded compile, as V8 and SpiderMonkey recompile on what their ICs learned, and
  is an effect where they did not (a fifth shape, an uncacheable receiver), JSC's slow version being
  an IC in optimized code; WrongCallee builds the guard's mismatch edge, so another callee runs in
  place as the template JIT's second stage runs it (JSC's couldTakeSlowPath,
  bytecode/CallLinkStatus.cpp:98-120; V8's call feedback turned megamorphic drops its CheckValue);
  NeverRan builds the block (JSC's InadequateCoverage backstop, DFGByteCodeParser.cpp:7105-7124).
  Much of this also comes from feedback alone: an exit resumes before its failing operation, and the
  VM's run records the value at the baseline site and fills code2's caches.
- The warm-up. A discard installs the template JIT's code, adds 1 to R and arms code2's four
  checkpoints at (100, 400, 1,600, 6,400) << R native entries from the discard, with the
  settled-feedback rule at each and the last compiling whatever its fingerprint: JSC waits twice as
  long after each jettison (CodeBlock.cpp:2602-2608). The recompile generates code2's code again
  with the tier and installs it in place. R saturates at 19, where the warm-up's u32 word would
  overflow, as JSC's cap comes from its counter's range; optimization is never disabled for good.
- Throws. Exits for throws and unsupported operations count, as in stage 1, and flag nothing (there
  is nothing to decline); a discard whose exits were all such arms no recompile, which would build
  the same code, as SpiderMonkey disables Ion for frequent unfixable bailouts (jit/IonScript.h:
  336-341). With the in-place resume, exception exits leave the count as JSC's do
  (bytecode/ExitKind.cpp:31-39, exitKindMayJettison) and this rule goes.
- Epochs. The discard and the recompile each move the call caches' global epoch, as the fallback
  does: a cached call names the replaced code. The exemplars reach only the replaced code's callers
  (JSC unlinks incoming calls; V8 and SpiderMonkey load the callee's current entry at each call).
  TRust's form of that, a per-chunk current-entry word that the direct sequence, which loads the
  callee chunk's word already, compares at each call, is the mechanism the dependencies'
  invalidation points need too, so it is designed with them; meanwhile the log counts epoch moves,
  P2's measured cost of recompiling (P2 plan:6558-6602).
- Departures from point 12, each for a missing precondition: (1) the recompile takes code2 again,
  since code2 is set once and different splicing is stage 3's; (2) flags at unmapped pcs key on
  code2; (3) the hot-loop limit (5 x 2^R when the exited frame then loops hot) waits for stage 5's
  back-edge counters, since template code counts no iterations and the one signal now, a VM run
  that reached a back edge, fires on cold loops as on hot ones; (4) the warm-up counts native
  entries, where JSC also weighs iterations; (5) the unfixable-only rule above.
- Also in this step: two defects the input found. A fallback whose template compile was deferred
  for capacity left its slot unavailable for good, so the chunk ran in the VM thereafter (the slot
  now goes cold and compiles at its next entry); the settled-feedback map's entries outlived their
  chunks in default builds, so a new chunk at a freed address could inherit one (the state moves
  onto the chunk). With the tier on, a stale chain's rebuilt second stage (code3) is compiled by the
  tier as code2 is.
- Switches and log (DIAGNOSTICS.md): TRUST_JS_OPT_NO_REOPT=1 keeps stage 1's fallback for good,
  TRUST_JS_OPT_NO_EXIT_PROFILE=1 recompiles without declines (P2's variants), and
  TRUST_JS_OPT_EXIT_LIMIT=N sets the base limit; TRUST_JS_OPT_LOG gains lines for a discard (its
  exits by site and reason), each flag, each decline, each reoptimization and each epoch move.
- Tests: per reason, the graph with the flag and without it; a flag set through a splice found by
  the callee's own compile and by another caller's splice, an own flag by code2's recompile; end to
  end for each reason (a discard, a recompile at R = 1, no exits after it, the VM's results:
  strings into add, int32 overflow, a fifth shape, a call over four callees, a block that starts to
  run); backoff under types that keep flipping; an unfixable-only discard; a long activation that
  finishes in its discarded code while later calls run the new; forced exits recording nothing. The
  gates gain a configuration that cycles reoptimization: the tier eager with
  TRUST_JS_OPT_EXIT_LIMIT=1.
Step 3 landed (f1c61d77, 2026-11-09). The tier's tests (106) pass with the tier off and on eager,
the library's suites with the tier off (3,000) and on eager (3,001), and the x86-64 type-check of
the library and its tests. Choices the implementation made where the design was silent: "ways
grew" compares the site's own-data way count with the most shapes a discarded check there
expected; a NeverRan flag at a mapped block start applies at either inline kind (only WrongShape
and WrongCallee are kind-bound); the warm-up is armed only on chunks with a record map, which every
chunk the tier compiles has; R grows on an unfixable-only discard too; and the backoff test flips
int32 overflows under TRUST_JS_OPT_NO_EXIT_PROFILE, since types that flip converge after one
discard through the feedback or the profile and never reach the doubling. End to end, NotNumber,
NeverRan, a fifth shape and an uncacheable receiver converge through the feedback alone (the IC
marks the site generic at an evicting or refused fill); Overflow and WrongCallee need the profile
in code2's own recompile, and a WrongShape flag keyed on a first stage declined its site in another
chunk's compile (a stale chain's code3). Untimed; a discard and its recompile move the epoch twice.
Coverage attribution (2026-11-09). Stage 1's measurement joined each sample to the last code the
perf map listed at its address (target/p3/stage1/coverage.py), and Octane frees and maps code
memory again: in stage 1's run, 26,551 of the 42,958 samples it placed in second-stage code lay
where more than one such code had been, 8,582 where a first stage was listed last. The engine now
writes perf's jitdump (TRUST_JS_PERF_JITDUMP, 33e4d523; V8's --perf-prof), whose records carry
CLOCK_MONOTONIC times, and target/p3/stage2/tools/coverage2.py joins each sample of a `perf record
-k mono` run to the code at its address when it was taken; coverage-s2.sh is stage 1's runner on
it. One untimed run of main at 33e4d523 (steps 1 and 3, a non-LTO build): Octane's coverage is
92.6% joined by time, 84.0% by the old join of the same samples (which gives fallback code 8.0% of
them where it held 2.4%), with 298 discards and 261 recompiles; the fixtures' is 88.0% (Vue 87.6%,
React 100%, Djot 91.6%; their steady state 83.8%, 100%, 93.8%), Vue's rest a second stage the tier
refuses for `finally` (8.3% of its samples, 14.6% in the steady state). Stage 1's 46.9% came from
the old join; step 5 measures the bar by time on the fat-LTO engine.

Step 4, free names in line (design, 2026-11-09; input target/p3/stage2/names-input.txt, which
cites the code and the exemplars line by line, and its census, target/p3/stage2/names-census).
Every name operation is an effect in the tier. In Octane's tier code free names ran 1.91 G times,
of two classes: global-object properties (Mandreel 99.7% of its name executions: its heap views and
functions are script-level declarations) and static addresses, the bytecode compiler's hops and
slot (zlib 100%: its asm.js module's own variables); the cache's dynamic modes are 0.2%, stores
and updates 4.2% of a benchmark's on average, typeof of a name none. Codes whole once names and
step 2's elements are lowered: 56.1% of Octane's second-stage time (zlib 100%, Mandreel 99.9%),
against 4.6% today and 9.7% with names alone. Per class, the exemplars' form:
- Static addresses (closure variables; LoadName, LoadNameForCall, LoadNameIn and their caps,
  LoadCap, StoreCap and UpdateCap, at hops 0): the running record (ctx.env_raw, hoisted when the
  chunk has no scope operation), a parent load per hop, the slot; no guard, as V8's context
  slots, SpiderMonkey's GetAliasedVar and JSC's GetClosureVar. A splice's reads start from its
  pinned [[Environment]], the hops folded into the holder at the compile (JSC's SkipScope on a
  constant base). A lexical binding's word is checked not empty, exiting with a new reason, Tdz,
  as SpiderMonkey's LexicalCheck and JSC's CheckNotEmpty do (V8 throws in place, which needs a
  throwing check node the tier lacks); once checked, a (record, slot) is initialized for the rest
  of the function. Facts: parent links are value facts within an activation (the one later write,
  class evaluation re-parenting its fresh outer record, eval.rs:6148-6150, happens after its
  heritage and keys ran, when no running activation's chain holds that record; a test pins it); a
  const binding's value is a value fact once initialized (V8's loaded_context_constants_); let and
  var values are heap facts, reloaded after an effect, as all three reload mutable slots.
  Stores and updates at a static address are nodes: the old word's TDZ check for a lexical, the
  store, the record's card unless the value is a Number (the template's emit_cap_store), as
  Maglev's StoreContextSlot, SpiderMonkey's SetAliasedVar and JSC's PutClosureVar.
- Global-object properties (static globals and the running global environment whose cache is in
  global-object mode): SpiderMonkey's GetGName, a run-time generation guard and the slot's load
  (jit/CacheIR.cpp:3467-3482). TRust's global object changes shape at every addition, so the
  cache's shape proof as an exit guard would exit every reading code after any later global
  appears, where V8 (a cell per property), SpiderMonkey and JSC (GlobalVar addresses) leave
  additions alone. The step adds SpiderMonkey's counter (vm/GlobalObject.h:226-232): a per-realm
  epoch that moves when a property of the realm's global object is removed (removal closes the
  gap and moves later slots, value.rs:11004-11115), turns from data to accessor or back, or is
  shadowed by a later script's lexical declaration (vm/EnvironmentObject.cpp:3891-3895), and when
  the global object stops being ordinary and IC_PLAIN if anything can do that. Additions append
  slots, so a slot found at the compile stays the name's while the epoch holds. The compile
  resolves the name again in the realm's global object (data property, no global lexical of that
  name, the slot's location, the epoch); the code checks the realm (ctx.genv, the template's
  identity test; a value fact per activation, implied in a splice past its InlineGuard), the
  epoch (a heap fact: an effect makes it be tested again, as a call does SpiderMonkey's movable
  guard), and loads the slot (a heap fact per location). Generated code reads the epoch through a
  field it already reaches (ctx.genv, the global object, ctx.interp), or through its address only
  after the realm check proved that realm; no shared stub holds it. A failed check exits with a
  new reason, WrongName, kind-bound as WrongShape is.
- Script-level let and const (a static global whose cache is in scope mode on the global
  environment): the binding read unguarded and without a TDZ test, as SpiderMonkey reads the
  global lexical environment (CacheIR.cpp:3456-3466; the cache fills only initialized bindings,
  and such a binding never returns to its TDZ), if the global declarative record only appends and
  keeps its slots in place; otherwise under the cache's own proof. No workload ran one hot.
- LoadNameForCall's `this` is the constant undefined (the `with` case never reaches the tier), as
  all three call a free name with an undefined receiver.
- The rest stays an effect: the cache's scope, depth-1, layout and deep modes (V8's and JSC's
  generic forms: their proofs test a fresh activation's identity, which as exit guards would exit
  where the template refills in place), global stores and updates (0.8% of Octane's name
  executions; later SpiderMonkey's shape-guarded store), typeof, deletes, References.
- Evidence: an empty cache is evidence that a site never ran, so a block whose sites all never
  ran exits (NeverRan), as V8 deopts, SpiderMonkey bails out and JSC exits at a site without
  feedback; a refused fill leaves a mark no probe matches, so that an uncacheable site is not
  taken for an unrun one, and a deep cache is seeded with the mark. The snapshot gains a view of
  each name site, and the feedback fingerprint hashes the sites' classes.
- The exit profile: WrongName declines to rebuilding the check where the proof differs from the
  discarded code's (the epoch moved, the cache refilled; the profile keeps the epoch's low byte
  as it keeps WrongShape's shapes), else the effect; Tdz declines to the effect, whose template
  throws in place (SpiderMonkey keeps its checks after such a bailout). The declined mask widens
  past 16 reasons (step 2 adds five). The loop prepass learns the lowered forms, so a loop whose
  only names are lowered has no effects.
- Departures, each for a missing precondition: (1) no name folds to a constant (V8's constant
  cells and context cells, SpiderMonkey's ObjectFuse constants, JSC's watched variables), which
  needs dependencies with invalidation points; (2) a let that is never reassigned is reloaded
  after an effect, where V8's parser marks it unassigned, which needs a maybe-assigned flag per
  planned binding; (3) no fact survives a loop header (Maglev's loop effects), a builder-wide
  change that step 5's measurement weighs against the second stage's preloaded loop names; (4)
  the dynamic modes and global stores stay effects (above); (5) TDZ by exit, not V8's in-place
  throw.
- Order: (a) the reasons, the wider mask, the name view, the refused-fill mark and the evidence;
  (b) static loads, own and spliced, with their facts; (c) static stores and updates; (d) the
  epoch in the runtime, then global reads; (e) script-level lexicals; (f) the loop prepass and
  the declines.
- Tests: the builder's graphs (a static read at hops 0, 1 and 2 without guards; a lexical checked
  once per record; a const kept across a call, a var reloaded after one; LoadNameForCall's
  undefined; a splice's reads from its pinned environment; a global read checked once per effect
  epoch; the effect forms; an empty cache as NeverRan; each decline). End to end with the tier
  eager, with exits forced and with TRUST_JS_OPT_EXIT_LIMIT=1: a closure reading an outer let
  before and after its initialization (the ReferenceError at the right pc, inside try and
  without); a const and a var read in a loop whose callee assigns the var; a static store of an
  object into an old record under GC stress and verification (the card); a class whose heritage
  makes and calls a closure that reads outer variables; a global read in a hot loop while another
  function adds globals (no exit), deletes one (WrongName, then a recompile that reads the new
  slot), redefines one as an accessor, and while a later script declares a lexical shadowing a
  configurable global; a function of another realm reading its own globals; typed arrays bound to
  a global and to a module variable, with step 2; sites that first run after the compile; the
  difftest corpus seeded with the same.
Step 4 landed (be68a483..ddc7801d, 2026-11-09). The library's suites pass with the tier off and on
eager (3,077 each), the tier's tests eager with exits forced every 7 and with an exit limit of 1,
Test262's let, const, class, global-code, identifier-resolution and global-object tests (8,792) and
a wider sweep of scope, eval, with, function, assignment, update, typeof and delete tests (5,712)
in the tier's four configurations, and on x86-64 the type-check and the library's suite (2,747); a
difftest corpus of 12 name cases agrees in five configurations but lives outside the tree (carried
with step 2's to the stage's gates); wasm32 is unchecked (no target on the x86-64 host; the one
file it compiles is target-independent). Choices: scope records' addresses are a representation of
their own (Env), which may live across effects within an activation and never enters a frame
state; a store ends its slot's facts on every record (a splice's holder and a chain from the
running record can name the same one); the realm proof is two identity checks, the search record
against the compile's global environment (its address pinned weakly per realm, as the call caches
pin it) and ctx.global_body against the compile's global object; the loop prepass and the builder
read one rule (Rules::lowers), and where a value's facts contradict the rule the builder exits at
the operation, which the profile then declines, instead of taking an effect the prepass did not
count (debug builds panic on one). Departure: the epoch is a u16 in every property map, the
realm's being its global object's, where SpiderMonkey keeps one counter in the global's data: every
slot-moving path is a Props method, the bytes were spare, and it saturates into reads kept as
effects. Q4: the global declarative record does not only append (a script's var and function names
are inserted and removed again by swap, a sloppy eval's deletable bindings removed, its slots moved
to a block when it outgrows its cell), so script-level lexicals read under the cache's proof. Open:
an epoch move makes every compiled global reader in the realm exit until its limit, then
reoptimize (step 3's policy); checks inside a loop are not hoisted out of it (LICM).

Step 6, untagged phis (design, 2026-11-09, pulled forward from stage 4's untagging; input
target/p3/stage2/phis-input.txt, which cites the code and the exemplars line by line, with its runs
in target/p3/stage2/phis-research). The builder makes every phi Tagged and boxes every dirty value
before every terminator, so a loop-carried int32 costs 7 instructions per iteration (its boxing at
the back edge, a CheckNumber and a CheckInt32 at its first use), 11 when it is also an index; the
back end, the allocator's classes, the edge moves, the exit recipes and the verifier already take
I32 and F64 phis. The template's loop chains keep counters int32 in x registers and the rest f64
behind proofs made once per loop entry. Per the exemplars:
- Structure: Maglev's, a selection pass between the build and the verifier over every phi, loop
  headers and forward joins, rewriting inputs, uses and back edges (maglev-phi-representation-
  selector.cc); the builder keeps making tagged phis, its facts unchanged.
- The rule: SpiderMonkey's, from inputs alone (TypeAnalyzer, jit/IonAnalysis.cpp): all inputs
  int32, I32; int32 and f64, F64; anything else, Tagged; inputs only widen (an int32 input of an
  F64 phi converted at its predecessor's end), never a checked narrowing (Maglev's deopt-loop
  rule). Maglev also keeps tagged a Float64-input phi with int32 or tagged uses, for Smis and
  HeapNumbers, which TRust lacks (Decision 5): its tagged phi is its costliest form.
- Liveness: loop phis only for slots live at the header and written in the loop, as Maglev and
  JSC prune by liveness and Ion eliminates unobservable phis; a template that may read any slot
  counts as reading all, and a try region's handlers keep the slots they read; a dead slot is
  Dead in frame states.
- Edges: values cross single-predecessor edges in their representation; words only for phis that
  stay tagged and for tagged uses (a retagging per block, Maglev's EnsurePhiTagged); a phi input's
  forms at its predecessor's end are recorded for the selector (Maglev's alternatives).
- A tagged input on a loop's entry edge (a parameter, a reload, a load) of a phi with numeric uses
  in its loop and untagged other inputs: CheckNumber, and CheckInt32 for an I32 phi, at the end of
  the entry predecessor, exiting at the header's pc with two new reasons, PhiNotNumber and
  PhiNotInt32, whose flags decline the speculation at that header (to F64, or tagged). One check
  per loop entry, the steady-state code of V8's peeled loops and JSC's checks at entry; Maglev keeps
  hoisted untagging experimental for want of a back-off, which the exit profile is. No speculation
  on back edges or at forward joins (Maglev's default outside peeled loops).
- Overflow: an I32 phi's increment keeps its Overflow exit; the decline's f64 arithmetic widens the
  phi to F64 at the recompile, as V8's feedback, SpiderMonkey's IC and JSC's makeSafe do. No Int52:
  f64 holds the integers below 2^53 exactly. No truncated-int32 phis (V8's Turbolev only).
- Allocation: the builder's block order (a loop's body after its header, its exit code after the
  body) and Maglev's loop-phi register hint for the back-edge input.
- Not in this step: Bool and Cell phis (SpiderMonkey's and JSC's; no measured loop carries them
  hot), loop peeling, Maglev's truncation of int32 additions whose every use truncates (Mandreel's
  Overflow exits), immediate operands in the back end's arithmetic (12 of am3's instructions per
  iteration), loop-invariant checks hoisted (LICM) and range analysis.
- Departures, each for a missing precondition: (1) the representation from inputs alone (above);
  (2) the hoisted entry check by default where V8 peels (the precondition: peeling); (3) flags per
  loop header, not per phi (a profile key per header and slot); (4) no back-edge speculation (V8
  checks peeled loops' back edges).
- Estimate (Crypto's counted run, from am3's classified loop): +43.7% against the tier off to
  about +29% with untagged phis, +25% with liveness, +22% with the block order and hints, +9% with
  step 4's prepass fix; zlib and Mandreel about +1% and +3% before names are lowered.
- Order: (a) the allocator's block order and hints; (b) liveness and Dead slots; (c) words for phi
  inputs only, the inputs' forms recorded, StoreSlot and effect operands widened; (d) the selection
  pass; (e) the entry checks, their reasons, flags and declines; (f) the counts again.
- Tests: the builder's graphs (am3's counters I32 with no check in the loop and its dead slots
  without phis; an F64 accumulator; a forward join of int32 and f64; undefined keeping a phi tagged;
  a self back edge; an inner join feeding a loop phi; a loop with a call, its phis in callee-saved
  registers; retaggings; ToBoolean of untagged phis; each decline; the verifier's refusals). End to
  end with the tier eager, exits forced, TRUST_JS_OPT_EXIT_LIMIT=1 and TRUST_JS_JIT_NO_JSCVT: a
  counter passing 2^31 (the exit, then the F64 phi; 2^53 exact); a loop entered with a double and
  with a string where int32s were seen; -0 and NaN through an F64 phi; a swap of two f64 locals;
  exits at the poll and at every check with phis in registers and spill slots; a catch reading a
  loop variable; GC stress; Test262's for, while, do-while, update and compound assignment tests in
  the four configurations; the difftest corpus seeded with the same.
Step 6 landed (dbdd73cd..c23c36a7, 2026-11-09). The library's suites pass with the tier off and on
eager (3,109 each); the tier's tests (230) off, eager with exits forced every 7, with an exit limit
of 1 and without FEAT_JSCVT; Test262's for, while, do-while, update and compound-assignment tests
(1,055) and its try, for-of, for-in, switch, labeled, break, continue, if, conditional and logical
tests (1,401) in the tier's four configurations with no failure beyond off's, and 2,126 of them
(the first group with try, for-of and for-in) eager with an exit limit of 1; on x86-64 the
type-check, Windows' and the library's suite (2,764). The difftest corpus is carried with steps 2
and 4's. Untimed, on non-LTO builds of main (faf2c03e) and the step (target/p3/stage2/step6-impl):
am3's loop runs 102 instructions per iteration (146: no boxing and no check of a phi, against 14
and 28, and 5 moves against 7), montReduce's splice of it 102 (157, whose 18 moves and spill
accesses the block order and the liveness remove); Crypto's count with the tier on falls from
931.1M to 713.0M, against 715.7M off: -0.4% (+30.1% before). One run each of the rest of Octane
with the tier on against main's: Mandreel -11.2%, zlib -4.0%, TypeScript -2.5%, Splay -2.4%,
EarleyBoyer -2.0%, RegExp -1.1%, RayTrace -1.0%, Richards -0.9%, the rest within 0.4% (Splay's
and EarleyBoyer's counts off move by 2.3% and 0.5% between the two engines, which run the same
code there: one run's noise); DeltaBlue is the one benchmark whose count with the tier on stays
above its count off (+5.7%; +5.5% before). Crypto's 11 compiles hold 1,495 nodes (2,065) and
61.7 KB of code (68.8 KB). Choices: the selector (opt/phis.rs) runs inside build(), after the
blocks' renumbering, on SpiderMonkey's optimistic lattice (undecided, I32, F64, Tagged; widened in
block order until none changes, the undecided then tagged); the checks and boxings the rewrites
replace leave the graph, whose nodes are numbered again so that each sits in one block; an entry
edge's state is the header poll's with the header's phis replaced by their entry inputs (a
boxing's operand for a boxing); the speculation's numeric use is a CheckNumber of the phi in its
loop, and a tagged phi's word on an entry edge speculates as any word does, as Maglev's hoisting
takes any input; a decline is listed only where the flag changed the phi's representation;
ToBoolean of an f64 is F64ToBoolean (Maglev's Float64ToBoolean), the builder's too where stage 1
boxed the f64; the liveness (opt/liveness.rs) counts an operation it does not list as reading
none or only its named slots as reading every slot, which answers Q7: EvalExpr's and
AssignTarget's frame projections read them all, the for-of and destructuring steps their hidden
slots. Departures: an untagged phi that is an element store's value is stored as its I32 or F64,
which step 2's store takes, where the design listed a tagged element value among the retaggings
(the kind bits stay exact, as for an int32 form); a retagging sits before the first use of its
block that needs a word, where Maglev's EnsurePhiTagged starts the block and serves the
dominated path. Open: the counters' back-edge moves stay (each increment's overflow exit keeps
the old value in its state, Maglev's output-register rule), so the hints move none of am3's
four; loop-invariant checks stay in their loops (sum's n, am3's receivers' kind checks: LICM);
the flag per header (D4), peeling (Q2), back-edge speculation (Q3), Bool and Cell phis (Q1);
compile time is not measured.

Step 7, immediate operands (design, 2026-11-09). The back end rematerializes every constant operand
into a scratch register at its use (`mov x17, #0x3fff` before `and w4, w3, w17`), where Maglev's
ARM64 code generator takes an int32 constant as the instruction's immediate when the encoding
allows (TryGetAddImmediateInt32ConstantInput and TryGetLogicalImmediateInt32ConstantInput,
src/maglev/arm64/maglev-ir-arm64.cc:30-50, Int32Add and Int32Subtract at 278-306), as Ion's and the
DFG's ARM64 lowerings do. The tier does the same for its int32 nodes: additions and subtractions,
with and without the overflow exit, by a 12-bit immediate or one shifted by 12 (a negative constant
by the opposite operation); AND, OR and XOR by an encodable 32-bit logical immediate; shifts by a
constant count (masked to 5 bits, as ToInt32's shifts are); comparisons by CMP or CMN immediates;
a constant that does not encode keeps today's register. F64 constants that FMOV's 8-bit immediate
encodes take it. The register allocator gives an encodable constant operand no register. Tests: per
node, constants at each encoding's bounds (0, 4095, 4096, 0xfff000, 0x1000000, -1, -4096,
INT32_MIN), encodable and unencodable logical immediates (0x3fff, 0xfffffff, 0x12345), shift
counts 0, 31 and 32 and above, and the overflow exits taken with an immediate operand; Crypto's
counts again.
Step 7 landed (34f1999b, 2026-11-09). The tier's tests and the library's suites pass with the tier
off and on eager (two template tests that count the store helper's calls failed on main as well
when the tier's own stores took over theirs; 1a6a545a pins them to the tier-off pipeline they
test), and the x86-64 type-check; encodings are checked against llvm-mc and every FMOV immediate
on the CPU. Not every node the design names has a form to take: the IR has no int32 add without
its overflow exit, comparisons are not fused with their branches (CheckBounds takes CMP's
immediate), and zero, which no logical immediate encodes, keeps its register. A `>>>` by a
constant count of 1 to 31 drops its sign test, which cannot fail. am3's loop, non-LTO, tier on:
172 instructions per iteration at 3b5e38ec, 158 with step 4's prepass, 146 with immediates;
Crypto's count with the tier on 977.8M before step 7 and 931.2M after, 715.8M off.

Richards (target/p3/stage2/diag-richards). With the tier on, 71% of the run's cycles are in the
template's first-stage code of Scheduler.prototype.schedule (richards.js:188), whose one loop runs
the benchmark's tasks; the tier compiles the functions it calls, never schedule. schedule is
entered once per run, and the second stage's checkpoints count native entries (100, then 400,
1,600 and 6,400 more). With the tier off, its second stage (2 sites spliced, 218,804 bytes) is
built at the first checkpoint, its 100th entry; with the tier on, the settled-feedback rule never
compiles at a first checkpoint (no earlier fingerprint to equal), so the earliest is the second,
its 500th entry, which Octane's two seconds of Richards at the tier-on speed never reach. Alone
in Octane's driver: off 1,665 to 1,673, on 448 to 708, on with TRUST_JS_OPT_EAGER 1,409, off
without second stages (TRUST_JS_INLINE_AT=1000000) 1,053 to 1,078. And in that state the Cortex-X925
mispredicts: 400 fixed runs (fixed-400.js, setarch -R, pathology.sh) on an X925 take 3.12G cycles
with the tier on against 1.24G off, for fewer instructions (6.19G against 7.10G), with 53.2M
mispredicted branches against 1.4M (66 to 100M with ASLR); front-end stalls 569M cycles against
165M, instruction-cache refills 40M against 154M. On a Cortex-A725 the same runs take 1.68G
cycles with the tier on against 2.15G off, 6.0M mispredictions against 4.3M. On the X925, eager:
1.19G cycles and 5.0M; TRUST_JS_JIT_INLINE_STUBS and TRUST_JS_JIT_CHUNK_STUBS: 1.46G and 11.4M;
TRUST_JS_JIT_NO_JSCVT: unchanged. SPE (arm_spe_0, branch_filter, period 2048): with the tier off
and no second stage 961 of 1.19M sampled branches mispredict, no unconditional jump among them;
with the tier on 15,775 of 600K, 6,494 of them unconditional jumps, and the same branches of
schedule's first stage (a `tbz` on a constant slot offset, the unconditional `b` after it, two
more of the same pattern) mispredict on every execution, as do taken branches of the tier's
code (TaskControlBlock.run's, the tasks' run methods), while their neighbours predict. Not code
written or mapped in the steady state (no code loads after warm-up, 0.013 s of system time), not
the codes' data reads, and no 12- to 20-bit address alias or BTB set crowding that the samples
show; the trigger is open. TaskControlBlock.run's polymorphic `this.task.run(packet)` was
discarded for WrongCallee and declined at its recompile, so it calls through the shared stubs.

(+29%: am3's loop, step 6's target) with 7.1M mispredictions against 1.0M; with the stub switches

Order (proposed for the owner's agreement, with the larger regressions above):
8. Back edges counted toward the checkpoints (new): with the tier on, a first stage's loop back
   edges count as native runs toward its second stage's checkpoints and a discard's warm-up, as
   SpiderMonkey's warm-up counter counts entries and loop heads alike
   (BaselineCompilerCodeGen::emitWarmUpCounterIncrement, jit/BaselineCodeGen.cpp:1679-1712), JSC's
   execution counter takes 15 an entry and 1 an iteration (runtime/OptionsList.h:376-377), and
   V8's budget takes a back edge's bytecode distance (InterpreterAssembler::JumpBackward,
   interpreter/interpreter-assembler.cc:1252-1256). Without OSR the code takes the next entry.
   The settled-feedback rule stays: V8's own delay after an IC change is 500 invocations' worth
   of budget (minimum_invocations_after_ic_update), which a loop spends as fast. The tier-off
   configuration keeps P2's entry counts. This ends step 3's departure "the warm-up counts native
   entries where JSC also weighs iterations". Expected: Richards near the eager compile's figure.
6. Untagged phis, as designed (Crypto).
Then the X925 effect measured again; if it remains (Crypto's 7.1M), a reduced case to find its
trigger. Then compile time: per-phase times, the back end's code size per op, Djot's discards and
recompiles; the bar needs Djot's compiles about five times cheaper, or compiling off the main
thread (point 13), the exemplars' answer and a larger step. Then the rest of the list: values
kept across effects (React's steady state), the polymorphic call after its decline (Richards'
TaskControlBlock.run), allocation in line, closures, strings, natives, exceptions, `arguments`
and rest, getters and setters, dependencies.
The owner's order (2026-11-09): step 8 next, then the measurement again.

Step 8, back edges counted toward the checkpoints (design, 2026-11-09). With the tier on, a
template code's loop iterations count as native runs toward its chunk's checkpoints: the second
stage's, where the tier compiles, and a discard's warm-up, where it recompiles. SpiderMonkey's
warm-up counter counts a loop head as an entry: its baseline code increments the script's counter
in the prologue and at each LoopHead, after the loop's interrupt check (emit_LoopHead,
jit/BaselineCodeGen.cpp:2603-2614; the prologue, 7095-7106; the increment, 1679-1712), against one
threshold for Ion (normalIonWarmUpThreshold 1,500, jit/JitOptions.cpp:249). JSC's execution
counter takes 15 an entry and 1 an iteration, against thresholds scaled by code size
(runtime/OptionsList.h:373-377); V8's budget is in bytecode bytes, a back edge charging its jump
distance and a return the function's length (InterpreterAssembler::JumpBackward,
interpreter/interpreter-assembler.cc:1252-1256), against invocations times length
(invocation_count_for_maglev 400). The checkpoints count events against fixed numbers, one counter
for entries, as SpiderMonkey's counter does, so the step takes SpiderMonkey's weight: a loop head
counts as an entry (JSC's weights and V8's byte budget would change what the counter counts).
- Where: at a loop header's poll in template code (the interrupt targets, which every iteration
  of a loop run in canonical code passes, as SpiderMonkey's LoopHead does), at each back edge of a
  register region (a general region's poll guard, a linked scan's), and for a helper-free region,
  whose countdown polls once per 16,384 iterations, the iterations the countdown ran, at each of
  its polls and at its exits. Every template code of a chunk, with the tier on: first stages and
  a discarded code's fallback.
- How: the running record's chunk (JitCtx::chunk) counts while its checkpoint word is nonzero (a
  checkpoint or a warm-up pending), as a direct entry counts, saturating; three instructions per
  iteration with no checkpoint pending, six with one. The next native entry's gate (the direct
  sequence's runs against the word, or H_CALL_HIT) takes the checkpoint as it does today, so the
  loop's function gets the tier's code at its next entry; transfer into a running loop is OSR,
  stage 5's.
- Unchanged: the settled-feedback rule (V8's own delay after an IC change is 500 invocations'
  worth of budget, which a loop spends as fast), the checkpoints' distances, and the tier-off
  configuration (P2's entry counts). TRUST_JS_OPT_NO_LOOP_COUNT=1 turns the counting off, for
  comparisons. Step 3's departure "the warm-up counts native entries where JSC also weighs
  iterations" ends; its limit for an exited frame that then loops hot (JSC's 5 x 2^R) stays with
  stage 5.
- Tests: a function entered a few times whose loop runs thousands of iterations each time gets
  the tier's code by its third or fourth entry with the tier on, and keeps its first stage with
  the counting off; with no checkpoint pending the count stays; the count saturates; a loop in a
  general region, a linked scan and a helper-free rotated loop each count; a discard's warm-up
  reaches its recompile through a loop's iterations; with the tier off loops count nothing; the
  poll and interruption tests unchanged; Test262's loop tests in the tier's four configurations;
  Richards' schedule compiled in its log, and Richards' counts against the eager figure.

Step 8 landed (cb5307ce..97d09da3, 2026-11-09). With the tier on, each loop iteration of template
code counts as one native run toward its chunk's checkpoint word (JitCtx::chunk's: each activation
runs on its own record) while the word is nonzero: at a loop header's poll in canonical code, at a
general region's and a linked scan's back edges after their poll guards, and for a helper-free
rotated loop the iterations its countdown ran (16,384 less x28) at its exit, at its poll's bail and
at each speculation's bail, nothing in the loop. First stages, a discard's fallback and
borrowed-frame (OSR) code count; the tier's code counts nothing; the next native entry's gate
takes the checkpoint as before. Costs: three instructions an iteration with no checkpoint pending,
eight with one (seven once it is due), a rotated loop's thirteen at each way out.
- Departures: (1) the count saturates one below the word, not at u32::MAX: the direct sequence's
  gate adds one in 32 bits, so a word saturated at u32::MAX wraps to 0 there, is stored and loses
  the checkpoint; a loop counts as that gate does (while runs + 1 is below the word), as
  SpiderMonkey's LoopHead compares its count with Ion's threshold (emitWarmUpCounterIncrement,
  jit/BaselineCodeGen.cpp:1756) and, without OSR, has nothing to do past it; (2) so eight
  instructions an iteration with a checkpoint pending, not six; (3) twelve poll, interruption and
  call tests whose loop functions now reach their checkpoints inside the test changed: ten whose
  subject is template code (a region's bails at the poll word, a warmed parent's mapping in an
  Agent test, regions, rotated loops, baked reads, fused String steps, direct returns, a
  hand-published second stage) pin the tier off, as stage 1 did for such tests, and two whose
  callees must tier up before their caller (callee_pickup_tests' polymorphic and megamorphic
  sites) turn the counting off (pin_loop_count_off_for_test).
- Tier off unchanged: with the tier off the template emits main's code. Every code object of 40
  fixed Richards runs and the three fixtures with the tier off is the same instruction stream on
  main (2bf8412c) and the step but for embedded values (109, 162, 61 and 274 of Djot's 303; its
  other 29 are RegExp programs whose literal pools hold Rust function addresses; main against
  itself is byte-identical). Richards' count off is 7,095,419,188 against 7,095,408,444, and on
  under TRUST_JS_OPT_NO_LOOP_COUNT 6,116,476,916 against main's 6,116,458,209, with the same 22
  compiles and 200 exits.
- Tests: the library's suites with the tier off and on eager (3,118 each; one eager run of two
  failed native_stub_tests' a_boundary_natives_author_code_re_enters, 20 of its 40 natives through
  the stub, which ten runs alone and beside the tier's tests pass: open), the tier's tests eager
  with exits forced every 7 and with an exit limit of 1 (232 each), the poll and Agent tests with
  the tier on (51), and Test262's for, while, do-while, for-in, for-of, postfix-increment and
  compound-assignment tests (1,821) in the tier's four configurations with no failure beyond off's;
  removing any one counting site fails its shape's test. On zombie (x86-64): the check with tests
  and Windows' (x86_64-pc-windows-msvc) check clean, and the library's suite passes (2,764).
- Richards, 400 fixed runs, instructions:u, median of 3: off 7,095,419,188; on 5,755,458,807
  (-18.9%); on without the counting 6,116,476,916; eager 6,224,366,744; eager without the
  counting (step 5's eager compile) 6,324,561,226, so on is 9.0% below that figure rather than
  near it. Schedule's log: run 1's loop transfers from the tree-walker into borrowed-frame code,
  which counts to the first checkpoint; run 2's entry takes it and waits, run 3's waits again
  (the code before the loop first ran natively in run 2), and run 4's compiles its second stage
  (2 sites spliced, 440 ops, 524 nodes, 39,268 bytes); one WrongCallee exit a run (pc 63, then pc
  348: the spliced TaskControlBlock.run's polymorphic call) discards it at runs 104 and 305, each
  recompile coming at the next run's entry, whose fallback's loop reached the warm-up's
  checkpoint; no exit after run 306. Without the counting the tier never compiles schedule. Eager
  now takes its first checkpoint at the first native entry, before the code ahead of its loop ran
  natively, and exits NeverRan at pc 0 100 times first.
- Fixtures, the tier on (compiles and the summed compile time the log prints, CPUs 10-13 under
  load, median of 3; counting off equals main): Vue 36 and 8.7 ms, unchanged; React 9 and 3.1 ms
  against 7 and 2.1 (the self-hosted iteration callback loop compiled, discarded for WrongCallee
  and recompiled); Djot 38 and 22.2 ms (4,486 ops, 5,885 nodes) against 33 and 17.7 (3,440,
  4,769): +26%, against the compile-time bar.
- The call microbenchmarks (review, target/p3/stage2/step8-review; the step's release shell, one
  process a case; instructions:u on CPU 10; times on CPU 5, 7 interleaved rounds, 0 s capped):
  each case's loop function is entered twice (its warm-up and the measured call), so its
  measured loop runs in template code with a checkpoint due, at seven instructions an iteration
  (+35.0M on the 5M-iteration cases, +7.0M on the 1M; recursion unchanged). Instructions, on
  against off (main's in parentheses): direct-call +1.2% (-1.6%), closure-call +1.5% (-1.1%),
  method-call -8.0% (-10.0%), arguments-object and rest-params +3.7% (+1.6%), array-push-pop
  +0.6% (0.0%), constructor 0.0% (-1.1%). Times, on against off: direct-call +0.6%,
  closure-call +1.3%, method-call -6.8%, array-push-pop +0.7%, arguments-object +2.8% and
  rest-params 0.0% (36 and 37 ms at the script's 1 ms resolution, the same without the
  counting); on against on without the counting: closure-call +2.0%, method-call +1.7%,
  array-push-pop +0.9%, direct-call -0.6%. SpiderMonkey's baseline code pays its counter at every

- Open: SpiderMonkey emits no increment where a script can never be Ion-compiled
  (maybeIonCompileable, jit/BaselineCodeGen.cpp:1696-1700); Script bodies, loop fragments and
  resumable chunks count here though the tier compiles none of them, seven instructions an iteration

  failure is unexplained (a round run outside the template's code would fit; an eviction under the
  process's code-memory pressure is the agent's guess, unconfirmed).

against 784M, 2.6M mispredictions against 1.0M (step 5: 1,311M and 7.1M), without the counting 968M

The X925 effect's trigger (2026-11-09, target/p3/stage2/diag-navier): new code mapped at the
addresses of code just freed. NavierStokes alone, 400 fixed runs (fixed-400.js) on CPU 5 under
setarch -R with TRUST_JS_GC_PACING=0: every run's code at the same addresses (the tier's log) and
the same 45.76G instructions, but 6.0G to 33.8G cycles and 6.1M to 598M mispredictions, in three
modes (about 6M, 400M and 598M) from run to run; with ASLR the same spread. The runs take 10 ms
each until run 101 and 50 to 79 ms from run 102 on in the slow modes: run 102 is where the tier
discards project (pc 53 NotInt32), lin_solve (pc 35 NeverRan) and a third function (pc 15
NeverRan) after 100 exits each and recompiles each into the pages its discarded code had just
freed (the same addresses in the log): each code object is its own mmap (jit.rs, sys::alloc_exec),
a discard unmaps its code once nothing runs it (release_retired), and the kernel hands the hole to
the next mapping that fits, the recompile's. With an LD_PRELOAD shim that keeps every freed
executable range reserved (shim/noreuse.c: its munmap becomes mprotect(PROT_NONE)), every run takes
5.64G cycles with 1.1M mispredictions; with its variant that frees the pages and keeps the
addresses (noreuse-decommit.c: a fixed PROT_NONE mapping over the range), the same; so the
addresses, not the physical pages. On an A725 (CPU 10) the runs take 8.63G cycles with and
without the shim; off, 7.80G cycles on the X925 and 8.43G on the A725 (32.04G instructions), so
NavierStokes' tier code is 28% faster on the X925 and 2.4% slower on the A725, with 43% more
instructions (open).
Octane through NavierStokes (octane-driver.js, six runs each, ASLR on): without the shim
NavierStokes 2,223 to 12,305 (five of six below 4,500), Richards 1,154 to 1,977 and Crypto 6,088 to
8,528; with it 14,309 to 14,395, 3,209 to 3,262 and 9,235 to 9,290. All of Octane with the shim
(three runs, not under the session's cool-down or cap check): Score 5,687 to 5,746 against the
session's off 4,561 (+25%), every benchmark at or above its off score (Richards 3,220 to 3,258,
DeltaBlue 2,451 to 2,466, Crypto 9,217 to 9,239, NavierStokes 14,380 to 14,395). The shim changes
neither React's and Djot's passes nor the constructor case. This is step 5's open trigger (the
same signature: branches mispredicting at every execution beside neighbours that predict, the
X925 only, coming and going with the code's configuration; Richards' discarded and recompiled
TaskControlBlock.run among its code), and step 8 moved it by moving which code is discarded and
recompiled when.

9. Executable memory from one reserved region (new). V8, SpiderMonkey and JSC each reserve one
   region for code and place code inside it: V8's code range (kMaximalCodeRangeSize 128 or 256 MB
   on arm64, src/common/globals.h:543-545; NormalPage pages, heap/code-range.cc:160-185),
   SpiderMonkey's process executable memory (MaxCodeBytesPerProcess 2,044 MB on 64-bit,
   jit/ProcessExecutableMemory.h:26, 64 KB pages and pools: ExecutableCodePageSize, :65) and
   JSC's fixed pool (fixedExecutableMemoryPoolSize 128 MB on arm64 without jump islands, 512 MB
   with, 1 GB on x86-64: jit/ExecutableAllocator.cpp:92-105). In none of them does a recompile
   land on its invalidated predecessor: they bump-allocate (ExecutablePool::alloc,
   jit/ExecutableAllocator.cpp:69-78; a pool is freed only when all its code is dead,
   :32-57), and they free invalidated code only when the GC finalizes it (JitCode::finalize,
   jit/Ion.cpp:662-694; V8's and JSC's code objects are the GC's as well), so its addresses
   stay taken until well after the recompile. Here every code object is its own mapping, freed
   as soon as nothing runs it, and the next mapping takes the hole.
   - The design: on Linux (aarch64 and x86-64) and Windows, executable memory comes from one
     region per process reserved at the first allocation (1 GiB of addresses with no access,
     or twice the code budget if TRUST_JS_JIT_CODE_BUDGET_MB sets more than 512): a bitmap of its
     pages and a cursor. An allocation takes the first run of free pages at or after the cursor,
     wrapping at the region's end, commits it writable, copies, flushes and protects it
     executable as now, and moves the cursor past it; a free decommits its pages (Linux: a
     fixed no-access mapping over them; Windows: MEM_DECOMMIT) and clears their bits. Each code
     object keeps its own pages, so the W^X protections stay per object as now. All executable
     code takes it: the template JIT's, the tier's, shared stubs, native RegExp programs.
   - Departure: SpiderMonkey's cursor moves back to a freed page "so we can reuse pages instead
     of fragmenting the whole region" (ProcessExecutableMemory::deallocate,
     jit/ProcessExecutableMemory.cpp:881-885), and its frees come from the GC's finalization,
     well after an invalidation. Here a discard frees its code before its recompile, so a cursor
     moving back would hand the recompile its predecessor's pages, the trigger above. The cursor
     never moves back: a freed page is taken again only after the cursor has passed the rest of
     the region, a GiB of later code, which stands in for the exemplars' wait for the GC.
     SpiderMonkey's random skip of a page (allocate, :792) is a hardening the other two lack;
     not taken.
   - macOS keeps its MAP_JIT mapping per object (its JIT write protection and its MAP_JIT rules
     make a region another design; no machine here measures it).
   - When the region cannot be reserved (an address-space limit) or has no free run long
     enough, an allocation maps its own memory as now. The budget's accounting is unchanged
     (live requested bytes). The region is shared by the process's threads under a lock.
   - Expected: NavierStokes' 400 fixed runs at the shim's 5.64G cycles in every run, Octane at
     the shim's scores (Score near 5,700, Richards near 3,250), the A725's counts unchanged, and
     the shim, run on the step, changing nothing.
   - Tests: an allocation after a free of the same size gets other pages while the region has
     them; the cursor wraps and reuses freed pages; a region with no free run long enough falls
     back to an own mapping; allocations from several threads; code in the region runs, and
     each page's protection is its object's (/proc/self/maps on Linux); the library's suites,
     the tier's four configurations and the RegExp tests on Crystal; zombie's x86-64 suite and
     Windows' check (and its tests under Wine if they run there).
   - Measurement: the reduced case's counts (X925 and A725), Octane through NavierStokes, then a
     session and the coverage run on the step.
The order (proposed): step 9 now (the trigger the measurement's plan asked for, and the largest
open regression); then compile time as ordered (2026-11-09), React's and Djot's passes with it;
then NavierStokes' instructions on the A725, constructor's stalls and the list.

The compile-time investigation (2026-11-10, target/p3/stage2/compile-diag; a probe counting each
phase's instructions and cycles, branch p3-compile-probe, unlanded). The log's time is generate's;
of its user cycles on Djot: the register allocator 23.7%, emission 20.0% (2.6 points a CFG
rebuild), the Builder 19.2% (17 hash maps of facts cloned at each branch and met at each join;
an element load's typed-against-words diamond), the verifier 9.9% (3.6 points its own CFG), phi
selection 7.3% (a whole-graph copy), the CFG 4.7%, initialized reads 3.1%, slot liveness 3.1%,
the snapshot 2.0%, finishing 4.5%; malloc and free 13.7% across them, Vec growth 8.5%, hash maps
6.7%. The CFG is built three times a compile (build.rs:111, verify.rs:33, a64.rs:128) and the
initialized reads twice. About 3,200 cycles a node, near linear (29K cycles plus 3,200 a node);
React and Vue alike. Outside generate, per Djot run: second stages' bytecode recompiles 3.0M
cycles against generate's 17.6M (21 of 37 plans splice nothing and still recompile), the
fingerprints 0.86M, the discards' fallback template compiles about 1.6M. Of Djot's compile
cycles, 21.5% go to three compiles later discarded, 5.8% to two recompiles, 28.2% to four
compiles on unsettled feedback at the last checkpoint, 9.4% to three whose code never ran; the
tier's code ran 17.2M cycles in all against about 20M to compile it. With the first checkpoint
at 400 (TRUST_JS_INLINE_AT=400; V8 tiers to Maglev at 400 invocations' budget) Djot compiles 20
functions in 7.4 ms against 38 in 15.1, and its measured pass runs 1.1% faster than the tier off
against 6.4% slower. V8's Maglev on this machine and CPU (node, Maglev only, its trace): 13.0 ms
of Djot's 35 ms pass compiling (37%) on one CPU; 23 ms for the pass on two CPUs with Maglev's
concurrent compiles. The clock: the X925 cores deliver 1.37 GHz under load (a spin loop's cycles
over task-clock; cpuinfo_avg_freq; CPPC's feedback counters since boot, 1.39 GHz on average),
not 3.9 GHz, with thermald at state 0 and scaling_max_freq at 3.9 GHz, which is all uncapped.sh
reads; the A725 cores deliver 1.15 of 2.81 GHz. Every session since at least 2026-11-05 ran so
(Richards off 1,656 to 1,670 in each), so their ratios stand.

10. Compile time, first part (new): three changes that leave the tier's code identical.
   - The verifier runs in debug builds (the library's tests) and, in release builds, only under
     TRUST_JS_OPT_VERIFY=1, as Maglev verifies only #ifdef DEBUG (VerifyGraph,
     maglev/maglev-compiler.cc:74-80), SpiderMonkey's AssertGraphCoherency is empty without
     DEBUG (jit/MIRGraph.cpp:1812-1822) and JSC validates with assertions or the validateGraph
     option, false by default (validationEnabled, dfg/DFGCommon.h:88-95;
     runtime/OptionsList.h:183-184).
   - One CFG and one set of initialized reads a compile, made with the graph and handed to the
     verifier and the emitter, as Maglev's graph builder keeps the one BytecodeAnalysis it
     made (maglev/maglev-graph-builder.h:1775-1777, 1818).
   - The register allocator's internals, its decisions unchanged: register pools as bitmasks,
     as Maglev's register state keeps its free registers (maglev/maglev-regalloc.h:57-94),
     frame-state values listed once, flat next-use lists, no allocation in each pass, and an
     index for live_at in place of its scan of every value at each slow-path call.
   - Expected (the probe's counts): generate's cycles on Djot -22% (15.0M against 19.3M a run),
     React -19%, Vue -21%; the code identical (masked code hashes equal).
   - Tests: the tier's tests in its four configurations and the library's suites (debug: the
     verifier on); code hashes before and after for the three fixtures, Richards and Crypto's
     fixed runs and the tier's tests; the fixtures in release under TRUST_JS_OPT_VERIFY=1.
   These with the further single-thread work estimated (one forward pass for the allocator's
   live ranges and next uses, as Maglev's; an arena for the IR; frame states shared until an
   effect; phi selection without its copy; copy-on-write facts) take Djot to about 10% of its
   pass, and with a tier-up policy nearer V8's (thresholds scaled by size, no compile on
   unsettled feedback at the last checkpoint, second stages that splice nothing built from the
   first stage) to about 4%. The bar, 3% of a fixture's measured pass on one pinned CPU, is past
   what Maglev does here (37%); the exemplars meet their budgets by compiling off the main
   thread (V8's prepare, execute and finalize, compiler.h:395-401; SpiderMonkey's off-thread
   Ion, which it turns off with one CPU, jit/Ion.cpp:2110-2118; JSC's useConcurrentJIT, on by
   default, runtime/OptionsList.h:291). Which CPU model the bar assumes is the owner's (asked
   2026-11-10).

Step 9 landed (709e8a1b..1ab94b0e, 2026-11-10). On Linux and Windows executable code comes from
one region of addresses reserved at the first allocation (jit_code_region.rs): max(1 GiB, twice
the code budget), a bitmap of its pages and a cursor under a lock. An allocation takes the first
free run at or after the cursor (wrapping, never across the end), commits it with SpiderMonkey's
flags, publishes (copy, flush, RX: one `publish` now shared with the own-mapping path; the two
Linux modules merged, x86-64's flush empty), and moves the cursor past it, for every size
(SpiderMonkey moves it only past runs of two pages or fewer); a free decommits, then clears the
bits; the cursor never moves back; no random skip. Bits are taken under the lock and committed
outside it, as SpiderMonkey's. macOS keeps its mapping per object (an uninhabited CodeRegion
stub there); an ExecutableBuffer remembers where its pages came from.
- Departures: (1) the decommit's fixed no-access mapping adds MAP_NORESERVE, as the reservation
  has, so a freed range merges back into its no-access neighbours (/proc/self/maps shows one
  ---p mapping again after all frees) rather than staying a mapping of its own against
  vm.max_map_count; (2) failures: a commit the system refuses leaves its pages taken for good (a
  kernel that unmaps before it maps could leave another mapping there), a refused protection
  decommits and releases, a refused decommit leaks its pages where SpiderMonkey crashes, and any
  failure in the region falls back to a mapping of its own.
- Counts (fat-LTO builds of main 9107efbc and the step from git-archive exports; NavierStokes'
  400 fixed runs, setarch -R, TRUST_JS_GC_PACING=0, the tier on; target/p3/stage2/step9-impl,
  measure.out): CPU 5, main 5.786G, 5.760G, 7.768G and 7.353G cycles (1.7M, 1.6M, 40.1M and 33.8M
  mispredictions), the step 5.634G to 5.661G (1.13M to 1.28M), main with the shim 5.642G to
  5.645G, the step with the shim 5.632G to 5.635G (the shim changes nothing on the step); the
  same 45.762G instructions (the step +28K). Tier off 7.793G and 7.797G. CPU 10 (A725) 8.633G on
  both. Octane through NavierStokes, four runs each alternating, CPU 5, ASLR on (CPUs 5 and 10
  capped 0 of 299 s): main Richards 1,280 to 2,595, Crypto 6,335 to 8,644, NavierStokes 3,304 to
  12,280; the step 3,329 to 3,336, 9,229 to 9,265 and 14,323 to 14,395.
- Tests: the library's suites with the tier off and on eager (3,126 each), the tier's tests eager
  with exits every 7 and with an exit limit of 1 (232 each), the region's 8 tests (100 repeats),
  Test262 in the tier's four configurations (53,573 of 53,575 each; the two staging/sm failures
  as gate 1's), clippy clean but main's single_range_in_vec_init (opt/a64_tests.rs, since step 6).
  The RegExp tests under TRUST_JS_REGEXP_TIER_UP_AT=1 and TRUST_JS_REGEXP_NATIVE_VERIFY=1: 146
  pass and deeply_nested_patterns_compile_on_a_small_stack_or_fail_cleanly overflows its 256 KiB
  thread there, as on main (it passes at the default tier-up); allocation_failure's
  exhausted_memory_throws_range_error_for_array_buffers fails as on main in every tier (under
  its 192 MiB limit the region is refused and code maps its own, as on main). On zombie
  (x86-64): the check and clippy clean, the library's suite passes (2,772); Windows' check clean
  but date.rs's 20 warnings, and its tests under Wine pass but the three snapshot codec tests that
  fail on main's Windows build under Wine as well (2,763 of 2,766; the region's 8 pass).
- Open: after the cursor's first wrap a page freed just ahead of it is taken when the cursor
  reaches it, however recent the free; a full Octane run commits 34,104 pages in 4,930 commits
  (13% of the region), so the first wrap comes after about seven Octane runs in one process. The
  1 GiB reservation counts against address-space limits (a process under one maps its own).

Step 10 landed (4d34bdb8..a38b3dd8, 2026-11-10). (1) driver::verifies: the verifier runs where
cfg!(any(test, debug_assertions)) holds or TRUST_JS_OPT_VERIFY is set (to any value, as the
tier's other switches), documented in DIAGNOSTICS.md; a rejected graph still panics in tests and
debug builds and is refused in release. (2) build::build returns Built { graph, cfg, initialized
}, which verify and a64::compile take, the code generator keeping a reference
(TRUST_JS_JIT_NO_TDZ_PROOF as before); tests on hand-built graphs make the chunk's Cfg in a
helper. (3) The allocator: bit-mask register pools with the same lowest-register choice, each
frame state's values and each edge's phi inputs listed once, flat bit sets with reused scratch,
flat sorted next-use lists, no allocation in the passes; live_at reads an index (ranges sorted by
start with a tree of their latest ends, built at the first query), and the old scan stays as the
tests' reference (live_at_scan), which assert_consistent compares with the index at every node of
every graph the allocator's and back end's tests allocate.
- Code identical: masked code hashes (setarch -R), main against the step: Djot 39 compiles (38
  and a refusal), React 9, Vue 38 (36 and 2 refusals), Richards' 400 fixed runs 25, Crypto's 40
  fixed runs 38, the tier's 155 module tests one thread 426: each sequence identical, outputs
  equal. Release under TRUST_JS_OPT_VERIFY=1: the fixtures, Richards and Crypto verify every graph
  with no failure, the same compiles and refusals, the same outputs.
- Compile cost (generate's user counts a run, medians, CPU 8; target/p3/stage2/step10-impl):
  Djot 48.58M instructions and 19.28M cycles against 33.94M and 14.82M (-30.1%, -23.1%; 20 runs),

  10.16M and 5.01M (-31.2%, -23.0%; 10); min-max ranges apart.

The order (proposed): step 11, the tier-up policy nearer V8's, from a measured input
(target/p3/stage2/policy-input.txt: a census of each compile's runs, entries and loop iterations,
and which feedback moved before the unsettled compiles; the variants measured). Maglev's budget is
400 invocations' worth of executed bytecode (a back edge spends its loop's length, a return the
bytes before it: interpreter-assembler.cc JumpBackward, UpdateInterruptBudgetOnReturn;
tiering-manager.cc InterruptBudgetFor), where step 8 counts an iteration as a whole entry; an IC
change raises the budget to 500 invocations' worth (NotifyICChanged) and nothing compiles while the
ICs change, where the tier compiles at its fourth checkpoint whatever the fingerprint says
(JSC's maximumOptimizationDelay). Then the further single-thread compile work (step 10's list) or,
if the owner's answer on the bar is two CPUs, compiles off the main thread (plan point 13); then
NavierStokes' instructions on the A725, Crypto's churn and the frame limit's refusal of zlib's a8.
Correction (2026-11-10): the 8.3 points of Octane's second-stage samples that the coverage above
gives to zlib are a8's (<anonymous>:1:126768, 2,462 operations), in template code the log labels
a second stage; the refusal for a frame over the record's 256 words is another function's, a1
(<anonymous>:1:70112, 21,869 operations), after 2.36 ms. Why a8 runs template code there is open
(the census's a8 is compiled, discarded after 100 exits and recompiled).

The owner's answer on the bar (2026-11-10): the compile-time bar assumes two CPUs, the tier
compiling off the main thread (point 13), as V8, SpiderMonkey and JSC do. Proposed with it, for
the owner's agreement: the 3% counts every part of a compile that stays on the main thread (the
snapshot, the bytecode recompile that makes the tier's chunk, the install), of which the
sessions' figure, generate's time, leaves out the recompile (3.0M cycles a Djot run, 2.9% of its
pass); and the reference runs (node, node-mg) take the same two CPUs (node-mg's Djot pass 35 ms
on one CPU, 23 ms on two). With one CPU the tier compiles on the main thread, as SpiderMonkey
does (jit/Ion.cpp:2110-2118).

Step 11, the tier-up policy of point 2 (design, 2026-11-10; input
target/p3/stage2/policy-input.txt: a census of every compile on the fixtures, Octane's fixed runs
and Richards' and Crypto's marked runs, and policy variants, each measured in its probe binary
against that binary's default; branch p3-policy-probe, unlanded). V8 (63481c19) gives
Maglev a budget of invocation_count_for_maglev (400; flags/flag-definitions.h:1126) times the
bytecode's length (execution/tiering-manager.cc:187-195, 264-290), drawn down by the bytecode
executed: Ignition charges the whole length at each entry (InterpreterEntryTrampoline,
builtins/arm64/builtins-arm64.cc:1427-1433) and a back edge its loop's length
(InterpreterAssembler::JumpBackward, interpreter/interpreter-assembler.cc:1252-1256); Sparkplug
charges at a return the bytes through it and at a back edge its loop's length
(baseline/baseline-compiler.cc:2905-2913, 2666-2671). An IC change raises the budget to at least
minimum_invocations_after_ic_update (500; flag-definitions.h:1150) times the length
(NotifyICChanged, tiering-manager.cc:497-576): a property IC's monomorphic, polymorphic and
megamorphic configurations notify (ic/ic.cc:346-430, 661), a megamorphic IC's cache updates do
not (:1061-1072), nor do call and binary-operation feedback (builtins/ic-callable.tq:22-50;
codegen/code-stub-assembler.cc:13851-13879). When the budget runs out the function compiles,
whatever its feedback (MaybeOptimizeFrame, tiering-manager.cc:339-432): nothing is forced, since
nothing waits but the budget. A function over max_optimized_bytecode_size (60 KB;
flag-definitions.h:1557) gets the largest budget and is never optimized (tiering-manager.cc:
282-283). The tier: checkpoints at 100, 500, 2,100 and 8,500 native runs, an entry and a loop
iteration each counting one (step 8, SpiderMonkey's weight); it compiles at the first checkpoint
whose fingerprint equals the previous one's, and at the fourth whatever the fingerprint (JSC's
maximumOptimizationDelay).
- The census (the default policy, one run each): Djot 38 compiles in 17.25 ms (CPU 10, median
  of 5), 4 of them at the fourth checkpoint on feedback that had really changed (code running for
  the first time, call sites going polymorphic or megamorphic; 4.47 ms); over the 20 workloads,
  116 compiles on unsettled feedback, 47 of them changed only by refills of sites past their
  capacity (generic named sites, megamorphic call sites; all TypeScript's); 359 of the 1,421
  compiles at a checkpoint reached it by runs at least half loop iterations (503 of 967 ms).
- The variants (Octane's fixed runs' instructions, the fixtures' cycles and generate's time on
  CPU 10): (a) V8's weight, Ignition's: Octane -0.7 to -0.8% (DeltaBlue -6.8%: long functions
  with short loops no longer compile; Mandreel's initPhysics, 35.6 ms, among them), Djot's
  generate -6 to -10% and its measured pass -1.2 to -2.0%, Vue and React unchanged; (r)
  Sparkplug's weight, at returns, with a: Octane -0.07% against a, React's measured pass +2.2%
  instructions (its reducer returns early and compiles after 3,264 calls in place of 2,100),
  compiles never entered 28 to 13, the call microbenchmarks within 0.3%; (b) nothing forced, the
  gaps 4x past the fourth checkpoint: Djot's passes 2-10 +10.7% (its four functions never
  compile), TypeScript +13.5%; (B) nothing forced, a checkpoint every 500 runs past the fourth
  (V8's wait): Octane +0.07%, Djot's passes 2-10 +0.3%; (c) the refills of sites past their
  capacity left out of the fingerprint (V8's megamorphic rule): TypeScript -1.4%, Djot's generate
  +7% and its passes 2-10 -1.5%; aB -0.73% and aBc -0.88% (TypeScript -2.0%; Djot's measured
  pass +0.8%, its passes 2-10 -1.5%, generate +8%); (u) a checkpoint every 500 runs from the
  first, nothing forced (V8's wait at the checkpoints' grain): Octane +1.3 to +1.6% (DeltaBlue
  +32 to +35%: functions compiled at the windows of 1,000 and 1,500 runs are discarded, 1,701
  exits and 22 epoch moves in its 5 runs against a's 460 and 6), Djot's generate +10 to +32%,
  and without c TypeScript's churning functions fingerprinted every 500 runs (4,311 fingerprints
  in 114 ms against 1,612 in 22); (v) only V8's notifications in the fingerprint, no call ways
  or value records: Djot 54 compiles and 10 discards against 38 and 3; the first checkpoint at
  400 (e400): Djot's generate -52%, but Vue's measured pass +2.5% and Richards +5.0%. No variant
  near V8's rule moves Djot's compile time by more than a tenth: the bar's answer is point 13.
  The settled rule's own cost: Djot's 156 fingerprints take 1.10 ms a run (6.2% of generate).
The step, with the tier on (off, P2's checkpoints and counts stay):
1. V8's weight, Ignition's. The runs word counts in 64ths of an invocation (K = 64): an entry adds
   K (the direct sequence's gate, H_CALL_HIT, note_native_entry, the slice and OSR entry; the
   construct sequence's take-back K); a loop's back edge adds max(1, round(K x B / L)), B the
   loop's span (its header to its last back edge), L the chunk's operations (jit_ops), the
   weights computed where compile_entry arms the counting and kept per header beside it (a
   rotated loop's countdown adds its iterations times its weight); every distance is K times as
   long. A loop-free function keeps its schedule; a long function with a short loop needs as
   many iterations as V8's budget would.
2. Nothing forced: past the fourth checkpoint the checkpoints go on every 500 runs (x K,
   minimum_invocations_after_ic_update), so a function compiles at the first such window its
   feedback does not change in, and one whose feedback never settles keeps its template code; a
   discard's warm-up likewise, (100, 400, 1,600, 6,400) << R, then every 500 << R. The counts
   saturate below the word, and a checkpoint past u32::MAX is not armed. R's cap falls from 19 to
   13, where the warm-up's fourth distance, 409,600 << R, still fits the word (profile.rs's
   argument, as JSC's cap comes from its counter's range).
3. V8's notifications in the fingerprint: a generic named site counts its generic mark and not
   its ways, a megamorphic call site its state and not its ways (ic.cc:1061-1072).
4. V8's size limit: the tier refuses a chunk of more than 15,000 operations before its snapshot
   (60 KB at three to six bytes of V8's bytecode an operation: Mandreel's initPhysics, 19,580
   operations and 66,944 bytes in node 24, which V8 never optimizes and the default policy
   compiles in 35.6 ms; zlib's a1, 21,869 operations, refused now for its frame after 2.36 ms). A
   refusal keeps today's second stage, as every refusal does.
- Departures, each measured: (1) Ignition's weight, not Sparkplug's at returns, the template
  code's analog: r costs React's reducer 1,164 more calls in template code (React's measured pass
  +2.2%) and moves nothing else by more than 1%. (2) The four checkpoints stay before V8's
  500-run windows: u's earlier compiles are discarded where V8's would deoptimize once (V8
  invalidates code at its first deoptimization), the tier's discard taking 100 << R exits in the
  VM and moving the call caches' epoch (step 3, after JSC's exit counts); the widening windows
  are JSC's delays, which go with JSC's exits. When a discard costs what V8's does (an exit that
  invalidates, no global epoch move), u again. (3) The fingerprint keeps call ways and value and
  element records, which V8 does not notify (v's compiles and discards). (4) Sampled at the
  checkpoints, not notified: a notification at every fill would put a test into template code's
  record updates; a fingerprint a window costs Djot 1.10 ms a run.
- Unchanged: the tier off (P2's checkpoints and counts); exits, discards and the warm-up's start
  (step 3); the snapshot and the code.
- Tests: a loop-free function's tier code at its 500th entry with settled feedback, as now; a
  function of L operations with one loop of span B compiles after about 500 x L / B iterations,
  a nested loop's back edge charging its own span, a rotated loop's countdown its iterations at
  its exits; a function whose feedback changes until its fourth checkpoint compiles at the first
  quiet 500-run window after it, settled, and one whose feedback changes in every window never
  compiles; a generic named site's and a megamorphic call site's refills leave the fingerprint
  unchanged and their transitions change it; a chunk over 15,000 operations is refused before its
  snapshot and keeps its second stage; the counts saturate below the word, no checkpoint past
  it, R capped at 13; the tier off unchanged (a second stage at the 100th entry); Richards'
  schedule compiled in run 4; the library's suites with the tier off and on, the tier's tests in
  its configurations.
- Expected (binary 2's aBc and binary 1's a, against their defaults): Octane's fixed runs -0.9%
  (TypeScript -2.0%, DeltaBlue -6.8%, Crypto and RegExp -1.1%), Vue and React within 1%, Djot's
  passes 2-10 -1.5% and its measured pass about +0.8% with 8% more generate (functions that only
  churned now settle and compile, a cost that leaves the main thread with point 13), no compile
  on unsettled feedback; Richards' schedule in run 4; Crypto 33 compiles and 7 discards in 40 runs.
The order after it: the tier's chunk made without the bytecode recompile where its plan splices
nothing (21 of Djot's 37 plans), as Maglev compiles from the function's own bytecode; then
compiling off the main thread (point 13).

The owner's answers (2026-11-10), both as proposed: the 3% counts every part of a tier compile that
runs on the main thread (the bytecode recompile that makes the tier's chunk, the snapshot,
generate while it stays there, the install, and the template JIT's compile of a discarded code's
fallback), each logged under TRUST_JS_OPT_LOG from step 11 on; and the reference runs take the
same two CPUs as the engine ("otherwise we're cheating"). From the next session, every engine
and reference run (main, base, off, node, node-mg) is pinned to two X925 cores (CPUs 5 and 6),
both checked uncapped; the tier-off engine is single-threaded and loses nothing by it, and until
point 13 neither is the tier. Point 13's design will say what stays on the main thread. Decision
1's exit (the fixtures and Octane within 2x of node-mg) is measured the same way, which moves the
reference: node-mg's Djot pass is 23 ms on two CPUs against 35 on one.

The empty plans' recompile (input, 2026-11-10: target/p3/stage2/recompile-input.txt). An empty
plan costs a whole AST-to-bytecode compile of the function and a second chunk with seeded caches
and an identity record map, on which everything after runs (entries, exits, discards, warm-up,
the chain recompile). Per run (the compile probe, CPU 5): Djot's 21 empty-plan recompiles 0.866M
cycles (0.69 ms, 0.9% of its measured pass) against 1.868M for its 16 spliced ones; React 0.119M
