   jit/Ion.cpp:1795-1845), where the step's measurement shows the allocator's share of prepare
   (9-13% today) still there after points 2 and 4.
Not in the step:

Step 13 landed (e7047743, 2026-11-11; target/p3/stage2/step13-impl, report.txt's "LANDING
VERSION"; evidence land/ and land/r47). Option (c) with the cheaper due path, as the owner
approved, rebased onto step 15:
- The loop limit (5 << R, never above the exit limit) is armed at each exit of the tier's code
  that does not discard it. Its warm-up goes on the chunk's loop word (Chunk::loop_word, four bytes
  after the entry word, so that no field the template code addresses moves; Chunk 1,896 -> 1,904
  bytes).
- Template code's loop iterations count toward the loop word; entries count nothing while it is
  armed.
- A count that reaches the word is the crossing: the checkpoint word takes the loop word, and the
  loop word is cleared. A due chunk's loops then cost 3 instructions an iteration (main: 7 or 11).
- The next native entry's check (opt::loop_limit_due, first in try_inline_recompile) discards the
  code as at its exit limit, returning a first stage's in-place code to its kept template code.
Departures:
- (1) A loop limit ending idles both words: 7558959b could leave a stale due checkpoint after a
  discard that arms no warm-up.
- (2) Forced exits arm nothing.
- (3) supersede drops a crossing's pending request.
- (4) Interp::call_prepared is hinted inline, as main inlines it: the entry test's growth had left
  it outlined after LTO (a sort comparator +2.16%; now +0.26%, 3.5 instructions a call).
- (5) The Rust entry paths count nothing while the warm-up is armed and not crossed: +1 instruction
  an entry with a checkpoint pending, +3 with none.

The owner's answer on the shell allocator (2026-11-11): "With 2 MiB pages". The allocator
(p3-alloc; target/p3/stage2/alloc-impl) lands with its segments advised for huge pages. Without
them, a compiled chunk's separate arrays (ops, names, caches), adjacent in glibc's heap, sit in
their classes' spans, and the A725's L1 data TLB misses twice as often (Vue's passes +6%). After
it comes a study of a compiled function's data layout (the exemplars keep a function's inline
caches in fewer blocks), dropping the advice if that removes the need.

  +4.75% -> +0.76%. Its calls reaching glibc 210,842 -> 3,562.

  equal instructions).
- The fixtures, at equal or fewer instructions:
  - React even;
  - Vue's measured pass +2.41% and passes 2-10 +4.06% (tier off); +2.42% and +1.02% (tier on);
  - Djot +2.39% and +2.69% (tier off); +0.20% and -0.05% (tier on);
  - with randomized addresses: Vue +1.65%, Djot +0.49%.
- RSS: about one segment (2 MiB) more where a program's spans reach into a segment, less where
  glibc fragmented (zlib -26.5 MiB after a trim).
An independent review (target/p3/stage2/alloc-review) found two platform bugs, to be fixed
before it lands:
- On aarch64 kernels with 64 KiB pages, a 16 KiB span's decommit discards its neighbours' live
  blocks: madvise rounds to whole pages. Confirmed by a test under emulation.
- On Windows, the main thread's exit drain can deadlock: ExitProcess kills the other threads
  first, one of them may hold a class lock, and the drain runs in the detach phase.
- Also, trim's partial discards inside a huge page are only unmapped. The kernel frees them when
  it splits the page, typically under memory pressure, so RSS counters overstate what a trim
  returns.

Step 17, the shared stubs made on the compile thread (design, 2026-11-11; input
compile-bar-input.txt sections 1 and 5, the exemplars' thunk caches). Today a compile off the main
thread that requests a stub its snapshot of the process's stub table lacks emits a veneer to
nothing, finishes, and returns Needs: the main thread assembles the stubs (driver::provide, logged
as the bar's `stubs` part, 0.21 ms a run on Djot, A725), takes the table's snapshot again, and
queues the job to run again from the start. JSC's compiler threads make a missing thunk
themselves: JITThunks::ctiStubImpl takes the thunks' lock, looks the key up, generates the thunk
where it is missing and adds it, marking the entry for a cross-modifying-code fence when a
compilation thread made it; the main thread runs the fence the first time it takes such an entry
(jit/JITThunks.cpp:189-222). V8's builtins are made at the build (the embedded blob), and
SpiderMonkey's VM wrappers and trampolines at the runtime's start (JitRuntime::initialize): in
neither does a compile meet a missing stub. TRust's stubs are made lazily, by key (several
hundred flag and helper combinations, a few dozen of which a program uses), so JSC's form is the
one that fits.
1. The lookup. An emission off the main thread asks the snapshot first, as now. A stub the
   snapshot lacks is looked up again under the table's lock (another thread may have made it
   since the snapshot) and, where it is still missing, assembled there, under the lock, by the
   main thread's own path (global_stub_address, assemble_global_stub): the same context (the
   job's layout, interpreter layout and direct-sequence offsets), the same buffer form, the
   perf map's entries. The table's generation moves, so the main thread's next snapshot is
   taken again.
2. The fence. The compile thread's flush (dc cvau, ic ivau, dsb ish) makes the stub's words
   visible to every core's instruction side, as for the job's own code; the main thread's
   instruction barrier at the install (sync_instruction_stream) covers the job's stubs as it
   covers its code. A later compile on any thread that finds the stub in the table branches to
   it from code it publishes itself, whose flush ends with dsb ish and isb on that thread, after
   it took the table's lock: no thread runs a shared stub without a context synchronization
   event after the stub was made. JSC needs its per-entry flag because its main thread can take
   a thunk without publishing code; TRust has no such path (the step checks it: every
   global_stub_address caller emits code). The step states this in the code.
3. The callee-saved flag. A stub that writes d8-d15 sets STUB_WRITES_CALLEE_SAVED_FP before it
   enters the table under the lock, and the job's finish reads the flag after its stubs, on its
   own thread, as the template JIT's finish does: a compile that reaches the stub keeps its
   saves.
4. A failed assembly (no executable memory) keeps the stub's body in the chunk (StubLookup::Copy)
   at once, recorded in the job's copies for its next compiles, with no second run.
5. Then no job runs twice: the Needs outcome, run_here's loop and install_one's requeue go, and
   so does the bar's `stubs` part. The debug check that a compile off the main thread reaches
   none of the main thread's state (OffMainCompile) admits the table's lock and the executable
   buffer's mapping, which are the process's.
6. Test builds keep a table a thread, as their stub bodies embed the main thread's test counters
   (TestAddresses): the job carries its main thread's table (a shared handle in place of the
   thread-local cell), so that the compile thread adds to the table its stubs' counters belong
   to, and a test's perf map capture receives their entries.
Tests: a compile off the main thread that requests a missing stub makes it and runs once (the
stub in the main thread's table after the install, the generation moved); two engines on two
threads requesting one stub at once make it once; a stub made off the main thread that writes
d8-d15 keeps the saves of the job that reaches it and of a later main-thread compile; a failed
assembly keeps a copy without a rerun; the tier's code identical before and after (but for the
stubs' addresses: identity_norm.py); the tier off untouched. Mutations: the lookup under the lock
skipped (a stub made twice), the generation not moved, the flag set after the insert.
Expected: the stubs part (0.21 ms A725, 0.11 ms X925 on Djot) gone from the bar, and the jobs
that met a missing stub installed one compile sooner. The tier's code unchanged.

Step 16 landed (726843c6..ea40878e, 2026-11-11; target/p3/stage2/step16-impl, report.txt). The
five points as designed, each its own commit:
- (1) The instrument: a log line's arguments, formatting and printing are left out of the
  main-thread parts' clock, and each part ends before its line is made.
- (3) The hand-off (opt::compiler::Queue): the compile thread marks itself waiting under the
  queue's lock before it waits; an enqueue takes the mark under the lock and notifies only where
  it found it. No job is stranded: the thread's look at the jobs and its mark share one hold of
  the lock, an enqueue's push and its read of the mark another, and the condition variable's wait
  releases the lock and sleeps as one step. The job's clock reads happen under the log only.
- (2) Keys carried by the chunk: the frozen view takes every name's key from the chunk's table
  (made by the VM's first named access or the tier's first freeze); a second stage's table is
  carried at its recompile, with the tier on only, through a map of its record map's chunks'
  tables by text (Chunk::carry_name_keys: 48.5 us a Djot run against 61.0 for a lookup a name).
  No job reruns for an atom.
- (4) The snapshot sparse, decoded on the compile thread: the main thread copies the words the
  decode reads, by the index it reads them by (records, a second stage's baseline records through
  the record map, the call and construct entries' words, the literals' readiness, the call caches'
  epoch); the property caches' ways come from the frozen view's one read, which the templates and
  the decode share. One decode (feedback_read::site_view), generic over its source, serves the
  live chunk and the copy; debug builds compare both at every compile.
- (5) The job's memory reused (needed: the allocator was 24% of start_job after (2) and (4)): the
  frozen view's and the raw copy's vectors go back to the preparing thread at the install (two
  kept a thread), as SpiderMonkey reuses a finished compile's LifoAlloc. Reused vectors rather
  than a bump arena: Rust has no stable allocator-parameterized collections.
Measured (fat LTO, main be6b5ee2 against the branch):
- Djot's bar, asynchronous on two A725 cores, medians of 7 log runs: 3.889 -> 3.288 ms a run
  (snapshot -0.443, install -0.155, stubs -0.042, recompile +0.064 for the carry); by the study's
  ratios 3.82% -> 3.29% on X925 (the plan: about 3.4%). React 0.451 -> 0.421 ms, Vue 2.504 ->
  2.209. Per job the snapshot line is 30.5 -> 21.0 us, the install 6.3 -> 3.0 us. 8 of Djot's 50
  enqueues found the thread busy (React 4 of 10, Vue 19 of 43). The snapshot line fell less than
  the expected 0.5-0.6 ms: log runs keep the job's clock reads by design, few enqueues found the
  thread busy, and the raw copy and the first freezes' key tables are new work.
- The tier's code and the tier off's template code identical to main's on the fixtures, their fx3
  copies, Octane's fixed runs and the tier's tests (every differing hash an address loaded in a
  different number of MOVZ/MOVK instructions). Instructions: Octane geomean +0.03% off, +0.10%

  layout, +0.03% randomized); the calls within 0.01%.

  and on in both modes), the tier's in three configurations (297 each), poll and Agent (51 each,
  both modes), Test262 53,573 of 53,575 in four configurations; clippy, fmt; zombie's check,

The owner's answers (2026-11-11):
- Step 15's rule: "Maglev's form". A site whose ways hold several distinct callees splices them
  guarded again where the call is a named method call; keyed and plain calls with several
  callees still decline, and so do sites that met another closure (below).
- The delivered clock: "Time as the machine runs". Sessions accept the machine's power state,
  record the delivered clock with each phase (user cycles over task clock, cpuinfo_avg_freq), and
  require it steady across phases and between compared sessions. The logger since showed that
  the GPU is not the cause: with the GPU idle (225 samples, 11:54-13:08) the X925 cores still
  delivered 1.36-1.37 GHz and the A725 cores 0.98-0.99 GHz, thermald at state 0, about 60 C. The
  limit sits below cpufreq, its cause unknown; nothing on the machine was changed.

Step 15b, Maglev's polymorphic method calls (design, 2026-11-11; the owner's answer above; the
diagnosis in the be6b5ee2 session's record). With the tier on, a call site whose ways hold several
distinct callees splices them, guarded by callee as before step 15, where the call's callee is a
named property's value: a method call o.m(...), whose callee Op::GetMethod reads. Maglev continues
a polymorphic named load's arms through the call of the loaded function, where each arm's function
is a constant it can inline (maglev_poly_calls, on by default;
FindContinuationForPolymorphicPropertyLoad, maglev-graph-builder.cc:6038-6125). Its load has at
most V8's four maps, our ways at most the cache's four. A site whose callee is a keyed read's
(Op::GetMethodElem: Gameboy's memoryReader[address](...), OPCODE[opcode](...)) or any other
value's declines, as Maglev's continuation follows GetNamedProperty alone and V8's call feedback
has no polymorphic state. A site that met another closure of a way's function declines as now
(stage 3 guards those by code).
- The record: the bytecode compiler marks each call site whose callee GetMethod made (a flag set
  once at the compile), so the planner reads it without walking the operations. The tier off
  keeps P2's policy.

The shell allocator's spans landed (d3601756..04d730d1, 2026-11-11; target/p3/stage2/alloc-impl
and alloc-fix, report.txt each; the review alloc-review). fastalloc::ClassAlloc, the
#[global_allocator] of the shell, the Test262 runner and the differential tester, refills each
thread's class lists from its own 16 KiB spans carved from 2 MiB segments (the owner's answers of
2026-11-11: the thread's own pages; with 2 MiB pages), instead of a glibc call a miss.
- The review's findings, fixed:
  - 64 KiB-page kernels (AArch64 RHEL, Grace): a span's discard took its neighbours' live blocks.
    A span is now 16 KiB or the system's page where larger, up to 64 KiB (a1e205b5); every
    allocator test passes under an emulated 64 KiB kernel (the head before: 2 of 16).
  - Huge pages: a span's MADV_DONTNEED inside a THP-backed segment frees nothing until the kernel
    splits the page under pressure, and costs the segment its TLB entry. Segment-granular release
    (3ca5b64f): inside a huge page no span is discarded alone; a segment whose spans are all free
    is unmapped whole (past the pool's bound and at trims); where no huge page backs a segment
    (Windows, macOS, THP off, 16/64 KiB kernels) spans are still discarded alone. As TCMalloc's
    HugePageFiller subreleases a used huge page only when it must and jemalloc's HPA dehugifies
    before a partial purge. Given up: 2.6-8.4 MiB that six workloads freed under memory pressure
    through splits (zlib, CodeLoad, GBEMU, Mandreel, Splay, Earley-Boyer); HPA's dehugify at a trim
    is the next step if it matters.
  - Windows' process end: ExitProcess ends the other threads wherever they are (one may hold an
    allocator lock), then runs the exiting thread's destructors, whose drain waited for good. The
    drain now returns at once while ntdll's RtlDllShutdownInProgress is TRUE, as JSC's libpas does
    (pas_thread_local_cache.c's destructor, pas_process.c:38-55), the function resolved once at
    the first thread's registration (04d730d1). Verified under Wine 11.21 (its ntdll exports it;
    a child process that exits while another thread holds the pool's and a class's locks ends at
    once, and hangs with the check made never true; the shell's every exit path skips the drain
    and takes no lock after it). Not run on real Windows; it rests on libpas's assumption.
- Measured (CPU 14, fat LTO, against main be6b5ee2): CodeLoad -6.20% cycles and -9.04%
  instructions with the tier off (-5.78% on); the idle second thread's cost +4.25% on main,
  +0.55% on the branch; the fixtures' measured passes and passes 2-10 within -2.3% and +0.6% (the
  first build's Vue +2.4%/+4.1% tier off, a layout residue, gone).
- Memory (each run in its own cgroup, charge): against glibc after a trim, zlib -18.5 MiB,
  Mandreel -14.6, TypeScript -8.0; CodeLoad +5.5, DeltaBlue +3.4, Crypto +3.3, GBEMU +2.9, small
  programs +1.2 to +1.7 (a segment's huge page). Corrections: the first report's RSS figures
  omitted split pages' held memory; the plan's "zlib -26.5 MiB after a trim" is -18.5 charged,
  Mandreel's -19.3 is -14.6.
- Tests: the allocator's 21 (23 on Windows) on Crystal, under the 64 KiB emulation, on zombie and
  under Wine; 20 mutations of the spans' fixes caught, and the Windows check's; the library's

- Open, not fixed: the collector's 64 KiB page discards inside its THP-advised 2 MiB chunks behave
  the same way. After TypeScript's final collection every build, main's included, holds 351-396
  MiB of split huge pages: RSS reads 168 MiB while the charge is 516-564 MiB until reclaim. RSS
  figures for collections overstate what they return. The same choice applies there

A compiled function's data layout and the huge pages (study, 2026-11-11; target/p3/stage2/
layout-input.txt, evidence layout/ and layout-x925/; the owner's request with the allocator's
huge pages). A compiled function is 37-59 separate heap blocks (11-31 KB), 92% of them 1 KiB or
less and so in the allocator's size classes; template code embeds each per-site cell's address.
JSC keeps a function's per-site data in two blocks (MetadataTable, BaselineJITData) reached
through pinned registers, SpiderMonkey in one JitScript with trailing IC arrays (attached stubs
bump-allocated per realm), V8 in one FeedbackVector reached from a frame slot; none embeds
per-site addresses, none advises huge pages by default, all release memory a base page at a time.
- The allocator keeps its segments' huge-page advice. Grouping a chunk's blocks (a probe, one
  binary with runtime switches, A725) removes only 31% of Vue's extra L1 TLB refills without the
  advice, 11% of React's and 5% of Djot's: the rest is the engine's other small runtime data
  (Maps' buckets, closures, the frame stack) and the collector's entries it evicts from the
  A725's 48-entry L1 TLB. One exactly sized block a chunk (the exemplars' form) is worth doing
  later for memory (the Vecs' slack is 25-50% of those arrays) and for platforms without huge
  pages, with the allocator serving blocks of a few KiB from its segments.
- The collector advises its 2 MiB chunks for huge pages from the heap's fourth chunk and discards
  64 KiB pages inside them, which frees nothing until the kernel splits the page under pressure:
  after TypeScript's final collection RSS reads 169 MiB while the charge is 524 MiB (161 after a
  reclaim). Dehugifying before the discard changes nothing (538 MiB); keeping chunks whole
  returns nothing even under pressure (531). Without the advice every discard frees at once
  (161 MiB), at cycles (fixed runs, the same binary): on an X925 core Splay +4.2% (tier on)
  and +4.6% (off), TypeScript +1.5% and +1.6%, Earley-Boyer +0.4% and +0.3%, Richards unchanged;
  on the A725 Splay +3.4%, TypeScript +1.4%, Earley-Boyer +1.0%, Djot's passes +0.7%. The
  exemplars' collectors advise none (V8's sweeper and pool, heap/sweeper.cc:1045-1090;
  SpiderMonkey's arenas in 1 MiB chunks, gc/Memory.cpp:999-1027; JSC's MarkedBlocks through
  libpas). The choice goes to the owner.

Step 17 landed (a0218103..c1b6aa85, 2026-11-11; target/p3/stage2/step17-impl, report.txt). As
designed: a stub missing from a job's snapshot is looked up again under the table's lock and made
there by the main thread's own path with the job's context; one hold of the lock covers the
lookup, the assembly, the insert and the generation's move, as JSC's ctiStubImpl
(jit/JITThunks.cpp:189-222). Outcome::Needs, run_here's loop, provide, assemble_stubs and
install_one's requeue are gone; the log says `met <n> missing stubs, made <m>`.
- The fence: every path that obtains a stub's address branches to it only from code synchronized
  after the lock (a main-thread compile publishes its own code, whose flush ends in dsb ish; isb;
  a job's code runs only after the install's isb), and no code is patched after it is published,
  so JSC's per-entry flag is not needed (the argument at jit::StubTable). The callee-saved FP flag
  is set under the lock before the insert and read at every compile's finish, after its stubs.
  Test builds: each thread's table behind a shared handle the job's snapshot carries, with the
  main thread's test counters and perf map capture.
- Departure (ff089590): the main thread's snapshot of the table no longer waits for its lock; it
  reuses its last snapshot while the lock is held. A stale snapshot is safe: the job looks up what
  it lacks under the lock. With the first commit alone Vue's snapshot line rose 0.106 ms a run.
- Measured (fat LTO, main eb7c0c98 against the branch, asynchronous on two A725 cores, medians of
  7 log runs, 1.14 GHz delivered throughout): the bar's sums Djot 3.446 -> 3.245 ms a run (the
  stubs part -0.193), React 0.424 -> 0.340 (-0.114), Vue 2.253 -> 1.772 (-0.415), the other parts
  within their spread; a job that met a missing stub installs one compile sooner (Djot's largest
  4.96 -> 2.96 ms from its snapshot to its install). The tier's code identical but for the stubs'
  addresses (1,227 tier and 2,079 template compiles, synchronous), the tier off's 2,491 of 2,492
  (the other one the conservative stack scan's). Instructions: Octane's geomean -0.84%
  asynchronous (Richards -5.81%: the reruns, and one callee compile fewer as its callers install
  sooner), -0.12% off, -0.09% synchronous; the calls within 0.014%.
- Tests: 8 new; the brief's 4 mutations and 2 more caught; the library's suites (3,206 tier on in
  both modes; tier off 3,205 and one wall-clock bound missed under a load of 15-21,
  long_bigint_operations_honor_the_deadline, which passes alone on main's and the branch's

Step 15b landed (14dd76d2, 2026-11-11; target/p3/stage2/step15b-impl, report.txt). As designed: a
call site's callee shape is marked once at the bytecode compile, CallSite::named_method (the
padding byte after hint_guard, offset 497: no field moves, the site stays 504 bytes,
compile-asserted), and the planner splices a site with several distinct callees, guarded by callee,
where it is set. Marked: o.m(), this.task.run(), (o.m)(), o?.m(), a chain's named links. Not
marked: super.m(), o.#m(), o[k]() (Gameboy's), free-name, local and comma calls, for-of's next();
o['m']() too (TRust reads it keyed). Beyond Maglev's reach and marked: o.m?.() and calls whose
arguments jump or run long, where Maglev's continuation stops because it replays the bytecode in
each arm (V8's own comments say so), which a splice does not do (a one-line change to match it
exactly). At depth > 0 the planner reads a spliced callee's own sites, marked at its compile.

  DeltaBlue 7, RayTrace 2 (shape.intersect), PdfJS 6, TypeScript 51; none on the fixtures, Gameboy
  or the rest.

The tier's bitwise operations on oddballs landed (764373b9, c54f2758, 2026-11-12; target/p3/stage2/
holey-impl, report.txt): the calls' array-shift-queue-holey loop ran 25% slower in the tier's code
than in template code because of `a.shift() | 0`, not push or shift. The hole makes one shift a run
return undefined, so the BitOr's record held Undefined, and the tier lowered a bitwise operation
only on Numbers: every iteration went through the generic executor (237 of the 312 extra
instructions an iteration). Now a bitwise operation whose record saw only Numbers, undefined, null
and Booleans truncates each operand not proven a Number in line (TruncateNumberOrOddball: a Number
in the same 4 instructions, undefined and null 0, Booleans 0 or 1 out of line, anything else
exits), as V8 records kNumberOrOddball and Maglev truncates it
(TruncateCheckedNumberOrOddballToInt32), JSC's DFG converts a NotCellNorBigIntUse and
SpiderMonkey's bitwise IC stub takes null and undefined as 0. An oddball first met after the
compile on Numbers takes the truncation at the recompile, as V8 reoptimizes after the deopt; only
the truncation's own exit declines the operation to the generic one (no exit and recompile cycles
on Symbols).

  against template code (+22.7% before), a late undefined +0.7% (+19.2%); Octane's fixed runs zlib
  -1.35% and Mandreel -0.88% instructions, the geomean -0.11% asynchronous and -0.16% synchronous;
  timed zlib +2.0%, Mandreel +1.3%; the fixtures and the calls at main's instruction counts; the
  tier off's template code identical but for addresses.

Stage 3's input, refreshed (2026-11-12; target/p3/stage2/inlining-input-2.txt and its evidence,
at main 24a52c5b; the first input, inlining-input.txt, was at da6933eb). The structural facts hold
(one frame per graph, frame state and exit; effects on ctx.chunk's canonical frame; splicing an AST
recompile behind identity guards; no per-site call counts); what moved: the builder reads only the
frozen view and the decoded snapshot, so each inlined callee needs its own raw copy and frozen view;
the emitters are generic over JitChunk; CallIc's offset 60 now holds step 14's entry word (free:
CallIc 100-103, CallSite 500-503); the splice rule declines a whole site before charging a way.
- Census (19 workloads): 1,146 plans, 717 empty (compiled in place), 429 spliced, 1,335 top-level
  sites, +49,116 operations; 51,887 exits, 17,982 WrongCallee (22,937 before), none on the fixtures.
  Of the WrongCallee exits 57% met a function none of the site's ways holds, 40% another way of the
  same site: the builder builds no edge to the next way until a guard is flagged (build.rs:2480-
  2488), so an n-way site needs up to n-1 discards to converge, where Maglev's polymorphic arms
  branch by map from the first compile and deopt only on an unseen map.
- Djot's recompile: 15 spliced recompiles a run (2,119 -> 3,309 operations, 33 ways), next()
  0.38 ms to splice tip() five times, feed() 0.29 ms for singleChar(). Building them in the graph
  instead costs each job its callees' first-stage preparation (1.5-8.6 us a chunk, 0.128 ms a run)
  and saves 0.087 ms of the callers' snapshots: Djot's bar to about 0.68 ms on X925, about 1.0%.
- Sixteen open choices, for the stage 3 design: where decisions and callee snapshots are made,
  policy units and budgets (Maglev charges graph-wide totals and never the caller's size; Warp's
  root total includes it; JSC a per-site balance and a ranked plan; TRust alone charges the
  caller), call counts and their denominator (V8: a count in each call slot and the vector's
  invocation count, times the parent's frequency; SpiderMonkey: a stub's entered count; JSC:
  polymorphic stub edges), frame states, multi-frame exits, effects in inlined frames, polymorphic
  dispatch, guards by code for closures, shared or per-site callee feedback, exceptions, positions,
  exit and loop limits, recursion, the callee shapes admitted first, what stays of splicing, and
  the install's checks with inlined callees.

Stage 3, the tier's own inlining (design, 2026-11-12; target/p3/stage2/stage3-design-draft.txt is
the detailed reference, with its file:line citations; inputs inlining-input.txt and -2.txt). The
exemplars build a callee into the caller's graph from the callee's own bytecode and shared
feedback, with a frame-state chain, and an exit rebuilds one baseline frame per function. TRust's
splice recompiles the caller's AST with callees spliced behind identity guards: no frames of their
own, and the bytecode recompile (1.31 ms of Djot's 1.97 ms at the bar). The steps, each measured
graph against splice on one binary (a switch, TRUST_JS_OPT_INLINE=graph|splice, padded):
1. Frame-state chains and exits through several frames: an inlined frame's table (its chunk, the
   parent state at the call's pc + 1, the callee and `this`), states naming their frame, the
   verifier per frame; the deoptimizer rebuilds every frame first (values rooted, a record and an
   FnFrame each), then runs the innermost and resumes each parent after its call with the value or
   at it with the throw, as all three exemplars do. Tested on hand-made graphs; no code changes.
2. The graph inliner for callees without effects (E0), behind the switch, with the planner's
   decisions: Djot's leaf-only callers stop recompiling (its bar about 2.6%).
3. Effects, calls and throws in inlined frames (D6 below), positions by the splice's mechanism:
   Djot's bar about 1.0%.
4. The flip: graph inlining by default, no tier compile recompiles bytecode; a session reads the
   bars.
5. Named method calls as Maglev's polymorphic arms from the first compile, an unseen callee exiting
   (input-2: 7,123 WrongCallee exits a census are a site's other way).
6. Closures guarded by code where a site met another closure (literal, realm), the environment
   read from the closure, as all three engines do (plan point 8).
7. Calls the tier does not inline, direct to the known callee (point 5, Maglev's
   CallKnownJSFunction).
8. Per-site call counts and Maglev's policy (D2, D3).
9. Callee shapes beyond the splice's (arguments and rest, activations, handlers, constructors),
   each on a measured need.
Splicing stays for the tier off and for functions the tier refuses (Decision 3 is the phase end's),
selectable until the stage's exit. Risks, each measured per step: the WrongCallee convergence (each
exit now rebuilds several frames), the compile thread's load, multi-frame exits' correctness
(rooting, records, positions), shared callee feedback going polymorphic where a splice's seeded
copy stayed monomorphic (measured per inlined site; trial inlining only on a measured loss), the
per-frame records' cost against a splice's free effects, Maglev's smaller limits, and a discarded
function's fallback to template code without splices. Forks for the owner: D2 (the policy's
numbers), D3 (call counts), D6 (effects in inlined frames), D7 (polymorphic sites); the rest are
detail, recommended in the draft.

The owner's answers on stage 3's forks (2026-11-12):
- D2, the policy's numbers: "Maglev's numbers" (callees under 100 bytes, under 30 eagerly to depth
  10 within a 3,000-byte small total, the 0.95 frequency rule past 27 bytes; sizes in JSC's cost
  unit, each instruction's operands plus one, scaled at 1.17 V8 bytes a unit), measured at step 8
  against step 7; a remaining loss comes back to the owner with Maglev's structure at TRust-sized
  numbers.
- D7, polymorphic sites: "Maglev: small arms": branches by callee from the first compile, arms under
  Maglev's small size inlined, the others called (step 7's direct calls).
- D6, effects in inlined frames: "Explicit operands", the exemplars' form, not the recommended
  records per frame. Every operation the tier does not lower becomes the tier's own generic node
  with explicit operands (its chunk, pc and input values), calling its helper with them, as
  Maglev's generic nodes call builtins, JSC's DFG calls its operations (the code origin stored
  before each call out) and Warp builds MIR calls; in every frame, the compiled function's own
  too, so that the template effects on ctx.chunk's canonical frame go (stage 1's departure, plan
  596-599, ends). Step 3 becomes that change, designed on its own input (the operations run as
  effects today, their helpers' signatures, what each reads of the frame, the exits).
- D3, call counts: "V8's counts": a count per call site and an invocation count per function,
  counted only by template code compiled with the tier on.

Stage 3's step 3 input, explicit operands (2026-11-12; target/p3/stage2/effects-input.txt and its
evidence; main b02f590d).
- The census (a counting probe; the fixtures, their passes 2-10, Octane at 5 and 40 fixed runs):
  314M effect executions a set of runs at 16,456 sites in 1,391 tier codes, 63 operation kinds,
  sixteen of them 95%: named loads 35.2% (GetMethod 19.3%, GetPropLocal 12.0%), equality 23.6%
  (EqEq 14.0%), calls 19.4% (CallWithThis 17.9%), named stores and updates 14.3%. About 73% are
  operations the tier does not lower yet (prototype and string method loads, `==` on values no
  fact has typed, stores that add properties, `o.x++`), which Maglev lowers from its feedback: a
  larger lever than step 3, apart from it.
- The canonical frame's cost: per effect 2 set-up instructions, 1.64 operand stores, 1.07 result
  loads and 0.54 flushes, and 1.33 billion slot reloads, 83% re-checked (71% of them zlib's and
  NavierStokes': after a call, a loop with no calls of its own reloads its invariant locals every
  iteration). Explicit operands remove up to 2.3% of instructions (zlib 3.5%, NavierStokes 5.6%,
  React's passes 2-10 5.2%), an upper bound that register pressure may take back.
- A plain helper call per operation would make about 92% of today's effect executions slower: the
  templates' in-line paths serve them. So the templates stay, fed explicit operands through an
  operand area, as JSC's DFG keeps its ICs in line (Maglev calls builtins with the IC probe inside).
- GC safety: TRust's collector never moves objects and scans the native stack, callee-saved
  registers and active records conservatively, JSC's model: values held across a helper call need
  no maps. Lazy deopt is not needed while the tier keeps no assumption across an effect (no
  dependencies yet). Exceptions: JSC's form, an exit by recipe with the handler's values kept live,
  in place of writing the whole frame state before each effect in a try region.
- The step's shape: ahead of step 3, an effect-free loop's invariant locals loaded once at its
  entry; 3a generic nodes in the compiled function's own frame (no flush, no reload, phis instead
  of frame merges, throws by recipe, templates unchanged); 3b origins (chunk, pc, inline site) where
  the templates take the pc, as JSC's call-site index; 3c explicit context for inlined frames, kind
  by kind in the census's order; 3d register forms of the hottest kinds where measured.

The small callees' calls into the tier's code landed (11275b17, 3a0d8427, 2026-11-12; target/p3/
stage2/smallcallee-impl, report.txt): template code calling the tier's code for a tiny function
was 1.2-2.5% slower than calling its template code (the session's constructor +2.21%, over the 2%
bar). Two causes, X925 cycles a call averaged over six caller layouts:
- The direct sequence's entry-word compare (step 14) loaded the way's copy with `LDP x6, x4,
  [x12, #56]`, which crosses the way's byte 64: where a way starts on a cache line, one alignment
  event every call (constructor +1.8 cycles), and the chunk pointer the call waits on later. Now
  the copy is loaded alone (`LDR w6, [x12, #60]`) and the chunk by its own load, nothing crossing
  byte 64 (4 instructions for 3; the tier off's sequence unchanged).
- The tier boxed an f64 with FMOV, FCMP, MOVZ and a CSEL on VS, which put the compare on a caller's
  loop-carried result; now FMOV, FCMP and B.VS to an out-of-line load of the canonical NaN,
  SpiderMonkey's form (MacroAssembler-inl.h:971-976).
The exemplars load the callee's entry on every call and compare nothing (V8's dispatch table,
SpiderMonkey's jitCodeRaw) or relink a code block's incoming calls (JSC, CodeBlock.cpp:2221-2240);
their optimized leaf entries are no heavier than their baselines'. Removing the compare their way is
designed (a current-code record in jit_cache::SlotState, the direct sequence reading the code
through the chunk, the Rust probes' entry_stale and the ways' entry copy gone), not built.
- Measured (fat LTO, the tier on against off, main -> branch; calls.js timed on X925 cores, 5
  rounds, 1.364-1.369 GHz): direct-call +1.18% -> -2.57%, closure-call +1.07% -> -0.87%,
  constructor +2.53% -> +1.26% (bimodal rounds), arguments-object +1.97% -> +0.19%, rest-params
  +1.61% -> -0.50%; on the A725, arguments +2.11% and rest +1.65% remain (per-call counts). Octane
  timed +0.11% cycles geomean (within the tier off's spread), counted -0.01%; the fixtures within
  0.17% instructions; the tier off's template code identical in 3,067 of 3,067 compiles.
- Tests: new tests and four mutations caught; the library's suites (3,240 tier off and eager
  synchronous; eager asynchronous 3,239 and a 20 ms timing test under another agent's load, which
  passes alone 10 of 10 and fails as often on main's binary under the same load), the tier's in
  three configurations (313), poll and Agent (51), Test262 eager 53,573/53,575; clippy, fmt;
  zombie's check, clippy, Windows' check and suite (2,796).

The collector's huge pages with honest discards landed (5f579b87, 6d704eba, 2026-11-12; target/p3/
stage2/gcfault-impl, report.txt and report-b.txt; the owner's answer: "B: huge pages, honest
discards"). The fault count that made dropping the advice costly was first touches, not churn:
with GC pacing on the shell never runs the memory reducer, so nothing is given back in a timed run,
and every fault is a new page's first touch (Splay's heap grows to 266 chunks: 16 faults a 64 KiB
page on base pages, one a chunk on huge pages). Two options were built and measured in one session
against main (X925 cores, 1.37 GHz delivered, 10 rounds):
- A, the exemplars' form: no advice, and a helper thread keeping a chunk faulted in
  (MADV_POPULATE_WRITE), as JSC's libpas prefaults its GC's blocks: TypeScript -1.44%, Box2D
  -0.93%, SplayLatency -0.92%, the whole suite -1.1%, Splay on one A725 core -11.4%, up to 0.5 s
  of system time on the helper; the charge after TypeScript's final collection 168.9 MiB.
- B, landed: the chunks keep their advice; a page given back inside one is discarded with MADV_FREE
  then MADV_DONTNEED (MADV_FREE splits the huge page, so the discard frees at once: TCMalloc's
  free-then-dontneed release), and a chunk's first such page advises it MADV_NOHUGEPAGE until every
  page given back has been taken again, then MADV_HUGEPAGE (jemalloc HPA's dehugify before a partial
  purge; without it khugepaged re-collapsed split chunks 13-14 minutes later, zero-filling what was
  given back). TypeScript -0.23%, Box2D -0.20%, SplayLatency -0.13%, the whole suite +0.7% (noise),
  Splay on one A725 core +0.0%; the charge after TypeScript's collection 164.8 MiB (main 526.0,
  RSS 168.4). With pacing as shipped B makes exactly main's madvise calls. None of V8, SpiderMonkey
  or JSC does this: none advises its heap for huge pages.
- Checks: the collector's tests (115, the new one reading the cgroup's charge), a mutation of each
  rule caught, the library's suite tier off and on (3,240), clippy, fmt; on main: clippy clean, the
  collector's tests, zombie's check, clippy and Windows' check.
Open, found on the way (gcfault-impl/retention.txt): long Splay runs retain dead tree nodes. In a
fixed 2,800-iteration run the live data after each major collection grows from 130 MB to 645 MB
with the tier on and to 1,060 MB with the bytecode VM, where it stays flat at 118 MB with the
template JIT alone (V8: 49-59 MB). Not floating garbage (a cycle allocates 8-43 MB), not the page
source. Octane's timed Splay (about 1,200 iterations) stays under it. To be found.

Stage 2's exit gates (2026-11-12/13; target/p3/stage2/gate-s2.sh, stage 1's gate-s1.sh copied, on
a git-archive export of main 0e77d515: target/p3/stage2/gate2; the triage target/p3/stage2/

Test262 53,573 of 53,575 (the two staging/sm failures); GC stress 1 over the stress set, off and
eager. Six failures, none the engine's; the fixes landed (e8406619..d678eb23):
- Two splice-rule tests under GC stress: the call caches' epoch is process-wide, and the other
  tests' engines ending and discarding moved it 11-127 times within a run; the tests that need a
  still epoch run alone in a child process, as jit::call_record_tests do. (Open: in a process of
  several engines one engine's discard stales every engine's call caches.)
- The cyclic walk's poll test under stress: the setup left a stress collection due, which the walk's
  entry poll ran inside the 20 ms deadline; the test runs it before arming the timer.
- The thread-local root test with forced exits: glibc gave the test's thread a stack an earlier
  thread had used, one slot the closure's frame had not yet written holding that thread's word for
  the cell; the thread now gets a stack larger than glibc's cache, always fresh.
- The native-stub count with forced exits: forced exits hand its natives to the VM, stage 1's case;
  the test keeps the tier without forced exits.
- The opt:: stress run past its hour (313 tests; one takes hours under stress): gate-s2b.sh splits it
  into four module runs, 4 hours each.
- Test262's runner with the tier eager and forced: its counting allocator counted a refused 7 PiB
  request for a moment, failing the compile thread's allocations meanwhile; it counts a request only
  where the cap admits it.
The combined tree (main, the retention fix and these fixes, 7 h 45 min): every stage passes but the
retention fix's own test, a small splay tree's live data, with the tier on: a stale operand word
above an active record's live top, inside the extent the fix scans; the retention fix is finishing
with records scanned up to their live top (SpiderMonkey's form) before it lands.

Stage 3 step 0 landed (c6b50adb, d2f9e626, 00e1391a, 4f9e98a0, 2026-11-14; target/p3/stage3/
step0-impl, report.txt and design-note.txt): a loop without effects loads its invariant slots once
and checks them at its entry.
- The load: at the header of a loop the prepass finds free of effects, a live slot the loop reads
  and does not write, which its entry edges leave in the frame's keeping, takes its value at the
  end of each entry predecessor (a FrameSlot there, as a written slot's phi inputs are taken) and
  is held Clean through the loop: the one value, or a phi of the entries' values. The frame still
  holds it, so every state in the loop keeps the slot in the frame's keeping and an exit rebuilds
  the frame it did. A slot the loop does not read stays in the frame, which holds it at no cost
  (GBEMU's scanline renderers held locals read only after their loops in registers and spill slots
  across them: +0.24% instructions without the read set, -0.18% with it).
- The checks: the phi selector moves the checks in the loop of a value loaded on the loop's one
  entry edge (CheckNumber and CheckInt32 of it, CheckObject and CheckElements of its cell, each
  kind once) to the end of that edge's predecessor, where step 6 puts its speculations, exiting at
  the header's pc with the state the edge leaves under a new reason, HoistingFailed, whose flag at
  the header leaves the checks in the loop: Ion's LICM of guards to the loop's predecessor and its
  back-off after a hoisted guard bails out (jit/LICM.cpp:204-246, BaselineBailouts.cpp:2061-2087)
  and the FTL's hoisting to the pre-header, which stops after a HoistingFailed exit (dfg/
  DFGLICMPhase.cpp:254-271, 337-386). Maglev has no LICM: it peels the loop, the precondition step
  6's departure 2 already left. TRust's loops are not rotated, so every body block is behind the
  loop's test and every move is a blind speculation in JSC's sense: a failing entry check costs one
  exit a call and at most one discard a header. Nothing moves with two entry edges (no pre-header),
  from a predecessor that ends in a branch, or for a kind check where the loop tests the same cell
  for a typed array.
- Measured (fat LTO against 87b38dea, the tier on): NavierStokes -8.25% instructions at 5 fixed
  runs (the load alone -1.34%), -12.4% instructions and -7.1% cycles at 200 on an X925 core,
  Octane's timed score +7.7% (10 rounds, +7.5 to +8.0%); its reloads 8,016,368 -> 996 a run. PdfJS
  -0.83%, GBEMU -0.18%, Crypto -0.13%; nothing above +0.01%. The tier off's template code
  identical (3,067 compiles); compile work equal (Djot -0.01% instructions with its compiles on the
  main thread). Mandreel's cycles read +0.2 to +0.6% with identical instructions and code (none of
  its 84 compiles changed; the tier off equal): unexplained, to be read again in the stage's
  session.
- zlib and Mandreel gain nothing, and the step 3 input's attribution does not hold for them (it put
  71% of the reloads in zlib's and NavierStokes' effect-free loops). Their reloads, 76% of the
  census's 1.40 billion, are in loops the prepass counts as having effects only because
  Rules::lowers has no case for equality, which the builder then lowers from its operands' facts.
  A diagnostic build that counts equality as lowered, so that those loops hold their values, made
  zlib 10.2% slower in instructions (103.95G -> 114.56G) with the same compiles, exits and flags:
  a8's code 52,004 -> 57,696 and 81,584 -> 86,324 bytes with fewer nodes, the typed element stub
  14.9G -> 21.5G instructions; Mandreel +1.77%, three functions refused where an untyped equality
  stayed an effect. Step 3a will hold every loop's values that way, so the cause is found first
  (step 0b: an investigation, the exemplars' answer to the cause, the remedy if it is contained).
- Tests: 13 builder tests, 3 end-to-end and a loop-limit test, a program of the shapes in the
  tier's equal-results test; 14 mutations, one a rule, each caught by a builder test (on zombie);
  the tier's tests in four configurations (251) and the new ones under GC stress; a stress
  program in the VM and six tier configurations, with and without GC stress; zombie's check,
  clippy, Windows' check and suite (2,810). On the landed tree, the tier off, on, eager and eager
  with forced exits: the library suite 3,259 passed and none failed in each, difftest 138 agree in
  each (12 skipped by budget), the three fixtures' masked outputs equal the tier off's in each
  (target/p3/stage3/step0-review/gate.out).
- Open: the checks of values the loop's entry already held (entry slots, `this`, slots read before
  the loop: am3's receivers) and of values that are no slots in loops with effects, the general
  LICM of checks, the same mechanism over a wider set, a step of its own; a pre-header for loops
  of two entry edges (rare in the measured code).

Stage 3 step 0b landed (b04c14ec, 6b6a353f, 2026-11-14; target/p3/stage3/step0b-impl, report.txt):
why values held through zlib's loops cost more than reloading them, and the remedy.
- The cause, by a counting probe on every emitted word and by SPE: a TypedElement node calls the
  template's shared typed-array stub on its main path, and the back end saved and restored every
  live caller-saved register around each call (the cold paths' register snapshot), while the
  allocator, which did not count the call, gave loop-long values caller-saved registers. Main paid
  it too: 15.3G of the tier's 59.7G executed words in zlib at 5 fixed runs (758M calls, 10.1 words
  a call). Step 0's diagnostic's +10.57G was +12.94G of those saves and +2.23G of spills against
  -4.61G of checks, reloads, conversions and moves it removed. The stub ran the same calls on both
  sides: the profile's +44% in it was PMU skid (SPE reads it flat).
- The exemplars agree: the allocator knows a main-path call's clobbers and keeps values live
  across it out of them (Maglev frees a node's specific temporaries before it, maglev-regalloc.cc:
  2017-2046; Ion's fixed temps cover their instruction, BacktrackingAllocator.cpp:1915-1928; Air's
  patchpoint clobbers are defs, b3/air/AirInstInlines.h:100-121); saving the live registers around
  a call is for out-of-line calls only (Maglev's register snapshot, Ion's saveLive, the DFG's
  silentSpill).
- The clobber set (b04c14ec): regalloc::clobbers names the registers a node's own stub call
  clobbers (TypedElement: x11-x15, d1, d2); a value whose range crosses the node takes no home
  among them (its hint, the free registers and a spill victim's alike), and the call saves only a
  clobbered register that holds a value its exit reads. Departure: one home per range keeps such a
  value out of those registers for its whole range, where Maglev moves it once at the node and Ion
  and Air split the range. Measured (fat LTO, main to it, the tier on; my recount of zlib and
  Mandreel agrees): instructions at 5 fixed runs zlib -14.67%, Mandreel -11.34%, GBEMU -1.48%,
  Splay -1.21%, PdfJS -0.80%, nothing above +0.07%; Octane's timed scores on X925 cores alone, 10
  rounds, 1.370 GHz: zlib +21.6% (cycles -17.7%), Mandreel +17.1% (-11.6%), GBEMU +2.7% (-2.5%),
  Crypto, NavierStokes, Djot's and Vue's steady states inside their spread; compile time inside
  its spread (Djot 20.1 -> 20.3 ms over 46 compiles). Box2D's timed rounds fall in two modes on
  both binaries (7.32G and 7.69G instructions a round, main mostly in one and the branch in the
  other: -1.55% score); at fixed work the branch runs -0.28% cycles at equal instructions. What
  picks the mode is not found: to read in the stage's timing session.
- The equality rule (6b6a353f): the loop prepass counts an equality lowered where its own block
  proves the builder's case (both operands made there by a Number constant or a bitwise or
  arithmetic operation the rules lower, or under === and !== one a constant no other value
  equals); where the builder then cannot lower it, it exits (Builder::mismatch) and the NotNumber
  flag withdraws the claim. With the clobber set this is the diagnostic's whole gain without its
  refusals: zlib a further -3.16% instructions and -1.22% cycles, its compiles the diagnostic's
  node for node; Mandreel unchanged, none refused. Its use ends with step 3a for loops whose only
  effects are generic nodes.
- For step 3a: held values win once the call is known. What they still pay is spills: one home per
  range, spilled at the definition and loaded at every use (a6 +1.30G and bc +0.23G words in zlib
  with values held; a6 spills 49 values where it spilled 10). The exemplars' form is Maglev's: a
  spilled value loaded into a register at its first register use and kept there while one is free,
  with the loop's reload and spill hints (maglev-regalloc.cc:1423-1490, 2240-2313). It goes into
  step 3a's brief as its allocator item.
- Step 1 must extend the call's save: an exit of an inlined frame's node reads its frame's chain
  values too, which save_live_clobbered does not yet count (no graph has inlined frames before
  step 2); step 1's rebase adds them with a test.
- Open, for the owner: the typed-array lowering gap (rule U's record filling, now counted). Every
  one of the tier's 758M TypedElement calls in zlib's 5 runs (89% of the stubs' entries; the stubs
  are about a quarter of zlib's sampled operations) comes from an element site whose record is
  empty, because template code's in-line accesses and the stub's hits record nothing: 1,360 site
  decisions with no record against 156 with one recorded kind, whose 545M accesses run in line.
  (a) Template code records the receiver's typed kind on its in-line path, as JSC's baseline
  profiles every element access with one store (jit/JITPropertyAccess.cpp:76, ArrayProfile.h:
  245-251) and V8's and SpiderMonkey's ICs record a new receiver at its first miss: it changes the
  tier off's template code. (b) The tier dispatches on the kind in line at such a site, as the
  stub does (Maglev's polymorphic element access, maglev-graph-builder.cc:5626-5700, but from
  recorded maps): more code at every such site and no exemplar for the unrecorded case. (a) is
  the exemplars' form. By the probe's executed words a Mixed site's typed access costs about 43
  instructions with the stub and a recorded site's about 18 in line (inferred from the words'
  sums, not measured as a change). The owner chose (a) on 2026-11-14 ("go ahead with the JSC fix
  for avoiding the stub if you think it will make an improvement"): it is built as a step of its
  own (rule U; target/p3/stage3/briefs/ruleu.txt), the store emitted only where the tier is on,
  which is off by default and alone reads the records, so that the tier off's template code
  stays as it is.
- Tests: the allocator's homes across a TypedElement call (fourteen words and three f64s, a loop
  phi's hint, a spill victim's register), the consistency check over every value across a stub
  call, the back end's call on three typed kinds with seventeen values live and one saved, the
  exit's frame where the stub declines; two builder tests of the equality rule; 13 mutations, 12
  caught (the thirteenth cannot change a result: every operation the rule classifies pushes one
  value); the tier's tests in four configurations (309) and the new ones under GC stress;
  zombie's check, clippy and Windows' check. On the landed tree, the tier off, on, eager and eager
  with forced exits: the library suite 3,265 passed and none failed in each, difftest 138 agree in
  each, the three fixtures' masked outputs equal the tier off's (target/p3/stage3/step0b-review/
  gate.out).

The retention fix landed (435036cf, 30fc9acd, 544d9c86, 2026-11-14; target/p3/stage2/
retention-impl, report.txt): long Splay runs' dead tree nodes were kept by stale words in the
JIT's activation records and below the stack pointer.
- An active JIT record is scanned up to its activation's extent, and where the collector knows it
  up to its live top (SpiderMonkey's form): a record waiting on a direct call up to the operand
  top the call sequence stores beside the callee's stack base (one STP where an STR was: no
  instruction added), a record inside a poll up to the top the poll helper keeps while it runs
  (two stores on the poll's slow path); any other wait falls back to the extent. The native stack
  below the stack pointer is zeroed as JSC sanitizes its stack. A debug assertion bounds a known
  top between the activation's slots and its extent.
- Measured (fat LTO): the live data after each major collection in a fixed 2,800-iteration Splay
  run stays at 119-134 MB with the tier on where main's grew to 604 MB, and at 118-131 MB with the
  bytecode VM where main's grew to 423 MB; the template JIT's was flat already. A call costs the
  same instructions and cycles (fib(30), 5,200.5M against 5,200.5M). Octane timed in one session
  (CPU 17, 1.318 GHz): 4,364 -> 4,435 with the tier off, 5,817 -> 5,854 with it on; Splay with the
  tier on 4,678 and 4,751 -> 5,101 and 5,101.
- The full gate on the branch's tree (target/p3/stage2/retention-impl/gate/gate2.out, 4 h 25 min):
  in the four configurations the lib suite 3,248, difftest 138 agree, the fixtures' outputs the
  tier off's, Test262 53,572 of 53,575 with no failure beyond the tier off's; GC stress 1 off and
  eager over the tier's four module runs and the fourteen stress sets: all pass. zombie's check,
  clippy, Windows' check and suite (2,804) pass. Six mutations of the live-top rules, each caught.
- Landed on top of stage 3's steps 0 and 0b, which touch only the tier's modules; on the merged
  tree the rooting tests (23, the tier off, on and eager), the collector's (116), the tier's (256
  in the four configurations), the back end's (53) and the poll's (14) pass (target/p3/stage3/
  merge-check/run.out).
- Residual: below a record's live top the optimizing tier's code writes only its effects'
  operands, so an earlier activation's words there are still scanned; a record waiting on a call
  into Rust other than a poll is scanned to its extent. Closed as stage 2's carried item.

Stage 3 step 1 landed (19112f3f, ed2b1611, 387b3f07, 2026-11-14; target/p3/stage3/step1-impl,
report.txt): frame-state chains and exits through several frames. No builder makes an inlined
frame yet (step 2's), so the tier's code and its exits are what they were.
- The IR: a graph's table of inlined frames (InlinedFrame: the callee's first-stage chunk, the
  parent state, the call's pc, the callee and the receiver), each Node and FrameState naming its
  frame: Maglev's DeoptFrame with its parent (maglev-ir.h:1455-1537), JSC's CodeOrigin with its
  inline call frame. A frame's parent state is the caller's at the call's pc + 1 without the
  call's operands, one per inlined call and shared by its states (Maglev's GetCallerDeoptFrame,
  maglev-graph-builder.cc:1042-1066). An inlined frame has no frame of its own while the code
  runs: its slots are values or dead, and its nodes read nothing of the compiled frame's context.
- The verifier checks each frame's nodes and states against that frame's chunk and admits the
  splice's callee set only (no activation, rest parameter, arguments value, prepared entry or
  coroutine; the compiled function's strictness; a frame that fits a pooled record); the back end
  checks the same rules, since its exits rebuild what they describe.
- The exit table: each record names its frame; a parent's recipe is kept once and shared by every
  exit through it (JSC's one InlineCallFrame per inlined call, dfg/DFGOSRExitCompilerCommon.cpp:
  293-415), which relies on a value's one home for its whole range: an allocator that splits
  ranges must make a parent recipe name the spill slot. A record stays 72 bytes and the table 96;
  a table without frames retains the bytes it did (13,495,987 over the measured workloads on both
  sides).
- The deoptimizer writes every frame's values to rooted storage first (the compiled frame's
  record, a pooled record per inlined frame, each call's window in its parent's operand words,
  which root the callee as SpiderMonkey's caller frame keeps the callee and this, jit/
  BaselineBailouts.cpp:1036-1060), enters each inlined frame outermost first with the call
  path's own bookkeeping (the depth and its RangeError, the FnFrame with the call's position, the
  construct state, this bound), runs the innermost in the VM and resumes each parent after its
  call with the result, or at the call with the throw pending (V8's deoptimizer advances its
  non-topmost frames past their calls, deoptimizer/deoptimizer.cc:2080-2094, 2296-2300;
  SpiderMonkey rebuilds a Baseline frame per inlined frame, jit/Bailouts.h:100-131). An
  interruption passes every frame unrun. One exit count and one limit per code; an inlined
  frame's exit is flagged on the callee's first stage, marked inlined (JSC's ExitFromInlined,
  dfg/DFGOSRExitBase.cpp:38-60).
- A call's bookkeeping is now one set of helpers (Interp::enter_call_depth, push_call_frame,
  leave_call_frame) which call_jit_committed and call_jit_fast share and the exits compose; the
  two halves stay apart on the cached paths so that no callee fact lives across the collection
  check (a single helper cost them 6 and 12 instructions; split, they are 321 and 548 against
  main's 323 and 553).
- With steps 0b and the retention fix (the rebase): a stub call's save covers the values its
  node's exit reads through its frames' chain (Graph::for_each_exit_value, which the allocator's
  reads use too); an inlined poll passes the compiled frame's operand top; a rebuilt record is
  scanned to its extent.
- Departures: the chunks a code's inlined frames run are held in a thread-local map by the code's
  base and released with its exit table, not in a JitCode field (which would grow every JitCode,
  template code's too); the callee is rooted through its call's window, where V8 and JSC write
  the function into the rebuilt frame. Not built here: JSC's loop rule (D12: step 2's), error
  positions inside inlined frames (D11: step 3's).
- Measured (fat LTO, 387b3f07's tree against 66ee302d; target/p3/stage3/merge-check): generated
  code identical after normalization with the tier off and on (the agent's run against
  87b38dea); instructions at 5 fixed runs, Octane's geomean +0.025% with the tier off and +0.08%
  with it on (TypeScript's bimodal +0.57% in it; without it +0.05%). One residue beyond noise:
  RayTrace +0.26% off and +0.29% on, and at 200 fixed runs on an X925 core +0.33% and +0.51%
  cycles. It comes with the third commit alone (a bisect by commit), in jit_new_inner, whose
  source and callees are unchanged and which fat LTO compiles 11 instructions longer (register
  moves around a call): the compiler's allocation in an unchanged function, not the step's paths.
  The agent's EarleyBoyer residue against 87b38dea (+0.81% off, more conservative retention) is
  gone on the retention fix (-0.03%). Djot's measured pass reads +0.14% and +0.24% instructions
  in the counts and no cycles at fixed work (-0.4%, inside a 2% spread). To be read again in the
  stage's session.
- Tests: opt::frames_tests, 21 on hand-made graphs (two- and three-frame chains exiting at each
  depth; values in registers, spill slots and constants; a constant callee behind its identity
  test; results, throws caught and passed, an interruption, a poll's throw; collections in the
  inner run; Error.stack and f.caller equal to the VM's; receivers bound as calls bind them;
  forced exits at every check; the depth check at a rebuilt frame; a declined typed-array stub
  call in an inlined frame with a parent value in a clobbered register; the verifier's 20
  refusals and the back end's 6), in six configurations with GC stress; 41 mutations, each caught
  (one, an interruption run through a parent's catch, only after its test learned to count the
  catch: an "interrupted" outcome alone does not prove a catch did not run, which the poll and
  interrupt tests elsewhere may also assume); the tier's tests in four configurations (277);
  zombie's check, clippy, Windows' check and suite (2,819). Not tested for want of a builder: the
  phi selector's frame for the nodes it makes in an inlined frame's loop, and an inlined poll's
  compiled-frame top: step 2's.
- After it, 26a0c2c7: the back end passes clippy on ARM64 with warnings denied (main's two known
  lints removed). On that tree, the tier off, on, eager and eager with forced exits: the library
  suite 3,292 passed and none failed in each, difftest 138 agree in each, the three fixtures'
  masked outputs equal the tier off's (target/p3/stage3/step1-review/gate.out).

Rule U landed (d3d2394b, fb21e95d, 2026-11-14; target/p3/stage3/ruleu-impl, report.txt; the
owner's choice of the same day): with the tier on, template code records its in-line element hits.
- The mechanism, JSC's: an in-line element hit stores what it saw in its site's last-seen bytes
  (two a pc in the chunk's ChunkProfile: a typed array's native kind; an Array's or another
  ordinary object's kind tag), as JSC's baseline stores an access's structure in its ArrayProfile
  (jit/JITInlines.h:328-348, ArrayProfile.h:245-246); the tier ORs them into the element records
  where it reads them, at the compile's copy and at each checkpoint's fingerprint, and resets
  them, as computeUpdatedPrediction does at the DFG's read and at every tier-up check
  (bytecode/ArrayProfile.cpp:126-136, dfg/DFGByteCodeParser.cpp:1354-1359, jit/JITOperations.cpp:
  2826-2829). A record that holds more keeps it. A wrong guess from a last-seen value is
  corrected as before: the tier's check exits, the VM records, the recompile widens.
- Where: every element form of the template JIT (stack, local, the numeric chain, the register
  regions, the fused name-element site; a loop chain once a loop entry). Through the shared
  typed-array stub the site stores after the stub's hit (the stub's code is unchanged; its
  contract now names w17, the kind); a second stage or a splice stores to the byte of the
  baseline site its record map names. Four instructions a recorded hit (the address and a byte
  store).
- The gate: emitted only where the tier is on (it is off by default, and nothing reads the
  records then) and only for chunks the tier would compile (not over its size limit, not
  resumable), as JSC's baseline profiles only where the DFG could compile (jit/JIT.cpp:722-733).
  The tier off's template code is identical (masked hashes and normalized dumps) and its
  instruction counts equal.
- Measured (fat LTO against 544d9c86, the tier on; my recount of the first seven agrees):
  instructions at 5 fixed runs zlib -29.05%, Mandreel -24.51%, NavierStokes -2.75%, GBEMU -1.80%,
  PdfJS -1.5%, Crypto -0.59%, Richards -0.53%, Djot's pass -0.39%; Box2D +1.53% at 5 runs only
  (+0.10% at 1, -0.03% at 20: one function waits a checkpoint longer); nothing else above +0.05%.
  The tier's stub calls a run: zlib 758.0M -> 15.5M, Mandreel 217.6M -> 0, GBEMU 7.9M -> 0.07M;
  element sites compiled for one recorded kind 995 -> 4,571 decisions (665.6M -> 1,635.7M
  executions), with an empty record 6,541 -> 1,445 (1,009M -> 21.5M). Octane's timed scores on
  X925 cores alone, 10 rounds: zlib +26.3% (cycles -18.0%; -16.8% at fixed work in my run),
  Mandreel +43.1%, NavierStokes +3.2%, GBEMU +1.9%, PdfJS +1.1%; the others within 0.2%. Template
  code's cost with the tier on: zlib 28.8M recording stores a run (0.19% of its instructions),
  Mandreel 55.6M (0.9%); template code 0.1-3.2% larger. Compile time within noise. No new
  WrongElements exit anywhere.
- Vue's steady state and the binary's layout. At fixed work on one X925 core Vue's steady state
  ran +1.2% cycles with the tier off and +1.8% with it on at equal instructions on the agent's
  binary, where the tier off runs none of the new code; on the rebased pair (8d67e365 against
  fb21e95d) +0.54% and +0.75%, React 0. Four of the day's binaries interleaved (target/p3/stage3/
  merge-check/cycles-drift.out; Vue's steady state, cycles against stage 2's exit build, the
  tier off and on): 544d9c86 -1.79% and -2.41% (instructions -0.69%, -0.74%), 8d67e365 with step 1
  -0.95% and -1.13%, fb21e95d with rule U -0.40% and -0.44%. So Vue's cycles move about 1% either
  way with the layout of the Rust binary at equal instructions, 544d9c86 was a fast draw, and
  rule U's binary is inside that band and under stage 2's exit. A profile by symbol shows the
  difference spread over the runtime's functions, none standing out. To be read in the stage's
  session; the exemplars order their binaries' functions by profile (PGO, order files), which
  TRust's build does not: a question for the owner (below).
- Tests: 17 new (every template form and native kind, the in-line path, declined views, the tier
  off and an oversized chunk recording nothing, second-stage and splice hits, three builder tests
  from frozen views, three end-to-end: typed sites in line with no stub call, Array and object
  sites without the kind branch, alternating sites that settle within two discards); 28
  mutations, each caught; jit:: and opt:: 815 in four configurations; difftest and the fixtures
  in four; zombie's check, clippy, Windows' check and suite (2,822). On the landed tree
  (fb21e95d), the tier off, on, eager and eager with forced exits: the library suite 3,309 passed
  and none failed in each, difftest 138 agree in each, the fixtures' masked outputs equal the
  tier off's (target/p3/stage3/ruleu-review/gate.out).
- Open, from its counts: (1) the sites still without a record (zlib's 15.5M stub calls, React's
  steady state 4.1M executions): with template code recording, an empty record now means the
  site did not run there either, so Maglev's deopt on no feedback (maglev-graph-builder.cc:
  6494-6497) and the plan's never-ran rule could take element sites: to be counted first.
  (2) Value records in JSC's form (its baseline notes a double on its in-line double path, jit/
  JITAddGenerator.cpp:158-160): sites computing in f64 with neither an int32 hint nor a proof
  execute 787.8M times over the workloads (zlib 391.8M, Mandreel 187.7M; an upper bound); the
  store would sit on arithmetic paths, hotter than element paths: the owner's to decide after a
  count of what it would buy. (3) TypedData, the typed array's data address and its checks,
  executes 666M times for 9.38G of zlib's 61G instructions, 1.74G of Mandreel's 24.8G: derived
  again after every loop header. Step 0c keeps it through loops without effects.
- For the owner: the layout question above. The fixtures' steady states spend most of their
  cycles in the runtime's Rust code, whose function order the build leaves to the compiler; V8,
  SpiderMonkey and JSC ship with profile-guided optimization and ordered text. A measured
  experiment (PGO or a link order file from the fixtures' and Octane's profiles) would say what
  it is worth and would also steady these readings.

Stage 3 step 2 landed (de39cacd, 945d2435, 7a0f5f61, 320a9b10, 0228810f, 2026-11-14; target/p3/
stage3/step2-impl, report.txt and design-note.txt): the graph inliner for callees without effects,
behind TRUST_JS_OPT_INLINE=graph. The default stays splice, at which the tier's code is identical
after normalization (1,339 of 1,339 compiles) and the tier off's template code identical.
- At graph, a function whose inline plan's ways are all leaves is compiled in place on its first
  stage with its callees built into its graph, each behind the guard a splice has and in an
  inlined frame of step 1's; every other plan splices as before.
- The oracle (opt/oracle.rs), on the main thread at the job's preparation, as SpiderMonkey's
  WarpOracle snapshots each inlined script there (jit/WarpOracle.cpp:1404-1554): the splice
  planner's ways (the same decisions, so that the step measures the mechanism), each distinct
  callee first-stage chunk frozen and copied once a job (its last-seen element bytes merged
  first, rule U; its exit profile read as an inlined frame's), each way's guard data and its
  closure's environment records folded, the callees pinned. 0.004-0.011 ms a job.
- The builder (opt/build_inline.rs): a nested builder over the callee's chunk into the same
  graph from the current block, as Maglev's BuildEagerInlineCall runs a second graph builder
  (maglev-graph-builder.cc:7669-7744): formals from the call's operands, missing ones undefined;
  `this` the receiver; the environment the pinned closure's, a constant under the guard; each
  return a jump to the continuation at the call's pc + 1, joined by the caller's own merge; value
  and heap facts flow in and back (no effect ran). A site's guards form a chain: a guard whose
  WrongCallee flag reached its place is a test and a branch to the next way, the first unflagged
  one a check that ends the chain; all flagged, the generic call follows. Such a call with no
  generic call left counts as lowered in the caller's loop prepass.
- E0: a callee with an operation the tier leaves to its template is not inlined; its call stays
  a call (generic nodes in inlined frames are step 3c's). An effect met in an inlined frame after
  all refuses the compile, and a refused graph compile's function splices at its next checkpoint.
- JSC's loop rule (D12): an exit through an inlined frame whose callee's first stage tried to
  enter a loop takes the loop limit at once (dfg/DFGOSRExitCompilerCommon.cpp:70-104).
- A reoptimization takes the ways its first compile took, as a second stage keeps its splices
  (re-planning at each compile made each new callee cost a discard of its own: Richards' four-way
  site 233 exits against the splice's 100); re-planning comes with the policy (step 8).
- Graph against splice on one binary (fat LTO): 514 ways inlined, 117 declined by E0 (EqEq 21,
  GetProp 18, NotEq 16, GetPropThis 11, GetElem 11, TypeofIs 10, AppendProp 10, others 20), 297
  plans left to the splice for a callee that calls. Instructions at 5 fixed runs: Djot -1.08%
  (steady state -0.89%), Mandreel -0.29%, Richards -0.24%, Crypto -0.19%; Earley-Boyer +2.63%,
  GBEMU +1.48%, Vue +0.40% (steady +0.53%); Octane's geomean +0.22%. At 200 fixed runs Box2D
  +0.98%, Earley-Boyer +3.03%, GBEMU +1.32%. Timed on X925 cores, 5 rounds: Mandreel +0.45%,
  Box2D -0.19%, GBEMU -1.86%, Djot's steady state 0. Djot's compile bar on the same binary and
  cores: 3.66% at splice, 3.13% at graph; the recompile part falls by 0.278 ms, as the design
  expected (0.28 ms). No exit through an inlined frame in any workload: those paths run in the
  tests, under forced exits (578 in difftest's inlining corpus) and nowhere else yet.
- Why graph is slower where it is: E0's declined callees stay calls where the splice inlined them
  (a diagnostic that splices any plan with a declined way: Box2D +0.98% -> -0.03%, Vue +0.40% ->
  -0.50%, DeltaBlue +0.20% -> +0.03%, Earley-Boyer +3.03% -> +0.75%, the last part not
  explained); GBEMU is the design's risk R7: a discard of in-place code falls back to template
  code that inlines nothing (the fallback's share 6.7% -> 16.0%), where a discarded second stage
  fell back to spliced code. Both are for step 3 (E0) and step 4 (the default's flip) to answer;
  graph must not lose to splice there before the flip.
- The branch at splice against main, my count (fat LTO, target/p3/stage3/merge-check/
  counts-s2.out): Octane's geomean -0.008% with the tier off and +0.028% with it on
  (TypeScript's bimodal +0.42% in it); nothing else beyond 0.12%.
- Tests: opt::inline_tests, 26 (builder: nodes and frames, returns merged, missing and extra
  arguments, strict and sloppy `this`, guards flagged and not, a callee's loop in a caller's
  loop, never-ran callee blocks, declines read as inlined, E0, recursion refused; end to end at
  graph: results equal the tier off's, forced exits inside callees, exit-profile keys on the
  callee's first stage, the loop rule, Error.stack and f.caller from an exit in a callee) in 12
  configurations with GC stress; 26 mutations, each caught; the tier's tests (308) in the four
  configurations at splice and at graph; difftest with a corpus of inlining shapes; zombie's
  check, clippy, Windows' check and suite (2,822).
- On the landed tree (target/p3/stage3/step2-review): at the default, the tier off, on, eager and
  eager with forced exits, the library suite 3,335 passed and none failed in each, difftest 138
  agree, the fixtures' outputs the tier off's. With TRUST_JS_OPT_INLINE=graph over the whole
  library suite, which the step had run at graph for the tier's modules only: difftest and the
  fixtures as at the default, and 21 library tests failing, each a test of a splice's second
  stage outside the tier's modules expecting one where graph compiles in place (callee pickup,
  the transformed sites' records, the inline contexts, getter and setter splices), none a wrong
  result; pinned to the splice in e7ee40b8, as the tier's own such tests were, after which their
  75 tests pass at graph in the four configurations. Before the default flips (step 4), the
  subjects those tests hold for splices need their counterparts at graph where the tier's tests
  have none yet: a callee of another realm, a callee that outlives its last reference, a
  callee's string constants.

Stage 3 step 0c landed (449fa24d, f7547b2f, 30d63461, ad490cc6, 5b4afc77, 2026-11-14; target/p3/
stage3/step0c-impl, report.txt and design-note.txt): a loop without effects keeps its entry's heap
facts that nothing in it can end, and checks the typed arrays it accesses once, at its entry.
- Where the 666M TypedData executions of zlib came from (a probe classing every typed node by
  its dominator chain): 27% a header between two accesses with the array's word taken again
  inside, 21% a first access inside a loop, 18% the same epoch with the facts dropped at a block
  edge, 17% a store's check after a read's, 6% a join. The brief's first form (the fact every
  entry holds, kept through the header) reached none: the fact is keyed by the view, the view by
  the array's word, and zlib's arrays are the asm.js module's mutable closure bindings, whose
  values are heap facts the header dropped and the builder's prune dropped at every edge.
- What can end the facts in a loop the prepass finds free of effects: nothing but the loop's own
  stores. A header's poll runs no JavaScript on its slow path (it links finished compiles, reads
  the interrupt state, which throws out, and may collect: the collector moves nothing and frees no
  attached storage), and only builtins and host functions, which are effects, detach, transfer,
  resize or borrow a buffer. The exemplars' interrupt checks alias nothing (Ion's InterruptCheck
  has no alias set, JSC's CheckTraps writes InternalState only, TurboFan's loop stack check is
  kNoWrite). F64Mod ends heap facts in the builder's model, so a loop with `%` keeps none.
- Built: (P) prune keeps the facts of the words a heap fact gives back to a later read (a
  binding's value, a global lexical's, a live object's field's); (A) a loop without effects and
  without `%` keeps through its header the typed data addresses and the free names' heap facts
  its entry edges agree on, but the values of the bindings its stores write (a static store
  ends its slot's facts on every record; a store to a global is an effect), as Ion's LICM hoists
  a load whose alias set no instruction of the loop stores (jit/LICM.cpp:154-172), the DFG's a
  node that reads nothing the loop writes (dfg/DFGLICMPhase.cpp:404-409) and TurboFan's loop
  state keeps what the loop's writes do not kill (compiler/load-elimination.cc:1370-1486); (B)
  the builder makes the checks of each typed array such a loop accesses in line (the receiver's
  word from a slot the loop does not write or a binding it does not store, the object, its
  kind, its view, length and data address) once at the end of the loop's one entry edge's
  predecessor, exiting at the header's pc with HoistingFailed, whose flag leaves them in the
  loop, as step 0's moved checks; (C) a typed read whose block stores later to the same array
  checks its data address for the store; (D) a loop's stored bindings include those the callees
  inlined in it store (latent: today's planner inlines no callee that stores a binding).
- Measured (fat LTO against main 6fb1b1e4, the tier on; my recount agrees): instructions at 5
  fixed runs zlib -25.27% (60.95G -> 45.55G; P -7.3%, A -18.0%, B -24.6%, C -25.3% cumulatively),
