Lowerings L2 landed (9 commits ending 64878bb6; the code is 7e3f51e8's; 2026-11-14; target/p3/
stage3/stepL2-impl, report.txt and design-note.txt): named loads through the prototype chain are
the tier's own nodes. The first piece of the separate lowering track to land (L1, equality and
typeof, is in work).
- The counts first (the effects probe at c822fbea, Octane at 5 fixed runs and the fixtures): 381.5M
  effect executions, named loads 125.8M of them: one data way at depth 1 to 3 on a prototype
  46.1M, generic sites 34.7M, String.prototype's methods 18.2M, a String's `length` 10.9M, several
  walks or own ways at different slots 6.8M, accessors 4.3M, absences 2.5M, key-checked Array
  holders 1.8M.
- The checked walk: TRust's shapes say nothing of the prototype and its code is written once
  with no dependencies, so a hit is the template's probe as nodes (jit.rs emit_baked): the
  receiver's shape (CheckShape), then for each level the [[Prototype]] loaded from the level
  below and checked non-null, of an ordinary kind, plain, and of the shape the way recorded
  (CheckProto), then the holder's slot (LoadField). No holder's address or identity is in the
  code. SpiderMonkey walks and guards the same where it cannot use a dependency (jit/CacheIR.cpp
  891-909), JSC's poly-proto access too (bytecode/InlineCacheCompiler.cpp 1866-1915). Depth 1 to
  3 (the template's limit), up to four receiver shapes sharing a walk; a string's method through
  the realm's String.prototype read from the context (CheckStringProto); a polymorphic site as
  Maglev's (one CheckShape of all receiver shapes, a ShapeIs test and an arm a walk, a phi).
  Refused: accessors, absences, Array receivers and wrapper levels, the names `prototype` and
  index names, a receiver shape that reaches two walks.
- Facts: a level's link and shape and the holder's field are heap facts of the effect epoch
  (no lowered node changes a shape or a [[Prototype]]: creation stores are effects; a lowered
  store ends every field fact at its location). They do not cross a loop header.
- Rules::lowers says so (named_load_lowers), so such loads no longer make their loops effectful
  or their callees E0 declines (named-load declines at graph 32 -> 11; ways inlined 514 -> 531).
- Measured (fat LTO against c822fbea, the tier on; my recount agrees): named-load effect

  alone, 5 rounds: DeltaBlue +41.6%, Richards +24.9%, TypeScript +10.8%, RayTrace +2.9%. The

- Found beside it (step 3a's agent, confirmed): since step 0c, zlib's fn(d,f,g,h) builds a graph
  the verifier rejects (a HoistingFailed entry check in front of a bytecode loop head that gets

The verifier and a loop head without a back edge (fa793efa, 2026-11-14; target/p3/stage3/
step0c-impl/report.txt's FOLLOWUP and followup/): the defect found beside lowerings L2, fixed.
- The cause, from a dump of zlib's fn(d,f,g,h): step 0c's entry checks (CheckObject,
  CheckElements, TypedData, each HoistingFailed at pc 1010) end the one predecessor of the block
  at pc 1010, which heads a loop of the bytecode; the loop's only path to its latch ends in an
  exit the builder makes where an operation the rules lower cannot take its operands
  (Builder::mismatch, pc 1440), so no back edge is built and the block never becomes a loop
  header in the graph (a block does at its first back edge). The verifier's rule for entry
  speculations knew graph loop headers only.
- No wrong frame could be rebuilt: the checks' exit is the one a real loop header's entry edge
  has (the head's pc, the edge's state; its flag leaves the checks at their sites), the fix
  changes no generated code, and with the rule passing zlib's whole graph passes every state
  check. Release builds compiled the graph unverified from step 0c (c822fbea) on; a debug build
  would have panicked on it, and TRUST_JS_OPT_VERIFY=1 refused the function.
- The rule now takes a HoistingFailed check at the end of the one predecessor of a block that is
  no loop header in the graph where that block's pc heads a loop of the frame's own bytecode
  (Cfg::loop_at_header), with the state's pc and the nothing-after-it conditions as before and no
  poll to compare handlers with. The phi selector's speculations and step 0's moved checks take
  only headers with a back edge, so only the builder's entry checks meet the case.
- Tests: a builder test of zlib's shape (it fails the old verifier with zlib's message) and an
  end-to-end test whose entry check fails on a detached buffer (the exit at the head's pc with
  the VM's result, the discard after its exits, the recompile without entry checks and without
  exits; the tier on, eager and with forced exits); a mutation of the new rule, caught; the
  tier's tests (361) in the four configurations and eager at graph; zombie's check.
- The sweep: every Octane benchmark at 5 fixed runs and the fixtures and their fx3 copies under
  TRUST_JS_OPT_VERIFY=1, at splice and at graph: 42 runs, no graph refused by the verifier (the
  agent's; my own build of the landed tree: zlib at both settings, none, the function compiled).
  This sweep is now a rule for every step before its report.

Stage 3 step 3a landed (six commits ending b5d38feb, 2026-11-14; target/p3/stage3/step3a-impl,
report.txt and design-note.txt): an operation the tier does not lower is a generic node with
explicit operands in the compiled function's frame (the owner's D6).
- The node (NodeOp::Generic): its inputs are its popped operands and the values of the slots its
  operation names whose frame words are stale; its outputs its pushes (Projection) and the slots
  it writes (SlotResult). Nothing is flushed before it and nothing reloaded after it: a slot it
  does not name keeps its value and its home across it, in a callee-saved register or a spill
  slot, which the collector scans. Its lowering writes the operands to the canonical operand
  words at its pc's depth and the stale named slots to their words, sets x20 and emits the
  operation's baseline template unchanged. Joins and loop headers merge by phis, as Maglev's
  merge points do; a slot is in the frame's keeping only after a canonical-frame effect.
- What a node names is one list, liveness::slot_use, which the liveness, the builder, the
  verifier and the back end all read: per operation the slots it reads and those it writes (an
  update's, a materializing read's, a `for…in` step's index, an array binding's iterator and
  next). Operations that may read any slot, and those whose helper runs the VM on a copy of the
  operand stack (bytecode::jit_runs_on_vm_copy: the bridge operations but the scope, object
  literal and Reference ones), stay canonical-frame effects (NodeOp::Effect) with the old flush
  and reload. The audit of what templates and helpers read is proved by a switch,
  TRUST_JS_OPT_POISON=1: the code writes a poison word into every stale frame word before each
  generic node, and the whole library suite, difftest and the fixtures give the same results
  under it.
- A throw from a generic node at a pc with a handler exits by recipe (JSC's exception exit): the
  handler's live slots and the stack below the handler's depth, each read from a home the call
  preserves, where an effect wrote its whole state to the frame before every execution.
- Step 0's rules now take every loop without a canonical-frame effect: an invariant is a value
  live across the header in a slot the loop reads and does not write, and its checks move to the
  entry edge across generic nodes too (a value's check does not depend on the heap); heap facts
  still end at every generic node, and step 0c's kept facts need a loop with neither.
- Bugs found on the way, each fixed with a test: an untagged phi taken for an invariant (PdfJS
  crashed); the notes' block numbers stale after renumbering (four workloads refused by the
  verifier); sibling loops sharing one note (NavierStokes +2.3% until each had its own); two
  kinds' checks of one value moved together, one always failing.
- Measured (fat LTO against 8a953ec1, the tier on; my recount and my timing agree with the
  agent's): instructions at 5 fixed runs Crypto -7.9%, Mandreel -4.2%, React's steady state -3.4%,
  NavierStokes -2.4%, PdfJS -1.8%, zlib -1.4%, RegExp -1.3%, GBEMU -1.2%, Box2D -0.85%, Djot's and
  Vue's steady states -0.6% and -0.5%; Richards +0.59% and Earley-Boyer +0.52% (compile work, the
  agent infers: the same compiles and exits), Splay and RayTrace +0.15 to +0.18%; Octane's
  geomean -1.34%. The tier off equal. Executed flushes 206M -> 0, reloads 628M -> 0.6M, all
  checks -25% (on an earlier base). Cycles at fixed work on an X925 core (my run): Crypto at 200
  runs -8.3%, React's steady state -5.0%, Djot's -1.3%, Vue's -1.1%; Mandreel +0.7% with 4.2%
  fewer instructions and zlib +0.14% with 1.4% fewer, Richards at 200 +0.4%. Mandreel's and
  zlib's cycles are spill loads on their hot loops' dependency chains (the agent's PMU reading:
  backend stalls +18% in Mandreel): a value live across a loop's calls has one home, a spill
  slot, and is loaded at each use. Code bytes -0.8%; exit-table bytes +36% (recipes name more
  slots).
- Not landed: the in-block reload of a spilled value (0.1% on zlib and Mandreel for +2.3% of
  Richards' compile work: it does not reach loop-carried spills); the removal of step 0b's
  equality rule (step 0c and the lowerings read Rules::equality_lowers).
- Tests: the builder's (no StoreSlot before and no FrameSlot after a generic node, a named
  slot's input and output per family, phis at joins and headers, a canonical-frame effect still

- Open: (1) the allocator: a loop-level reload of a spilled value (Maglev's HoistLoopReloads and

Lowerings L3 landed (14 commits ending c70c6281, 2026-11-14; target/p3/stage3/stepL3-impl,
report.txt and design-note.txt): named stores that create their property, `o.x++` and `o.x += v`,
and a String's length are the tier's own nodes.
- Creation stores (a constructor's `this.x = v`: a shape transition) on one receiver shape: the
  receiver's shape (which proves no own property of the name, the landing shape and the slot),
  then the prototype chain walked level by level, each level's live [[Prototype]] checked against
  the shape the snapshot recorded for it (L2's CheckProto) to the end or to the first level that
  holds the name as a writable data property, so that no setter or read-only property is
  skipped; the receiver extensible and no marked prototype; then the template's commit (the
  layout, the slot, the card mark, the shape, the count, with no call or poll between, the count
  last as the collector reads slots below it). Every check precedes the first write. Where the
  in-line commit cannot make the property (eight keys or more, no layout, no room) a call makes
  the VM's insert, which allocates and neither collects nor runs JavaScript, instead of an exit
  (exits there ran 31,522 times in TypeScript).
- Departure: the template proves the chain by the receiver's prototype's identity and a creation
  epoch that every structural change of a marked prototype moves; the epoch is process-wide, so
  another engine of the process moves it (a test exited at a creation its program never changed,
  one run in five). The tier checks the levels' shapes instead, as SpiderMonkey's add-slot stub
  guards its chain without a dependency: a check a level once per receiver and effect epoch.
- The facts after a creation, the rule L2 and step 0c asked for: the receiver's shape fact
  becomes the landing shape; every other value whose shape fact holds the old shape loses it (it
  may be the same object: Maglev's stale maps on a transitioning store, TurboFan's KillMaps,
  JSC's observeTransition); every field fact at the new location ends; links stay, but a walk
  through the object checks its level again, its cell having lost its shape fact, so a load after
  the creation through an object whose prototype it is sees the new property or exits. Nothing
  step 0c keeps through loops is written by a creation.
- Updates and appends on own writable data ways where the value record says Numbers: the load,
  the arithmetic by its hint, the store. A `length` read at a site that filled no way is a
  String's length (the tag, then the length as the template reads it).
- A defect of the step, found by the agent after its rebase onto step 3a and fixed with a test:
  the creation's slow call read its value's spill slot after the save of the live registers had
  moved the stack pointer (TypeScript failed with the tier on; step 3a's longer-lived values
  made the value a spilled one).
- Measured (fat LTO against 3bf4f72d, the tier on; my recount agrees): the family's effect
  executions 56.0M -> 15.0M, all effects -14.2%; instructions at 5 fixed runs RegExp -7.05%,
  TypeScript -4.4%, Earley-Boyer -4.1%, GBEMU -3.6%, Box2D -2.7%, PdfJS -2.2%, Djot's steady
  state -0.95%, Richards -0.7%; DeltaBlue +0.11%; Octane's geomean -1.70%. The agent's cycles at
  200 fixed runs on an X925 core: RegExp -8.5%, GBEMU -5.9%, TypeScript -4.8%, Box2D -3.8%,
  Richards -2.2%, DeltaBlue -1.8%; timed scores TypeScript +4.3%, Box2D +4.8%, RegExp +8.3%,
  Richards +3.8%; Vue's steady state +0.4% cycles and Splay +0.2%, which the same binary shows
  with the tier off too (+1.3%, +0.4%: the binary's layout, inferred). The tier off: counts
  within 0.26%, template code identical. Code bytes -3.3%. E0's declines at graph 106 -> 95.
  The verifier sweep on the landed tree (mine): 42 runs, none refused.
- Tests: builder, back end and end to end over the brief's table (a creation on an object that is
  another's prototype between two loads through it, two names for one object, a setter or a
  read-only property on the chain, frozen, sealed, non-extensible and dictionary receivers, each

- Open: creations at two or three receiver shapes (1.39M, TypeScript); the generic sites (11.5M,

Lowerings L1 landed (four commits ending 1375e3b8, rebased by the coordinator over L3; 2026-11-14;
target/p3/stage3/stepL1-impl, report.txt and design-note.txt): equality and typeof tests are the
tier's own nodes.
- Without feedback, from the operation and its operands' facts: `x == null` and `x == undefined`
  and their `!=` (a test of the value: null, undefined or an object that masquerades, as V8's
  TestUndetectable and JSC's CompareEq on a known other); every TypeofIs (the tag and the kind,
  its rare objects through a cold call that runs no JavaScript); under `===` a proven object or
  Boolean on either side (identity) and a Number, constant or proven, on either side (the other
  word compared as a double, which every word but a Number's makes unordered).
- With a record (the reversal of W4 decision 3 for template code with the tier on, in rule U's
  form): each form of equality in template code stores the pair of operand kinds it met in its
  site's last-seen byte, and the tier merges it where it reads; the builder speculates on two
  Numbers, two Strings (the same cell, the lengths, then a cold call of the contents' compare),
  two objects or two Booleans (identity), each operand checked with a reason whose flag declines
  the speculation (NotString is new), as Maglev builds a compare from its hint
  (maglev-graph-builder.cc:2402-2622). The record stays out of the settled-feedback fingerprint.
- The prepass claims every such case (Rules::equality_lowers, the one place), so loops holding
  them are free of effects (195 -> 236 loops over the workloads) and callees holding them are no
  E0 declines (106 -> 58 at graph). No compile with a claimed test ends in a mismatch exit.
- Measured (fat LTO; the agent's census on 3bf4f72d, my counts on the landed tree against L3's):
  equality and typeof effect executions 78.4M -> 0.69M (-99.1%), all effects -25.9%;
  instructions at 5 fixed runs, the tier on: Richards -6.9%, TypeScript -4.0%, DeltaBlue -3.8%,
  Vue -3.1% (steady -3.5%), React -2.1% (steady -2.5%), Earley-Boyer -2.3%, Djot's steady state
  -1.6%, PdfJS -1.4%, Splay -1.0%; Octane's geomean -1.32%; the tier off equal (template code
  identical). The agent's timed scores on X925 cores: Richards +58.6%, DeltaBlue +12.4%,
  TypeScript +7.7%, the steady states 1.9 to 3.9% faster; cycles at fixed work TypeScript -6.2%,
  Richards -5.5%. The tier's code bytes -18%. What a lowered equality is worth after step 3a,
  from single-purpose loops: 21 to 32 instructions an iteration against the generic node's
  template (64 for a loose `== null`), the spill traffic of values live across it, the heap
  facts a loop then keeps, and about 140 for a call the inliner no longer declines.
- Slower: RayTrace +0.8% instructions and +1.3% cycles: `shape != exclude`, an object and
  sometimes null, speculated as two objects from the last pair seen; 20 exits at the loop's
  entry, where step 3a had moved the check, 200 in the loop, and two more compiles. Djot's first
  pass +0.9% instructions (the recording stores in template code). Being answered in a follow-up
  (L1b): the record accumulates the pairs it has seen (V8's CombineFeedback) and speculates only
  where it holds one; a pair for an object or null or undefined on each side; and a report on the
  hoisted check's flag (a check that fails after step 3a moved it flags HoistingFailed at the
  header, not its own reason at its site, so the recompile pays a second round of exits).
- Tests: the brief's table of operand pairs for each operation against the VM with the tier on,
  eager and with forced exits; typeof over every kind; the alternating site; mutations of the
  claims, each caught; the whole library suite in eight configurations; difftest; the verifier
  sweep (mine on the landed tree: 42 runs, none refused); zombie's check and suite (2,877).
- Beside it (43b415d7): three of L3's tests that assert the tier's code creates now run alone in
  a child process. The template's creation epoch is process-wide; with L1's tests beside them the
  other engines moved it until the test's sites turned generic (one or two failures in two runs
  of three; none alone in eight).

The VM-copy helper copies its operation's operands only (five commits ending 52a1e3a5,
2026-11-14; target/p3/stage3/step3a-impl/report.txt's FOLLOWUP and vmcopy/): the question step
3a's record left open, settled.
- What was true: jit_vm_operation copied every operand word from the stack's base into the
  activation's stack, which the collector traces precisely; below a canonical-frame effect's own
  operands the tier's code writes those words only at a pc with a handler, and a pooled record
  keeps what earlier activations wrote. So stale words were copied and traced. Since stage 1.
- What was not: a use after free. The marker validates every word, precise or conservative,
  before it reads through it (Heap::mark_word: the page table, then the cell's allocated and
  zombie bits, or the large-cell map): a freed cell's word is ignored, and a word naming a cell
  allocated since retains it, as the record's own conservative scan already does. Shown by a test
  that plants a freed large string's word in every pooled record under GC stress: copied six
  times while free, with collections during those steps, and the VM's results.
- The fix, the contracts' form (4.3: a helper's precondition is its operation's operands in the
  top words, sp and the slots): the helper copies the operands its operation's stack effect
  names and copies the results back above the words below them; the tier's code gains no store.
  RequireCallable's stack effect is (1, 1), the tag its VM arm reads. This is template code's
  helper too: the library suite passes with the tier off, and the coordinator's gate adds Test262.
- The poison switch now audits both ways: before a canonical-frame effect it poisons the operand
  words below the operands and the helper reports a copied poison (22 and 50 reports over the
  library before the fix, from the Super operations, LoadLexicalThis, NewArray and ArraySpread;
  none after); after each generic node's template it checks that the words it poisoned still
  hold the poison: no hidden writer in the library suite, difftest, the fixtures or Octane.
- Measured: every workload within 0.01% in instructions but Vue's steady state +0.20% in both
  tiers (the helper's call is about 10 instructions longer; VM-copy effects run in the tier's
  code in Vue alone, 227k times in its steady state). Tests: 9 mutations caught; the tier's
  modules (442) in six configurations; the verifier sweep.
- On the landed tree, L1 with this fix (52a1e3a5; target/p3/stage3/main-review-1): the
  library suite 3,460 passed and none failed with the tier off, on and eager, and at graph
  on, eager and forced; Test262 53,572 of 53,575 in each of those and with forced exits, no
  failure beyond the tier off's; difftest and the fixtures as the tier off's throughout. With
  forced exits at the default one library test failed, the cyclic list walk's deadline poll
  test: under load the interruption comes before the walk's code is entered and the VM's
  safepoint at the script's call reports it, which the test did not allow for (8 runs at once
  on one core: 14 of 40 fail on this tree, 20 of 40 on step 0's binary, 7 of 40 with the tier
  off); fixed in 6c028243 (0 of 40). Under the poison at graph eager passes and on fails the
  known rare realm test alone (a_shared_inlined_caller_runs_cross_realm_callees_in_their_realm;
  8 of 8 alone).

Stage 3 steps 3b and 3c landed (six commits ending fe601a3c, 2026-11-15; target/p3/stage3/
step3c-impl, report.txt and design-note.txt): an operation the tier does not lower in a
graph-inlined leaf callee is a generic node of the inlined frame; E0 now declines only an
operation that needs the whole canonical frame, or a call. The default stays the splice.
- The form. An inlined frame's node writes its explicit inputs where the template ABI's helpers
  read them, for the node alone: its operands in the compiled frame's operand words at the
  operation's own depth in the callee's chunk; the callee's chunk in ctx.chunk (the template
  passes the callee's pc, as it always did); the frame's inline site in Interp::call_site (one
  site a way, registered by the oracle on the main thread, held and released with the code);
  for the kinds that read them, the receiver in this_word and the closure's environment in
  env_raw and env_parent_raw; for the kinds that name a slot, a slot window above the callee's
  operand capacity that x22, ctx.slots and ctx.n_slots name. The compiled frame's words are
  saved in the tier's native frame and restored after the template and on its throw path; a
  forced exit comes before the switch. No record and no flush: the inlined frame's slots stay
  values (D6). A throw with a handler in a frame of the chain exits by recipe and rebuilds
  every frame (D10); with none it takes the throw epilogue. A callee whose words would put its
  caller past a pooled record is declined; a generic node of a frame inlined into an inlined
  frame is refused (no plan builds one: the oracle keeps leaf callees).
- A departure from the wording recorded above for 3b ("origins where the templates take the
  pc"): the origin's chunk goes through the context's word and its position through
  Interp::call_site, JSC's store of a node's code origin into the frame before each call out
  (dfg/DFGJITCompiler.h:133-143), restored here after the node because the compiled frame's own
  generic nodes do not store theirs. Not E1: nothing of the callee's frame is kept current in a
  record. The two other forms, for the owner at step 4: an origin index passed in place of the
  pc, with thin helper entries, changes about 25 entries and the 40 helper cores that read
  ctx.chunk, the tier off's Rust code with them; JSC's exact form (every generic node stores
  its origin, none restores) would add 8 instructions to each of the compiled frame's generic
  nodes, which outnumber the inlined ones 200 to 1 (178.3M executions against 0.84M over the
  workloads): +0.04% to +4.7% instructions a workload (the agent's estimate from counts).
- The switch costs 16 instructions a node, 5 more with `this`, 14 more with the environment, 9
  more with a window: at most 0.11% of a workload's instructions (Earley-Boyer's 146,734 nodes;
  Vue's steady state 352,664, TypeScript 241,378, Box2D 23,775, none elsewhere; no environment
  kind ran).
- A collector hole, found in the coordinator's review and fixed in the last commit: a template
  that ends its allocating helper's call in the explicit poll gives the record a known live top
  (the poll's x20), and the collector scanned the record's words below it only; the slot window
  lies above it, so a slot the operation writes with a fresh cell (an update of a BigInt local)
  was unscanned at that poll until its slot result read it. The trace now scans the window's
  words as well where its extent does not cover them. A release run did not show it (3,000
  inlined BigInt updates under GC stress gave no wrong value: a stale copy on the native stack
  kept the cell); the test that does, a_slot_window_is_scanned_at_its_nodes_end_poll, has a
  hook in the pool's trace and aborts in the heap verifier without the fix, 4 runs of 4. The
  kinds that can write a cell into a window: UpdateLocal of a non-Number alone.
- What graph still pays: accessors. An inlined frame's property helpers are called in line,
  not through the shared stub, whose direct getter, setter and trap calls take their call site
  from the running chunk's accessor table; an accessor reached from an inlined frame therefore
  runs through the helper's interpreter call: 869 instructions more a setter call (Box2D:
  +0.56% against the splice at 200 runs, the whole of its loss; with the direct paths left on,
  -0.05%), 690 to 807 more a getter call. Step 3c-2's first piece, in a form that leaves the
  template code the tier off runs identical.
- Measured at graph (fat LTO; the census over the workloads): E0's declines 46 -> 0, ways
  inlined 596 -> 644, plans left to the splice for a callee that calls 297 -> 297. Graph

Stage 3 step 3c-2, piece A landed (f48abe5a, 2026-11-15; target/p3/stage3/step3c2-impl,
report.txt and design-note-A.txt): an inlined frame's property helpers call their accessors
directly again. The default stays the splice.
- The form. The tier's inlined nodes call a variant of the shared H_GET_PROP / H_SET_PROP stub
  (SharedStub::HelperInlined, made once a process as the others: its code reads the context,
  ctx.interp and the helper table only), whose direct getter, setter and Proxy trap calls take
  the accessor frame's position from Interp::call_site, the inline site the node published,
  where the base stub takes it from the running chunk's accessor table, which names no inline
  copy of the callee's chunk. Everything else is the base stub's sequence, and the base stub,
  template code, the compiled frame's own nodes, the splice and the tier off never name the
  variant (the tier off's template code identical in 2,593 of 2,594 compiles, the one
  GBEMU's own run-to-run difference; at the splice template code 2,174 of 2,174 and the
  tier's code 1,377 of 1,377). The exemplars take an accessor's caller from the node's own
  site too: JSC's DFG ICs carry the node's CallSiteIndex and the handler IC stores it before
  the getter's call (DFGSpeculativeJIT64.cpp:161-193, InlineCacheCompiler.cpp:3310-3313);
  Maglev's LoadNamedGeneric is a call with the node's lazy deopt frame (maglev-ir.cc:
  5301-5311); Ion's IC records its inlined script and pc (IonIC.h:120-125).
- A departure from the brief, taken: while the accessor runs the variant leaves
  Interp::call_site NO_POSITION, as the interpreter's frame push moves the published position
  into the callee's frame; with the site still published an error the getter's own operation
  raises started its stack at the inlined frame, and a toString the getter called named the
  wrong caller (the agent's mutation A2 shows both). The site is put back at the direct
  sequence's decline and after a trap's return, before the invariant check's error.
- From the coordinator's review, fixed in fc428c00 (A2): piece A did not put the site back
  after a getter's or a setter's return, on the argument that nothing reads it before the
  node's own restore. The node's end poll does: its gc_check raises the allocation-limit
  RangeError, whose stack is captured from Interp::call_site and lacked the inlined frame. Now
  the state after any direct accessor's return is the helper path's (three instructions on the
  variant's direct return; the throw paths unchanged). Its test forces that poll's error with
  a test-only request, since no program shape pins which poll first sees the ceiling passed,
  for GetPropThis, SetPropThisDrop, GetPropLocal and SetPropLocalDrop, uncaught and caught;
  two mutations, each caught. What it compares with is a reference stack (the frames of the
  accessor's own error), because neither other mode gives one: a poll's error is never placed,
  so the splice's stack starts at the compiled frame's start with no frame for the spliced
  callee, and the tier off's callee has no end poll at that site. Graph is the most faithful
  of the three there. On the landed tree (mine): the accessor tests at the default, at graph
  and under GC stress at graph eager; the recount within 0.05% at the default and at graph
  but TypeScript's two modes (+0.41%) and PdfJS with the tier off (+0.16%); the verifier
  sweep, 42 runs, none refused.
- The live top: a direct accessor call records the compiled record's top below a windowed
  node's slot window, and fe601a3c's rule scans the window at any known top; the test hook now
  counts a poll's top and a direct call's apart, and the new window test misses none.
- Measured (fat LTO; my build of f48abe5a is byte-identical to the agent's, and the base

Stage 3 lowerings L1b landed (28ad46d0, 08af20b3, 5f46bc17, cb34667a, 2026-11-15; target/p3/
stage3/stepL1b-impl, report.txt and design-note.txt): the equality record keeps every pair met
between two merges, an object-or-nullish pair is speculated on, and an equality with an
undefined or null constant keeps no record.
- A, the record accumulates. L1's last-seen byte held the last pair template code met before a
  merge, so a site whose pairs alternate faster than merges come lost the pair that is rarely
  last (RayTrace's `shape != exclude`: null for a primary ray's call, a shape for the many
  shadow and reflection rays' calls after it). Now each recording equality has a group of 16
  bytes, one a pair class, and template code stores zero into its pair's class byte: V8's
  semantics (a compare's feedback ORed at every execution, code-stub-assembler.cc:13847-13866)
  with JSC's plain store (JITInlines.h:312-319), so no load, no third register and no
  read-modify-write. The merge ORs each marked class's bit into the record and resets the
  group. Measured against the two other forms: an overwrite of one byte gives the gains back
  (RayTrace +0.74%, Earley-Boyer +0.36%); a single-byte OR costs two instructions a hit (up to
  +0.19%, Richards).
- B, the pair. A record of nullish pairs, with or without two objects', (a new bit, EQ_NULLISH;
  V8's kReceiverOrNullOrUndefined) checks each operand an object or nullish
  [NotObjectOrNullish, a reason of its own, so that an Objects speculation's flag leaves the
  recompile free to take this pair], then identity under === and !== (Maglev's form,
  maglev-graph-builder.cc:2597-2607) and under == and != TurboFan's (js-typed-lowering.cc:
  1009-1060: the same word equal; a nullish word equal to the other where that one is nullish
  or [[IsHTMLDDA]]; two objects unequal), which Maglev leaves generic. An object whose
  ic_plain is clear takes the cold call that asks, as `== null` does.
- C, comments: `tag | 2` takes undefined and null alone; the template's two-tag class test also
  takes the TDZ marker, which is no equality's operand (a test reads holes six ways and a
  binding in its dead zone).
- D, the coordinator's follow-up of Richards' +0.67% (5 runs) on C: its template code met an
  object against null 62,438 times at recording sites, on a two-tag path 13 instructions
  longer than L1's. An equality whose right operand is an undefined or null constant on every
  path to it (the operation before it Undef or such a Const, the equality no jump target: `x ==
  null`, `undefined != x`, `x === void 0`, either order) now keeps no record, no group and no
  template store, as V8's TestNull, TestUndefined and TestUndetectable have no feedback slot
  (bytecode-generator.cc:7862-7883, 8010-8017) and JSC's eq_null and neq_null profile nothing
  (BytecodeList.rb:1375-1378); the builder lowered these from the constant before it read a
  record. With it, the rules' prepass claims an equality with a constant from the constant's
  producer alone (the builder took the constant's node whatever made the other operand; the
  claim had needed both producers, so Richards' `while ((peek = next.link) != null)` counted
  as a possible effect in its loop: 16 of its 17 loops had effects on the base, 1 now).
- Measured (fat LTO; my recount of cb34667a against f48abe5a, merge-check/counts-l1b.out, the
  agent's against 7875566a agree to the digit shown): instructions at 5 fixed runs, the tier
  on: RayTrace -0.82%, Richards -0.62%, Vue -0.08%, Octane's geomean -0.10%; TypeScript -0.27%
  and PdfJS +0.11% inside their runs' spread; every other within 0.04%. At 200 runs (the
  agent's): DeltaBlue -0.63%, Richards -0.57%, TypeScript -0.19%, RayTrace -0.17%,
  Earley-Boyer +0.14% inside its 0.16% spread. The tier off within 0.03% but Splay -0.17% and
  PdfJS +0.14% (layout and the marker's schedule; its template code identical, 3,062 of 3,062
  compiles). By the synchronous logs over the workloads: equality effects 695K -> 275K,
  NotObject exits 567 -> 154, NotString 114 -> 14, RayTrace's exits 400 -> 200 and its
  HoistingFailed and NotObject exits gone (one discard, as before L1), no NotObjectOrNullish
  exit anywhere; loops free of effects 250 -> 266 of 1,034. Groups: 3,647 sites, 73 KB.
- TypeScript on one CPU with incremental marking off counts +0.5% (the agent's): one major
  collection more in 5 runs (10 against 9: the collection before it kept 173K objects where
  the base's kept 518K, so its budget grew less), not code: with major collections pushed out
  of the run the binaries count -0.1%.
- Cycles at fixed work (mine, core 5 at 1.38 GHz delivered, five alternating rounds,
  merge-check/cycles-l1b.out): DeltaBlue at 200 runs -1.40%, Richards at 200 -0.50%, Vue's
  steady state -0.27%, Earley-Boyer -0.10%, TypeScript +0.09%, RayTrace at 200 +0.11%; Djot's
  steady state +1.60% at equal instructions, of which +1.20% shows with the tier off as well,
  where the generated code is identical, and Vue's with the tier off is -1.24%: the binaries'
  layout, the size of what cycles can tell here. The agent's ten rounds over nine benchmarks:
  geomean -0.03%, RayTrace at 5 runs -1.4%, TypeScript +1.7% (the collection).
- Tests: the equality module's (the record's accumulation, each class, the pair's results over
  a 25-value table with $262.IsHTMLDDA, the no-record sites and their claim, convergence in
  one discard), 7 mutations of D each caught (three after the claim's test was made to check
  the loop's heap facts), earlier mutations for A and B; the whole library suite on the final
  tree in 8 legs (3,470 each; two legs with one failure, the creation test 6383ebda now runs
  alone); the verifier sweep (mine on the landed binary: 42 runs, none refused); zombie's
  check, clippy, Windows' check and suite (2,890). On the
  merged tree (mine, cb34667a over step 3c-2's piece A): the tier's modules (464 tests) with
  the tier off, on, with forced exits and at graph eager, none failing; fmt and clippy.
- For the exit policy's next step, from the agent's reading of RayTrace before this record: a
  check the phi selector hoists exits HoistingFailed at the loop's header, which disables the
  hoist but teaches the operation nothing, so the operation exits again until a second
  discard (220 exits, two compiles more than needed). JSC makes a control-equivalent hoisted
  check's exit an ordinary exit of its own kind at its own index (DFGLICMPhase.cpp:254-269);
  Ion's baseline fallback records the case (BaselineBailouts.cpp:2061-2087). Step L1c, started,
  with the agent's fork (7) widened: no record at any equality the builder decides from a
  constant before it reads one.
- Found beside it, for the owner (not the plan's): the parser makes every reference to the
  name `undefined` the literal, so a parameter, var, let or catch binding of that name is
  ignored in every tier (target/p3/stage3/stepL1b-review/shadowed-undefined.js). V8 treats only
  the unshadowed global as the literal (ast/ast.cc:107-117). records_equality keys on the
  compiled Undef and stays right when that is fixed.

Stage 3 step L1c landed (811edda4 piece 1, 36a32df3 piece 2, 9c75a199 piece 1's follow-up,
2026-11-15; target/p3/stage3/stepL1c-impl, report.txt and design-note.txt): a check moved to a
loop's entry edge keeps the checks it stands for, and its exit is their operations' ordinary exit
where every one of them runs whenever the loop is entered; and an equality that a constant
operand decides keeps no record.
Piece 1, the moved check's exit:
- What is kept. The selector's moves (CheckNumber, CheckInt32, CheckObject, CheckElements of an
  invariant) and step 0c's typed arrays' entry checks (CheckObject, CheckElements(Typed),
  TypedData) each list the checks they replaced: pc, frame, own reason, and whether the check's
  block runs whenever the loop is entered (Graph::moved; the back end copies the lists into the
  exit table, ExitTable::moved, by the record's index: data alone, 12 bytes a site, counted in
  the table's retained bytes; the tier's code identical, 1,521 of 1,521 compiles over the
  workloads after TypeScript's ten are normalized for an address).
- "Runs whenever the loop is entered" is JSC's control equivalence of the entry edge's
  predecessor and the check's block (DFGControlEquivalenceAnalysis.h:46-69) on this CFG: no path
  from the header that avoids the block leaves the natural loop (a Return's or an Exit's block is
  outside it; a branch on a constant takes its one edge); checks and throwing nodes inside blocks
  are not counted, as JSC's analysis does not count them. The loops here are not rotated, so the
  header holds the test and the moved check sits before the first test: only the condition's
  straight line and a do-while's body qualify. Every body check behind the test is blind, an
  unconditional one too (the loop may run no iteration).
- The discard (driver.rs flag_moved). Every replaced check runs anyway: each one's own pc is
  flagged with its own reason in its own chunk (a splice's at its baseline site, a graph-inlined
  frame's at its callee's chunk with the inlined key: the add_site an exit at that pc makes) and
  the header is NOT flagged, so the recompile takes those operations' other forms and still
  moves the loop's other checks: JSC's control-equivalent hoist, which exits at its node's
  semantic origin with its own kind (DFGLICMPhase.cpp:252-269, DFGOSRExitBase.cpp:38-60). One
  blind (or none listed): HoistingFailed at the header as before, and the sites that run anyway
  flagged too (their failure is certain), which converges in one round where JSC, hoisting each
  node apart, takes two.
- The forms compared (the agent's variants, fat LTO): R, body checks that dominate every back
  edge counted as running anyway (JSC's answer for the rotated form of the loop), flags PdfJS's
  copy loop's read NotObject though the read never met null (its 49 failing entries are all
  loops of no iteration, `buffer` null and `current` 0), which makes the read generic for every
  call: PdfJS +0.84% and GBEMU +0.15% instructions at 200 runs. S (L1b's report: both always)
  also teaches TypeScript's and Crypto's guarded sites wrongly. So J, the form built.
- In the measured workloads nothing changes, by design: all four moved checks that fail there
  are blind (PdfJS ensureBuffer's zero-trip loop, TypeScript typeCheckFunction's `targetParams
  && ...`, Crypto bnpFromString's `?:` arm, GBEMU getTypedArray's body store, the one that would
  have run). Synchronous logs equal on every workload (189 HoistingFailed exits on both;
  TypeScript's census difference is the marker's load: alone on one CPU the logs are the same,
  25,458 exits and 114 discards). What it buys is for a loop's condition: an invariant bound that
  became a double, an array or typed array of another kind, converging in one discard with the
  loop's other moves kept (opt::moved_tests, each kind at splice and at graph).
- Measured (fat LTO; my recount of 811edda4 against fc428c00, merge-check/counts-l1c1.out, on
  cores 5 and 6): instructions at 5 fixed runs within 0.03% with the tier on and off but Vue's

  but TypeScript +0.12% inside its spread. The verifier sweep on the landed binary: 42 runs, none
  refused.
- Tests: opt::moved_tests, seven (the class of a for loop's bound and body, a do-while's body, a
  conditional, behind a return, behind a break in `while (true)`; each kind converging in one

- Forks kept for later steps. (1) Body checks get the right answer only where the moved check
  sits after the loop's first test, as V8's peeling and JSC's rotation put it: for the step that
  peels (step 6's departure 2), not here (it moves code). (2) After a HoistingFailed discard the
  second round the brief described comes only where the operation's record does not refresh: an
  equality's record is template code's alone, the VM marks nothing (feedback-schema.txt 5.8),
  where Ignition updates a compare's feedback at every execution; a fork for the record's owner.
- The review's two findings, fixed in 9c75a199. (1) A compile-time cliff in piece 1 as it first
  landed: the typed entry checks' sites were classed by a scan of the graph's nodes from the
  start for every site of every check, and deduplicated by a linear search: a synthetic loop of
  2,000 typed sites (1,000 reads and 1,000 writes of one Int32Array, 12,021 nodes) compiled in
  548 ms against the base's 54.5 ms (a second round 250-364 against 35.5). Now one pass over the
  nodes for each loop with such checks and a block's class computed once: 36.0 ms (25.0); the
  moved lists equal on every workload (883 lists over 22 runs). Nothing in the measured
  workloads had met it. (2) A site is keyed by its check's frame state (pc and frame), from
  which its own exit record would be made, with a debug assertion that these are the node's (no
  mismatch in any workload).
Piece 2, no record where the builder reads none:
- The rule has one statement, feedback_read::constant_decides_equality: under == and != an
  undefined or null constant, under === and !== also a Boolean or any Number constant (identity,
  or the other word's double compare: Builder::equality's cases before its record). An equality
  whose right operand is such a constant on every path to it keeps no group and stores nothing
  (records_equality), and the rules' claim of an equality from its constant is the same
  predicate (the set L1b's D claimed, written once now). Not `==` with a Number, Boolean or
  String constant, nor `===` with a String (the record's pair is read there), nor a name (`x ===
  NaN`). V8 compiles a strict comparison with a Boolean literal to TestReferenceEqual, without a
  feedback slot, as it does TestNull and TestUndefined (bytecode-generator.cc:7884-7890,
  8005-8009); V8 does record `x === 5` (its kSignedSmall makes Maglev's Int32 compare), where
  this builder compares the other word as a double whatever it is, so nothing would read the
  record: the one departure, and safe for that reason. A debug assertion in the builder's record
  branch and a test that builds 23 shapes under seven records hold the two together.
- Measured (the agent's probes): sites with a group 3,647 -> 3,252 (GBEMU 327 -> 199, Mandreel
  752 -> 700, Djot 173 -> 133); recording hits in template code at 5 runs 27,081 fewer of 3.36M
  (React's steady state 23,676 -> 11,160, Earley-Boyer 10,678 -> 3,125); template words with the
  tier on -0.05%; the tier's code makes the same effects (equality effects 274,893 on both).
  Instructions at 5 fixed runs (my recount of 9c75a199 against 811edda4,
  merge-check/counts-l1c3.out): GBEMU -0.03%, React's fixture -0.05%, Splay -0.07%, every other
  within 0.03% but Vue's steady state -0.10% on and -0.07% off (layout) and PdfJS +0.25%, its
  clock's modes (one binary's three runs spread as far: 5,389M, 5,397M, 5,403M); the tier off
  within 0.05% (GBEMU's two modes). The agent's at 200 runs within noise (Earley-Boyer -0.10%,
  TypeScript +0.09%). The tier off's template code identical in 3,062 of
  3,062 compiles.
- Tests: the invariant's test and the in-line templates' (`a === true`, `false !== a`, `a !== 0`,
  `0 === a`, `a === -1.5` record nothing; `a == 0` and `a === 's'` record); 6 mutations caught.

Stage 3 step 3c-2, piece B landed (13e509e8, 2026-11-15; target/p3/stage3/step3c2-impl,
report.txt "PIECE B" and design-note-B.txt): an inlined frame makes plain and method calls.
Under TRUST_JS_OPT_INLINE=graph a plan compiles in place when each of its ways is a leaf or a
callee making only plain and method calls, none of which the plan inlines in turn; the default
stays the splice.
- The form. A callee's Call and CallWithThis are generic call nodes of its inlined frame. The
  oracle registers one inline site per (way, call): the way's function at the call's position
  in the callee, called from the compiled chunk's call; a way whose callee calls gets a view of
  its own whose call positions are those sites, so the template's direct sequence and the
  stubs' position words name them. The node publishes its call's site in Interp::call_site and
  runs its template with x21 on a second helper table (jit::inlined_call_helpers) whose six
  position helpers (exec, call, call hit, intrinsic, new, call spread) take the published site
  where the template's take the running chunk's plain position; x21 is put back after the
  template and on the throw path. JSC stores the call node's inlined origin's CallSiteIndex
  before each call (DFGSpeculativeJIT64.cpp:1000-1007, 1036-1171); Maglev's calls carry their
  inlined unit's lazy deopt frame (maglev-graph-builder.cc:1157-1211).
- The review's finding in the work in progress (reproduced on the agent's first build): the
  published site stayed in Interp::call_site while a callee entered by the direct sequence
  ran, so everything that callee's own operations captured started from the inlined frame's
  site (a stack printed "at mid, at inner" for inner's own error; import() resolved against
  the wrong script). Fixed: a direct callee runs with NO_POSITION (a variant of the shared
  direct-call stub and the in-line sequence under the mode consume the site at the frame push,
  past every gate, so that a declined gate leaves it for the helper that then makes the call),
  and the call's end publishes the frame's implicit site again for its end poll.
- The nested-ways rule. A way whose callee's calls the plan inlines in turn keeps its whole
  plan spliced: frames do not nest in inlined frames yet, and calling what the splice inlines
  lost to it when the first build took such ways (DeltaBlue +8.8% and React's steady state
  +9.4% at 5 fixed runs, Richards +16.4% at 200; Richards' -13.0% at 5 runs under that build
  was warm-up alone, earlier compiles in place: fixed-5 counts of a tier change again).
- The census at graph (the agent's): ways inlined 644 -> 931; plans left to the splice 297 ->
  211, of them 134 for nested ways (TypeScript 91, GBEMU 9, DeltaBlue 7, Richards 6, Box2D 6)
  and 77 for other calls (their callees construct). Exits through inlined frames 101 (100 of
  them one TypeScript code's repeat until its limit: the policy's, step 4's).
- Measured, graph against the splice on the landed binary (fat LTO; my recount, merge-check/

Stage 3 step 3c-2, piece B2 landed (af0bb7f5, 2026-11-15; target/p3/stage3/step3c2-impl/b2,
design-note.txt): an inlined frame's call node switches its context only on the paths that reach
a helper.
- What piece B's node cost: 25 instructions a call more than the splice's same call (20 as first
  built, 5 more with the review's fix): the chunk saved and switched, the call's site published,
  x21 switched, the direct callee's consumption of the site, the republication, the restore. On
  the direct path nothing reads any of it: the direct sequence and the direct-call stub take the
  position from the view's own word and the callee's chunk from the way, and the callee runs on
  its own record; the readers are the helpers (the operation and the call cache from ctx.chunk,
  the position helpers the published site, through x21's table) and the end poll's slow path.
- The form. Nothing before the template. A switch of constants (ctx.chunk the callee's, the
  call's site in Interp::call_site, x21 the inlined call helpers' table) at the template's
  entries that reach a helper: the call-hit form, the generic call, each of the 14 in-line
  intrinsic forms' helper calls (so that an intrinsic that finishes in machine code pays
  nothing), and a cold node's intrinsic section, whose forms are shared stubs. The restore writes
  constants too (ctx.chunk the code's own chunk, x21 from JitCtx::helpers, NO_POSITION), where
  those paths end and in the node's unwind, so a path may switch twice and a throw that
  switched nothing may run the restore; the direct path lands past it. The end poll publishes the
  frame's site in its slow branch alone. Piece B's variant of the direct-call stub and the
  consumption are gone. JSC stores one CallSiteIndex before a call out of DFG code
  (DFGSpeculativeJIT64.cpp:1008, 1036-1171); V8 and Ion store nothing and key their frames'
  data by the return address (frames.cc:3137, 3441-3462; JSJitFrameIter.cpp:319-322): a direct
  call here already records its position from a word in the code, so it stores nothing, and the
  slow paths keep JSC's one store.
- The constant restore is sound because the code is installed for one chunk and every entry
  runs with it in ctx.chunk (an in-place first stage, a second stage with a record map, a
  reoptimization, a function under two closures, a snapshot-shared function in two realms: each
  read from the installer, design-note.txt 6.2); test builds trap at every inlined frame's node
  where ctx.chunk is not the code's own or x21 not JitCtx::helpers.
- Measured (fat LTO; my build of af0bb7f5 byte-identical to the agent's measured binary; my
  recount merge-check/counts-b2.out and counts-b2-graph.out): the call's cost against the
  splice's, plain 278.7 -> 253.7 and method 292.7 -> 267.7 instructions an iteration, the
  splice's exactly. Graph against the splice at 5 fixed runs: React +5.06% -> +2.83%, its steady
  state +10.16% -> +5.78%; every other as piece B left it (Djot -1.90%, Crypto -1.07%, Box2D
  -0.85%, PdfJS -0.56%, DeltaBlue -0.56%, Vue -0.54%, GBEMU +1.73%); Octane's geomean -0.10%.
  Cycles at fixed work on one X925 core (the agent's, five alternating rounds): React's steady
  state at graph against the splice +4.83% (against piece B's graph -2.43%), Djot's -1.62%,
  Box2D -1.63%, Crypto -2.61%, DeltaBlue -0.84%.
- What is left of React's loss is not calls: the inlined Array.prototype.map runs two non-call
  generic nodes an element (In and the data-property definition: 7.86M executions in the steady
  state against 3.85M calls), each paying the eager switch of step 3c (16 instructions: the
  chunk saved and switched, the publication, the restore) though their templates finish in
  machine code and reach a helper only on their slow paths. Piece B2b, started: the same lazy
  switch for an allowlist of non-call kinds, each template audited (its work in progress reads
  React's steady state at graph against the splice +0.46% instructions, +0.09% cycles).
- The default and the tier off: template code identical with the tier off (2,594 of 2,594
  compiles) and at the splice (2,173 of 2,173, the tier's 1,375 of 1,375), the agent's dumps;
  my recount of the default within 0.1% but Vue's steady state +0.18% with the tier on and +0.16%
  with it off (the binary: the runtime's pointer-keyed tables, not generated code) and the
  usual modes of Earley-Boyer, PdfJS and TypeScript.
- Tests: two new (each helper path of an inlined call node finds its chunk and its site; the end
  poll after a helper call) beside piece B's nine, which hold unchanged; 17 mutations, each
  caught (the switch dropped at each entry, each restore, x21 left on the inlined table, the
  restore's chunk wrong, the unwind's chunk store, a publication on the direct path, the end
  poll's, the frame's site for the call's, and three of piece B's again). The tier's, the back
  end's, the traced and the inline-context modules (571 tests): the agent's at graph, at the
  splice, at graph under the poison and at graph forced; mine on main at graph eager and at
  graph forced. The verifier sweep on the landed binary (42 runs, none refused); fmt, clippy;
  zombie's check, clippy and Windows' check. Piece B's whole library suite (3,502), finished
  since its record: the tier off, on, eager and forced all pass; at graph on and graph eager
  one failure each, the test pinned to the splice in the landed commit; at graph forced that
  one and script_entry_tests' 30 ms top-level deadline under a load of 35 to 40 (3 of 3 alone
  on piece B's binary and on the one before it: a load flake of that test).

Stage 3 allocator R1 landed (c5949641, 07069582, 353f15b1, 5d607da9, 3f4b5a7a, 71ebd5ef,
2026-11-15; target/p3/stage3/stepR1-impl, report.txt and design-note.txt): a spilled value is no
longer loaded at every use: a register holds a copy of it through a loop, or over a run of its
reads in a block. The spill slot stays the value's one home; exits, throw and parent recipes and
the collector read the slot as before.
- The counts that chose the forms. Spill loads are 9.4% of zlib's executed tier words and 5.1%
  of Mandreel's, under 1.1% elsewhere. zlib's are loop invariants in call-free loops (64%: the
  typed arrays' data, length and view facts step 0c holds; one TypedData read 15 times an
  iteration of its hottest loop); Mandreel's are values defined in the loop (62%) in loops with
  no register free through them. After step 3a the backend stalls had risen 16.4% on Mandreel
  and 3.6% on zlib at fewer instructions.
- Loop caches (form A). After the homes are final and only where a value spilled: for a spilled
  value defined outside a natural loop and read in it, a register of its class free through the
  loop's hull (no home's range, no other cache, no stub clobber of the loop; caller-saved only
  where the loop makes no call, else callee-saved), chosen by the reads' weight, 10^depth a read
  less one load an entry (Air's frequency-weighted cost, AirAllocateRegistersByGreedy.cpp:
  733-752; B3EstimateStaticExecutionCounts.cpp:42-43), the outermost loop first. The header
  loads its caches before a second label that the loop's own edges take, so an entry loads once
  and a back edge runs nothing: Maglev's reload hint (HoistLoopReloads, maglev-regalloc.cc:
  2240-2316), placed at the header because the builder admits several forward predecessors.
- Read spans (form B). Within a block, the reads of a spilled value with no call between them
  (a call's own read ends a run) take one register, loaded before the first, free from the first
  read to the definition after the last (so that it is no home its readers write): Maglev's
  register kept from a reload until the next call (maglev-regalloc.cc:1423-1490, 1680-1683),
  within one home a value. A cold path's call keeps the caller-saved caches and spans with the
  live values. The review's case, fixed before it landed: a span's register could be the home
  of its last reader's own value.
- Compile cost (the brief's constraint, the review's return). As first built the passes cost
  about 28 us a compile that spilled (Richards +0.16%, Djot's fixture +0.18% instructions at 5
  runs). Now: the forward pass records what it spills, so a compile that spills nothing does
  nothing; the passes run only where a spilled value is read twice in a block without a call
  between or is live into a loop header; each block's reads are gathered once for both; and the
  allocator's next-use lists, a fifth of its time and read only by the choice of a value to
  spill, are made at the first such choice (a saving the base would take on its own). The
  tier's code is identical to the measured form's in 1,522 of 1,522 compiles.
- Measured (fat LTO). Cycles at fixed work on one X925 core (the agent's, three alternating
  rounds): Mandreel -4.26% (backend stalls -8.4%), zlib -2.07%, DeltaBlue -0.90%, TypeScript
  -0.73%, Box2D -0.28%; Crypto +0.18% and Vue's steady state +0.27% at equal or fewer
  instructions, each moving as far with the tier off, where none of the step's code runs.
  Instructions at 5 fixed runs with the tier on (my recount of 71ebd5ef against af0bb7f5,
  merge-check/counts-r1.out, the agent's to the digit): zlib -1.63%, Mandreel -0.87%, TypeScript
  -0.16% (inside its modes), Richards -0.16%, Earley-Boyer -0.13%, DeltaBlue -0.11%, Crypto
  -0.09%, RayTrace +0.04%, Djot's fixture +0.07%, React's +0.02%; Octane's geomean -0.21%; the
  tier off within 0.06% but Splay's and TypeScript's modes. My cycles at 5 fixed runs on one
  X925 core (five alternating rounds, merge-check/cycles-r1.out): Mandreel -2.14%, zlib -1.54%,
  Richards at 200 -0.41%, Earley-Boyer -0.39%, Djot's steady state -0.30%; TypeScript +0.39%
  at +0.07% instructions, again in six more rounds (cycles-r1-ts): at 5 runs on one core its
  177 spilled compiles' passes are in the count (inferred: the compile thread's work, about
  4.9 ms of walks with few instructions a cycle; the agent's 20 runs read -0.73% with the
  first form).
  allocate()'s own time (the agent's): Djot -2.1%, Vue -1.1%, TypeScript +8.0% (its 177 spilled
  compiles of 542). Two of the return's targets are missed by 0.01 and 0.03 points (RayTrace
  +0.04%, Djot's fixture +0.06% against +0.03%), and over a base with the lazy next uses alone
  the passes still cost Djot's fixture +0.19%, Richards +0.15%, DeltaBlue +0.10%, RayTrace
  +0.06% at 5 runs: accepted for Mandreel's and zlib's cycles, on a compile thread.
- Tests: the allocator's on hand-made graphs with a consistency check of every cache and span
  after each allocation and the first form's passes kept as a reference; the back end's (a
  cache read by its uses, the entry falling into the loads, the back edge past them, exits
  naming the slot, a poll's slow path keeping a caller-saved cache, a d8-d15 cache keeping the
  prologue's saves); end to end in six configurations with collections, forced exits, an
  inlined frame's generic node, its throw and its call in the loop. 23 mutations of the forms
  (22 caught, one equivalent: a spill implies every callee-saved register was already taken)
  and 16 of the cheapening (15 caught, one equivalent by design). The tier's and the
  back end's modules at the splice, at graph and under the poison (the agent's, 570 each, on
  the tree main now has); the whole library suite in six configurations on the first form
  (3,506 each but the cross-realm splice test under the poison, since fixed in a8a702ea); GC
  stress over the step's tests; difftest and the fixtures in nine configurations; the verifier
  sweep (mine on the landed binary: 42 runs, none refused); the tier off's template code
  identical (539 of 539); fmt, clippy; zombie's check, clippy and Windows' check.
- Found beside it, for later steps. The typed store's bookkeeping drew more of zlib's hottest
  function than its spill loads: step L4, started. Mandreel derives its global heap views'
  TypedData, TypedLength and TypedView again every iteration (39% of its spill loads): a
  global's typed-view facts through an effect-free loop, a candidate for the lowerings. Next
  for the allocator, parked: a span from the definition (Mandreel's defined-in-the-loop loads
  pay a store-to-load trip at their first read), then the victims' choice by weighted next use
  (through zlib's inner loops 6.5 of 21 x registers hold values live through the loop and not
  read in it while its invariants are spilled).

Stage 3 step L4 landed (6ad38b23, 2026-11-15; target/p3/stage3/stepL4-impl, design-note.txt and
report.txt): a typed-array store keeps the embedder's book (the buffer's dirty interval and
write count, read through array_buffer_version and take_array_buffer_dirty_ranges) only for a
buffer an embedder has observed.
- What it was. Allocator R1's profile of zlib's hottest function found the store's bookkeeping
  above its spill loads. Counted: after every native typed store, 13 instructions (the view's
  offset and buffer, the interval's two loads, compares and rare stores, the count's load, add
  and store); with the tier on 10.0% of zlib's instructions, 7.5% of Mandreel's, 1.9% of
  GBEMU's, 0.7% of PdfJS's; the count's three a store-to-load chain from each store to the next
  (20.7% of the cycle samples of zlib's a8, 38.6% of Mandreel's setAabb). No embedder calls
  either function (TRust at 8bd6b3f, TRust-release-0.3, trust-sb; TRust's removed
  lumen_wasm.rs read the version right after creating each buffer and compared it for
  equality), and nothing inside the engine reads the book.
- The form. The exemplars keep no such book; their per-store bookkeeping is the write barrier,
  which tests whether a store is interesting before it keeps anything (V8's page flags before
  RecordWrite, macro-assembler-arm64.cc:4425-4436; JSC's cell-state branch,
  AssemblyHelpers.h:1261-1285; SpiderMonkey's nursery tests, CodeGenerator.cpp:6070-6102). Here
  the interest is an embedder's: a buffer's `observed` flag, set by its first observation
  through either function and never cleared. Before it no write keeps a book: the template's
  stub tests the byte first (two instructions), the VM's and the builtins' mark_dirty_range
  returns.
- The tier, in V8's protector form. While no buffer of the Agent has been observed (a bit the
  snapshot reads on the main thread), a store's TypedData tests the buffer's flag, an exit of
  its own reason, ObservedBuffer, and its stores keep no book: in a loop whose typed array step
  0c holds, the test is one of the entry checks and the loop's stores do nothing more, as V8's
  typed stores carry no detach or immutability test while its ArrayBufferDetaching and
  ArrayBufferMutable protectors hold (js-native-context-specialization.cc:4093-4105). Once any
  buffer was observed, every compile makes each store test its own buffer's flag and keep
  today's exact book out of line where it is set, as V8's code carries the test at each access
  once a protector fell; code made before the first observation is discarded once.
- What the first build taught (the policy's, kept for step 4p): with the flag's exit as the
  only teacher, every buffer observed made zlib 291% slower and Mandreel 41%: a function of many
  store sites was discarded once a site (the first failing check ends each activation's stay in
  the code, so later sites never exit and are never flagged), R grew and the warm-ups went
  exponential. A speculation without a record needs a compile-time input; the protector bit is
  that input.
- The review's hazard, fixed here: a64.rs's exit_for made a node's second-reason record through
  `exit`, which attached a moved check's site list (step L1c) to every record of the node, so an
  entry TypedData's ObservedBuffer exit would have flagged its Detached sites; exit_for now
  takes the list back, held by a do-while test (a for loop's site is blind and hid it).
- The embed API's contract, changed, for the owner to accept: a buffer's first observation, by
  either function, is the embedder's baseline: it marks the whole buffer dirty and leaves the
  generation as it is; from then on the ranges and the count are exactly as before (the count
  still counts stores). An embedder that observes a buffer when it makes it sees no difference;
  one that first observes it later gets the whole buffer in its first take. Two tests changed
  for it (the native write test observes first; the mirror API test's first take is the whole
  buffer).
- Measured (fat LTO). Instructions at 5 fixed runs (my recount of 6ad38b23 against 71ebd5ef,
  merge-check/counts-l4.out, the agent's to the digit): with the tier on zlib -10.17%, Mandreel
  -7.24%, GBEMU -1.82%, PdfJS -0.67%, Octane's geomean -1.36% (the floor with no book at all:
  -10.5%, -7.7%, -1.9%, -0.6%); with the tier off zlib -2.64%, Mandreel -2.31%, GBEMU -1.03%,
  PdfJS -0.45%, geomean -0.43%; nothing else moves beyond its modes. Cycles at fixed work on
  one X925 core (the agent's, five rounds), by what the embedder does:
    nothing observed:                    zlib -8.34%, Mandreel -8.56%, GBEMU -2.19%, PdfJS -0.72%
    every buffer observed when made:     zlib +1.15%, Mandreel +1.28%, GBEMU +0.11%, PdfJS -0.07%
    an unrelated buffer observed first:  zlib -7.47%, Mandreel -7.13%, GBEMU -2.56%, PdfJS -0.93%
  (an observed buffer's store runs 16 instructions where it ran 13: the flag's load, its branch
  and the branch back). Mine with nothing observed (five rounds at 5 runs, merge-check/
  cycles-l4.out): zlib -8.34%, Mandreel -8.27%, GBEMU -2.33%, PdfJS -1.01%, zlib with the tier
  off -1.96%; the controls within 0.2% but Vue's steady state -1.4% with the tier on and off
  alike (layout). Every live buffer observed a second into the run: one discard a
  function that stores (zlib 3, GBEMU 6), zlib's 5 runs +12.2% for the recompiles' warm-ups.
- Tests: opt::observed_tests, nine (the book exact for an observed buffer in the VM, the
  template and the tier at splice and at graph, with forced exits and under the poison; an
  unobserved buffer keeps none and its first observation is the baseline; a warmed loop whose
  buffer is then observed exits at its header alone, one discard, the entry check still moved;
  a host function observing from inside the loop; two receivers in one loop; the two flags
  through a view made later, a transfer, a copy, a detach, a resize and a SharedArrayBuffer; an
  observation before the first compile, a late one, and one between a compile's snapshot and
  its install). 20 mutations, 19 caught, one equivalent under the protector. The
  verifier sweep (mine on the landed binary: 42 runs, none refused; the agent's with every
  buffer observed too); the poison (42 fixture runs the tier off's output); fmt, clippy;
  zombie's check, clippy, Windows' check and x86-64 suite (2,891). The agent's whole-suite
  matrix (13 legs), its 200-run counts and the late case's steady state were still running
  when this was written.
- Next from the same counts, not built: the count as a plain mark inside the observed path (the
  chain gone for a tracked buffer too; it would change the count's meaning: an owner's fork if
  an embedder ever needs it).

Stage 3 step 3c-2, piece B2b landed (a2a45609, 2026-11-15; target/p3/stage3/step3c2-impl/b2,
design-note.txt section 7 and report.txt): an inlined frame's In, data-property
definition and equality nodes switch their context only around their helper calls.
- The rule. Piece B2's lazy, idempotent switch of constants, for an allowlist of non-call kinds
  whose templates leave machine code only through a helper call (the op-helper emitters, the
  shared Equal stub's call), each admitted after its template was read end to end (In:
  emit_in_keyed; Abstract: CreateDataPropertyOrThrow's in-line form; StrictEq, StrictNotEq, EqEq,
  NotEq: emit_eq_inline and its compact form): those emitters switch ctx.chunk and publish the
  frame's site before the call and restore after it; the machine-code path switches nothing.
  Every other kind, and every kind under the poison, keeps step 3c's eager switch.
- The audit made a test. A mutation could not construct a failing kind, so the allowlist's
  soundness rested on reading; now, in test builds, a lazily switched node (piece B2's call
  nodes too) keeps a trap value in ctx.chunk while its machine code runs, an address that
  faults, the template's switch writes the frame's chunk, its restore the trap again, and the
  node's end checks it and writes the code's own chunk. It found one reader, the collector: a
  record's scan extent where its top is unknown is n_slots plus the largest max_stack its
  chunk's slot has held (JitCtx::frame_words), which piece B2's unswitched end poll reached in
  the debug assertion beside a known top. The release scan is right in every state, verified:
  a code with inlined frames declares max_stack as the largest of its own capacity and each
  inlined frame's capacity plus its slots (a64.rs, Assembled::max_stack), a record whose slots
  are a window or whose chunk held no code is scanned whole, and a switched helper's extent
  (the compiled frame's slots plus the callee's capacity) covers the node's operands. Test
  builds scan the whole record for the trap value. No other reader in the tier's tests.
- Measured (fat LTO; my recount, merge-check/counts-b2b.out and counts-b2b-graph.out, the
  agent's to the digit). Graph against the splice at 5 fixed runs: React +2.83% -> +0.04%, its
  steady state +5.78% -> +0.29%; cycles at fixed work on one X925 core (the agent's, five
  rounds): React's steady state -0.47% against the splice (+3.40% before). Every other workload
  as before (Djot -1.89%, Crypto -1.08%, Box2D -0.85%, DeltaBlue -0.57%, Vue -0.54%, PdfJS
  -0.53%, Mandreel -0.33%; GBEMU +1.80%, step 4p's subject; TypeScript +0.80% inside its
  modes, which its splice and its tier off show too); Octane's geomean -0.07%. The 1.86
  instructions an element still between graph and the splice on a map micro are two loads of
  a spilled value of the inlined frame, the allocator's. The default and the tier off: within
  their modes; template code identical with the tier off (2,594 of 2,594 compiles) and at the
  splice (2,173 of 2,173, the tier's 1,375 of 1,375).
- What is left on the eager switch, by the census at graph: GetPropLocal the largest (Djot's
  steady state 0.08%, Vue's 0.06%; 0.18% once frames nest), every other kind at 0.04% or under.
  GetPropLocal's template writes ctx.accessor_holder and ctx.accessor_slot in line and calls
  two property stubs: a piece of its own if the flip's numbers ask for it.
- Tests: the lazy kinds' slow paths from an inlined frame (a Proxy's `has` trap and a
  data-property definition's trap and TypeError seeing the inlined frame; an equality's
  valueOf whose stack names the inlined frame), collections and forced exits; mutations (no
  switch before a helper stub's call, no restore after it, the emitters not told, no switch
  around the Equal stub: each caught, three by the trap). The tier's modules (601 tests) on
  the trap build at graph, at the splice and at graph under the poison (the agent's); the
