Stage 3 step 4p, rules 1 and 5 landed (69e27b5d, 5ff5ca47, 2026-11-15; target/p3/stage3/
step4p-impl, design-note.txt, piece0-tables.txt, piece1-exemplars.txt): what a discard costs at
graph and the exits that repeat, measured, and the first two of five rules.
- What was measured first (a probe with perf's JIT dump; the fat-LTO counts give the numbers,
  the census only attributes: its own switches shrink GBEMU's gap). The slow time is the exits',
  at both modes alike: exited activations' template code and the exits' VM runs are 25% of
  GBEMU's cycles at 5 runs, 21% of Richards', 16% of DeltaBlue's, and at 200 runs 27% of
  Richards' and 22% of DeltaBlue's. Over 97% of exits are a site exiting again after its first
  exit in the same code (GBEMU 15,204 of 15,350, TypeScript 25,109 of 25,358). A discard costs
  what 10 to 250 exits cost, mostly 25 to 120; five sites over all the workloads are true
  transients.
- GBEMU's loss at graph (the design's risk R7) is not the first stage's unspliced fallback. No
  function is discarded at graph that the splice keeps (125 discards against 132). After a
  discard, an in-place function's first-stage template code goes on filling the first stage's
  caches, and the template JIT's stale-chain recompile then makes a bytecode recompile from
  them, a splice even at graph: executeIteration's plan 358 operations of splices against the
  splice's own 234, memoryReadNormal no longer inlined, and its warm-up waiting 7 checkpoints
  (84,008 invocations against 4,002) on one call site whose ways churn. With that recompile off
  at both modes graph is 1.43% under the splice on GBEMU at 5 runs and 0.20% at 200; it fires in
  GBEMU alone among the workloads.
- Rule 1 (69e27b5d): a discard's warm-up waits for no call site's ways. Its settled test
  compares a fingerprint without them (the call state's transitions still count; first
  compiles keep step 11's fingerprint), as V8's tiering manager hears of no call feedback
  (NotifyICChanged comes from property ICs' transitions, tiering-manager.cc:497-576;
  ic-callable.tq:22-50) and JSC and SpiderMonkey wait for no settling at all. GBEMU's warm-up
  waits 41 -> 21 at graph; its earlier recompiles meet feedback they had not seen (exits 15,350
  -> 17,150) and still count less. Instructions at 5 runs (my recount, merge-check/
  counts-4r1.out and counts-4r1-graph.out, the agent's to the digit): at graph GBEMU -1.32%,
  DeltaBlue -0.28%, graph against the splice on GBEMU +1.80% -> +0.64%; at the splice GBEMU
  -0.19%, DeltaBlue -0.27%; Djot's fixture +0.16% at the splice and +0.23% at graph with the
  same discards, exits, waits and reoptimization checkpoint as before (the binary: inferred).
  At 200 runs nothing moves beyond 0.2% (a warm-up's gain).
- Rule 5 (5ff5ca47): a reoptimization re-reads its call sites' ways before its snapshot. A site
  whose callees the code inlines and which has met a callee its guards do not hold gets the
  WrongCallee flag its guard's exit would have set (each guard of a splice's chain up to the
  generic call whose cache holds the new callee; a graph-inlined site at its call with every
  way's place), so the recompile builds those guards as tests with their mismatch edges, as the
  exemplars read their call feedback at every compile (JSC's CallLinkStatus,
  CallLinkStatus.cpp:98-120; Warp's oracle, WarpOracle.cpp:1404-1554). Before it each discard
  taught the one site whose guard failed first: the first failing check ends an activation's
  stay in the code, and kept ways changed only when an exit flagged them (step L4 met the same
  mechanism). Weakening only: no way added or dropped, a flag never cleared, so a guard changes
  once at most.
  Richards at 200 runs -9.04% instructions and -11.0% cycles, at both modes (mine at the
  default, five rounds, merge-check/cycles-4r5.out: [-11.13, -10.85]; the agent's ten rounds at
  graph and at the splice -10.96% and -10.97%): Scheduler.schedule's second discard is gone
  (its reoptimization's re-read flags its task dispatch's call, pc 348, where ten WrongCallee
  exits at its loop limit discarded it again before), and its long activation no longer spends
  the rest of a run in template code (inferred). TypeScript at 5 runs: discards 115 -> 103,
  exits 25,850 -> 21,964; PdfJS's discards 22 -> 19. Instructions at 5 runs (my recount,
  counts-4r5.out, counts-4r5-graph.out): Djot's fixture -0.22%, PdfJS -0.37% (-0.43% at
  graph), TypeScript -0.8% inside its modes, the rest within 0.09%. At 200 runs (the
  agent's): Octane's geomean -0.68% at the splice and -0.69% at graph, TypeScript -0.27%
  and -0.64%, nothing worse than +0.01%.
- Checks: each rule's tests (a warm-up checkpoint not waiting for a polymorphic site's new way
  and waiting for a transition; a function of four call sites relearning in one reoptimization,
  at the splice and at graph) and mutations (4 of 4, 5 of 5); the tier's modules in six
  configurations on the agent's tree (613 and 615 tests); the verifier sweep on each landed
  binary (mine: 42 runs, none refused); clippy; zombie's check, clippy, Windows' check and
  suite (2,891).
- Decided with the design note, for the commits to come: rule 2, the stale-chain request of an
  in-place first stage at graph makes the tier's own checkpoint due and no bytecode recompile
  (it takes GBEMU's 200-run gap; alone it costs GBEMU at 5 runs, its companion, the template
  code compiled again without chains, to be decided on its evidence); rule 3, a flag that does
  not change the build becomes the site's generic form (step L5's); rule 4, the exit limit 10
  << R (SpiderMonkey's 10 with JSC's doubling), last, on a stated acceptance test, else the
  owner's (JSC's 100 was the owner's number). The warm-ups keep JSC's 2^R; the fallback stays
  the first stage's template code.

Stage 3 step L5 landed (8b24a17f, 4db20728, 2026-11-15; target/p3/stage3/stepL5-impl,
report.txt; step 4p's rule 3): a flag that does not change the build makes its site generic.
- The case. Step 4p's census found a TypeScript read that exited after every recompile
  (typesAreIdentical, typescript-compiler.js 17475:51, pc 72: a GetElem on a plain object
  without element words, a sparse map, by a large int32 key): its OutOfBounds flag widened
  the load to its past-the-used-words form, which cannot answer undefined for a receiver with
  named index entries and exits OutOfBounds itself, and the builder does not consult that
  flag again once the form is widened: 100, 200, ... 1,600 exits, then 2,620, at 5 runs
  (6,400 at 200), only R growing.
- The rule, whatever the lowering. The back end marks each exit record whose reason its own
  frame's snapshot already held flagged at the record's site with the data the exit carries
  (a WrongShape flag with as many shapes, a WrongName flag with that proof, a NotNumber flag
  with that truncation mark: a flag whose data the exit would change did change the build);
  at a discard, an exit so marked adds a second-level flag, Generic, at the site; and the
  builder takes the operation's generic form at such a site before any lowering (its loop
  prepass counts it an effect), as JSC's ArrayMode::refine ends its ladder at Generic
  (DFGArrayMode.cpp:202-222, 299-327) and V8's and SpiderMonkey's ICs end megamorphic or
  generic, which their compilers lower with nothing to fail. Not for WrongCallee (the
  policy's ways, step 4p's rule 5), the reasons keyed at a loop header (each flag there ends
  what raised it), a never-ran block, forced exits, throws; not for an operation without a
  generic form (control flow, the stack's and frame's own, calls): the flag never makes a
  function uncompilable. Kind-bound, as a megamorphic IC's state is its site's: a callee
  inlined where its site went generic keeps its own speculation when compiled for itself. A
  moved check's sites carry the same mark.
- The audit of every lowered form (the report's table): each reason's flag removes the check
  it is named for or carries new data, so the form ends by its own flag: arithmetic, bitwise,
  relational, the equality pairs, length sites, named loads and stores, element accesses'
  kind, key and store checks, typed reads, free names, branches. The one family whose flag
  can leave a form that exits for the same reason is the widened element reads: the load
  past the used words (a negative index, named index entries, an indexed prototype) and
  holes read under the same proof. Those now end by Generic.
- Measured (the agent's synchronous census, 44 runs a side): only TypeScript changes. At 5
  runs its exits 25,554 -> 20,401 at the splice and 25,454 -> 20,397 at graph, exits at a
  site whose flag the code held 6,151 -> 273; at 200 runs 43,359 -> 31,970 and 14,986 ->
  1,712; typesAreIdentical's discards 5 -> 3 at 5 runs and 7 -> 5 at 200 (pc 330, the same
  map's second read, follows pc 72 to Generic). Instructions (fat LTO): within noise at 5
  and 200 runs (TypeScript -0.10% at 200, its runs' ranges overlapping). My recount
  (merge-check/counts-l5.out): within its modes, but Richards +0.07% and Djot's fixture +0.06%
  at both modes with spreads of 0.002%, where no flag is set: the rule's standing work in a
  compile (the test before every lowering, the pass over the exit records); a follow-up asked
  of the agent, the two tests made early-outs.
- Tests: opt::generic_tests, nine, at the splice and at graph (the sparse-map read: two
  discards, then Generic and no exit, results the VM's; a flag that ends its exits gets no
  Generic; grown shapes and another reason's exit are not held; a moved site; an inlined
  callee at both modes; forced exits flag nothing; Generic planted at an element read, a
  named read, an add and a strict equality: each generic, the prepass agreeing). 11
  mutations, each caught. Clippy; the verifier sweep on the landed binary (mine: 42 runs,
  none refused); zombie's check, clippy, Windows' check and x86-64 suite (2,891).
- For a later lowering, not built: SpiderMonkey reads a receiver's sparse index entries by a
  guarded stub (CacheIR.cpp:3067-3143; WarpCacheIRTranspiler.cpp:2383), a better second form
  than generic for these two sites.
Piece B's whole library suite, finished since its record (the agent's, 3,502 tests): the tier
off, on, eager and forced pass; at graph on and graph eager one failure each, the test pinned
to the splice in the landed commit; at graph forced that one and the 30 ms deadline test
(recorded with piece B2); its two poison configurations were still running. Step 4p's rule 5
at 200 runs, counted since its record: TypeScript's discards 189 -> 161 at the splice and 184
-> 157 at graph (exits 44,161 -> 37,145), DeltaBlue's unchanged.

Stage 3 step 4p, rule 2 landed (ae360f6a, d1b6ae23, 2026-11-15; target/p3/stage3/step4p-impl,
rule2b-evidence.txt and rule2b-results.txt): under graph inlining a first stage the tier compiled
in place makes no bytecode recompile for its stale chains. The design's risk R7 is closed: GBEMU
at graph is under the splice at every measure.
- (a) The stale-chain request of such a function makes its armed warm-up's checkpoint due at
  that entry (the tier's own reoptimization, the first compile's ways kept, the settled rule
  applying), where it made a bytecode recompile with a fresh plan of splices. Alone it took
  GBEMU's 200-run gap (+1.23% -> -0.04% against the splice) and cost it at 5 runs (+0.62% ->
  +2.37%): the settled rule then waits at checkpoints 2 to 4 for the property sites the
  stale chains' bails are teaching (37, 5 and 4 of them changing), and meanwhile the fallback's
  template code runs its five stale chains on every pass (1.06M helper-reached operations
  against 60K).
- (b) So the function gives its property chains up at the request, as the chain recompile's
  own new chunk builds none: it is marked to build no chain, its record of chains cleared and
  its replay count reset (the request is not made again); the slot's resident template code,
  which a warm-up runs, is compiled again without chains and the old one retired until no
  activation runs it; the kept fallback is discarded, so the next discard compiles one
  without chains; the borrowed-frame code is discarded, so the next loop transfer compiles
  one (an activation in flight finishes in the code it holds). The exemplars' baseline code
  keeps no state that a later shape makes stale at its site (Sparkplug calls the LoadIC
  builtin with the site's slot, baseline-compiler.cc:1071-1083; JSC's baseline cache repatches
  at a miss and, giving up, to the slow-path call, Repatch.cpp:754-786). The request fires for
  two functions of GBEMU and nowhere else in the workloads; executeIteration's five chains are
  exactly the five that bailed on almost every pass, so it gives up none that worked; its
  template compile is 0.8 ms on the main thread (the chain recompile it replaces made 0.14 ms
  of bytecode recompile and a tier compile). With the tier off, removing every property chain
  everywhere costs 0.06% in the geomean (Richards +0.63%, GBEMU +0.78%): the bound on what a
  function gives up.
- Measured (fat LTO). GBEMU at graph against the splice, instructions at 5 runs +0.66% ->
  -0.79% and cycles +1.26% -> -1.08% (mine, five alternating rounds on one X925 core,
  merge-check/cycles-4r2.out; the agent's -0.78% and -1.47%); at 200 runs +1.25% -> -0.15%
  (the agent's); Octane's timed GBEMU score at graph 1.8% above the splice's where it was 0.9%
  below (the agent's five rounds on two X925 cores, clocks recorded). The splice unchanged
  (its path is not touched: my recount within 0.05% but Vue's fixture +0.12% at both modes,
  the binary's); nothing else at graph beyond its spread. Graph against the splice over
  Octane at 5 runs now -0.31% in the geomean (my recount, counts-4r2.out and
  counts-4r2-graph.out), no benchmark above +0.17% (TypeScript, inside its modes) and React's
  steady state the one fixture above zero (+0.30% instructions, -0.47% cycles).
- Tests: six at graph, each also with forced exits and under the poison (the request in a
  warm-up: template code compiled again, entry word and epoch moved, the old code retired
  until nothing runs it; an activation running the old template code when the request comes
  finishes in it; the kept fallback discarded and the next one without chains; the
  borrowed-frame code discarded and the next loop transfer's without chains; an exited
  activation running borrowed-frame code at the request finishes in it and the code is then
  released; the splice's chain recompile as before); 13 mutations, each caught; the tier's
  modules in six configurations on the agent's tree (630 tests each); the verifier sweep on
  the landed binary (mine: 42 runs, none refused); clippy; zombie's check, clippy, Windows'
  check and suite (2,891). The tier's modules on main with rules 1 and 5 (mine, 615 tests):
  graph eager, forced and the default pass.

Since the records above (2026-11-15):
- Step L5's follow-up landed (e5bac176): the Generic rule's standing work in a compile is two
  snapshot bits tested first (whether any pc holds Generic; whether any pc holds a reason whose
  held exit could make a site Generic), the flags kept by frame state only under the second,
  and the back end's pass over the exit records skipped where none was kept. Richards +0.070%
  -> +0.026% and Djot's fixture +0.064% -> +0.020% against the main before L5 (my recounts,
  merge-check/counts-l5.out and counts-l5b.out; the agent's +0.024% and +0.022%: about 2K

Stage 3 step 3d landed (13bfd95f, 2026-11-15; target/p3/stage3/step3d-impl, design-note.txt and
report.txt; my checks merge-check/counts-3d.out, counts-3d-graph.out, cycles-3d.out and
step3d-review): frames nested in inlined frames. Under TRUST_JS_OPT_INLINE=graph a plan whose
ways' callees make plain and method calls that the plan inlines in turn compiles in place, each
such callee a frame nested in its caller's inlined frame, to the planner's own depth and
budgets (INLINE_MAX_DEPTH 3, the shared 320 and small-callee 384 operation budgets, 4 ways a
site): no new limit. The default (the splice) and the tier off are unchanged.
- What it builds. The oracle walks the plan's tree (oracle.rs Prep::way): a calling way's own
  view at any depth, its guard's data in the compiled chunk's view, one inline site a way and
  one a call of a calling way (at most 704 a compile; the largest in the census 76), a nested
  frame's site chained to the call site of the frame it is nested in. A callee guard in an
  inlined frame is frame-free (its explicit inputs, the compiled view's data, the running
  global environment; a way that would need its caller's environment is refused). check_graph's
  refusal of a generic node of a frame inlined into an inlined frame goes: such a node keeps its
  operands at its own frame's depth and publishes its own site. Every flag keys at its frame's
  first-stage chunk and pc whatever the chain, as JSC keys its frequent exit sites by the
  baseline code block (dfg/DFGGraph.h:694-697). Kept ways keep their nested ways, so a
  reoptimization nests again; step 4p's rule 5 re-reads a nested site from the outer callee's
  first-stage call cache and flags that chunk with the inlined key. An exit whose rebuilt chain
  holds a frame that already tried its loop's borrowed-frame code takes the loop limit at once
  (the innermost and the middle frame, tested).
- The fork, mine: a plan with a way anywhere in its tree that makes a `new` call still splices
  whole (the note's option A). Option B (such a way stays a call node of its frame) was measured
  behind a diagnostic branch: within 0.2% of A but for TypeScript at 5 runs, +0.58% (the deep
  constructing ways became calls where the splice inlined them); it comes with `new` from
  inlined frames (step 3e), where the two build the same plans.
- The census at graph (the agent's, fixed 5): plans left to the splice 211 -> 101, every one for
  a constructing callee (77 with the `new` in a top-level way's callee, 24 under a calling
  way; the most in TypeScript, 60, then Box2D 12, Earley-Boyer 8, RayTrace 7); ways inlined
  931 -> 2,092; exits 38,189 against the splice's 46,115 (WrongCallee 10,749 against 15,213),
  discards 288 against 299; outputs equal at both modes on every workload.
- Measured at 5 fixed runs (fat LTO; my recount, the agent's within 0.1%). Graph against the
  graph before it: Richards -12.16%, DeltaBlue -5.98%, Box2D -0.94%, TypeScript -0.91%, GBEMU
  -0.77%, Earley-Boyer -0.38%, Vue -0.17% (steady -0.20%), Crypto +0.23%, Djot's fixture
  +0.11% (below), the rest within 0.1%. Graph against the splice on the one binary: Octane's
  geomean -1.92% (-0.31% before), Richards -12.25%, DeltaBlue -6.51%, Djot -1.83% (steady
  -0.87%), Box2D -1.77%, GBEMU -1.58%, TypeScript -1.00%, Crypto -0.86%, Vue -0.82%, PdfJS
  -0.70%; above zero only React (+0.04%, steady +0.29%: piece B2b's residual, no nesting there)
  and RayTrace (+0.07%). Earley-Boyer's -2.86% is not a result: its splice side was counted
  beside the library suite (a collecting benchmark's counts move with load); the agent's quiet
  pair is -0.61%. The default and the tier off: within their modes (TypeScript +0.23% on, -0.48%
  off). Djot's fixture +0.14% at the default with the collector's pacing off is one collection's

  promoted) in this binary as in two earlier ones of the series (9c75a199's and 69e27b5d's)
  and not in the nine others; what holds them was not traced (a build-dependent root).
- Measured at steady state (200 fixed runs; mine on 13bfd95f, five alternating rounds on one
  X925 core alone, its delivered clock moving between 3.9 and 1.4 GHz; graph against the
  splice, instructions / cycles): Richards -4.53% / -7.79%; TypeScript at 5 runs -0.91% /
  -1.81%; Box2D at 5 runs -1.78% / -2.10%; DeltaBlue +1.74% / -0.68% (rounds -1.06 to -0.49);
  GBEMU +0.13% / +0.08% (three rounds), where graph was -0.23% / -0.74% before the step: 3d
  itself costs GBEMU +0.38% instructions and +1.06% cycles at 200 runs. The agent's 200-run

  place that the splice recompiled for), and at steady state the graph's code for them is no
  better than the splice's.
- DeltaBlue's steady-state instructions, attributed (the agent's, on main's binary: per-function
  samples and the annotated code, step3d-impl/db200): one function, Plan.prototype.execute,
  +13.5% in it. Its frames nested by this step (EqualityConstraint's execute, and output() and
  input() in it) read this.direction, this.v1 and this.v2 as two-shape reads, because the
  callee's first-stage feedback is shared by every caller (Equality and Scale constraints lay
  those slots out differently), where each spliced copy has caches of its own that only this
  path fills: 7 two-shape diamonds in the function at graph, 1 at the splice, each about 10
  instructions where a one-shape read is 2. Ruled out with counts: the loop limit's discards
  (4 of 5 are UnaryConstraint's, which the splice also takes to the same count), rule 5 at
  nested sites (none in DeltaBlue), spill loads, the eager switch, kept ways losing their
  nesting. This is the risk the stage's design names ("shared callee feedback going polymorphic
  where a splice's seeded copy stayed monomorphic"), now measured. The splice's own caches are
  SpiderMonkey's trial inlining (a fresh ICScript a call site, jit/TrialInlining.h:27-47);
  Maglev and JSC narrow in the graph instead: an access takes its feedback's maps intersected
  with the receiver's known maps (maglev-graph-builder.cc:4641-4654), a polymorphic load's arm
  knows its map set and continues through the call of the loaded function (:5816-5820,
  5877-5887, 6038 on), and JSC prunes a polymorphic access's cases by the proven structure set
  (dfg/DFGConstantFoldingPhase.cpp:648-666). Step 15b took Maglev's polymorphic method calls
  but guards by callee after the merged load, so the arm's shape is lost. Decided, mine: the
  graph takes Maglev's form, which is the design's step 5 and the owner's answer on D7, taken
  now as step 3f (a census of the sites it reaches first, then a design note): a read, store or
  method load intersects its feedback with the receiver's proven shapes, and a method call's way
  is entered on the receiver's shape group and then the callee's identity. Trial inlining stays
  the named alternative for what that does not reach, and would be the owner's question. The
  lowerings' agent takes the polymorphic read's own form (no boolean diamond; SpiderMonkey's
  GuardMultipleShapesToOffset select where only the offset differs), which both modes pay.
  Graph is not made the default before DeltaBlue's and GBEMU's steady states are at or under
  the splice's.
- Compile cost at graph against the splice (the agent's, all workloads at 5 runs): main-thread
  snapshots 25.8 ms against 19.7 and installs 60.5 against 56.3 (TypeScript 13.0 against 9.1
  and 29.9 against 26.9; Djot 0.6 against 0.5 and 1.7 against 1.6): a graph snapshot takes every
  nested callee's view; the tier's code bytes +1.8% (DeltaBlue +11.0%, Djot +10.4%, Richards
  -5.3%).
- Found on the way, open: an exit through a guard whose callee's own tier code was running did
  not refill the call cache it resumed at, so rule 5 read nothing there until the callee's own

- Tests: eight (leaves, generic nodes and calls from nested frames, each at the tier off, the

Stage 3 step 3e, stage (a) landed (d2f16daf, 2026-11-15; target/p3/stage3/step3e-impl,
design-note.txt, census-e1.report.txt, diag-new-e1.txt and countsE1-5.pairs.txt; my checks
merge-check/counts-3ea.out, counts-3ea-graph.out, cycles-3ea.out and step3e-review): `new` from
inlined frames. Under TRUST_JS_OPT_INLINE=graph an inlined frame's plain `new` is a generic
call node of its frame, so a way whose callee constructs no longer sends its plan to the
splice: no plan of any workload or fixture is spliced at graph (step 3d left 101). The
default and the tier off are unchanged.
- What it builds. Piece B2's lazy form for construct sites: the template's Op::New sequence
  from an inlined frame probes the callee chunk's own construct cache through the view (the
  cell its own activations use), allocates `this` and enters the constructor's machine code
  with nothing switched, its position the view's word for the `new` (the oracle registers one
  inline site a chain of ways and `new`, as for calls); only the helper path (a miss or a
  declined gate) switches the context to the frame's chunk and the inlined helpers' table and
  restores it where it lands. E0 and the in-place test (makes_plain_calls_only) admit Op::New;
  spread `new`, the spread and arguments-array calls, eval and tail calls are still refused.
  No constructor's body is inlined: the planner plans no construct site at either mode (Maglev
  inlines a known constructor behind an in-line allocation, maglev-graph-builder.cc:13082-13185,
  and JSC as InlineCallFrame::Construct: a later step if the counts ask).
- The construct cache is shared with the callee's own activations where each spliced copy had
  one of its own. Counted (the agent's diagnostic build, every workload at 5 runs): the
  helper's share of executed `new`s from inlined frames is 46.25% at graph against 46.68% for
  the same ways' spliced bodies, and refills 469 against 1,244: the shared cell stays.

Step 3f, known shapes: the census and the decisions (2026-11-15; target/p3/stage3/step3f-impl,
design-note.txt with its section 3b, the census k200). DeltaBlue's steady-state instructions
above the splice on 13bfd95f (the record above) were measured per access site on the tree with
`new` from inlined frames, where they are no longer a loss but still a cost.
- What exists: the builder holds each object word's proven shapes for the current effect epoch
  (State::shapes: made by a shape check and by a polymorphic load's arm, kept at joins as the
  union, ended by every effect) and uses them only to drop a check; an access's arms are still
  the feedback's, so a receiver proven to one shape pays the feedback's two-arm diamond. An
  inlined frame starts from the caller's state, so the facts already reach it.
- The census at 200 runs (a shape test with its branch about 7 instructions, each further
  shape of a check about 3). DeltaBlue: the proven shapes alone would narrow 15 sites, 31.3M
  instructions; 138 more sites, 48.2M, read the inlined frame's `this` where only the method
  load's arm would prove its shape; the two largest pairs are in Plan.prototype.execute's
  nested frames (13.9M a site). TypeScript 37.0M and 21.4M (under 0.01% of the run), Box2D
  nothing. GBEMU: neither rule reaches its remainder, which is the opcode loop's reads through
  two shapes (the first stage's cache gained the object's later shape, the splice's
  later-recompiled copy saw only that one): ten of its 13 sites are already one check of two
  shapes with the live one compared first, three are two-arm reads at slots one apart. Sites
  whose proven and feedback shapes are disjoint: five in GBEMU, never executed.
- Decided, mine. (1) Rule K is built now: an access's arms are trimmed to the receiver's
  proven shapes, one arm left being the monomorphic access, as Maglev intersects feedback with
  known maps (maglev-graph-builder.cc:4641-4650) and JSC prunes a polymorphic access's cases
  by the proven structure set (dfg/DFGConstantFoldingPhase.cpp:648-666); a disjoint site keeps
  its exit (Maglev's unconditional deopt, :4652-4654), its convergence through step L5's rule
  and its always-wrong case to be measured. (2) The arm (the design's step 5: a polymorphic
  method load's call built inside each arm, Maglev's continuation, :6038 on, within its
  limits, :6066-6193) is deferred: a property cache's prototype way keeps no holder and a
  shape does not imply its prototype, so nothing can pair an arm with the way it gives (V8's
  handler holds the holder weakly, ic/handler-configuration.cc:119-127; SpiderMonkey's stub
  embeds it, jit/CacheIR.cpp:1086; JSC derives it from the Structure); it would need a new
  always-on record, and its reach is DeltaBlue's 48.2M at today's diamond cost, about half of
  that after the fusion below, and nothing elsewhere. The holder record is assessed with
  steps 6 and 7's design, which changes the template's call feedback in any case and could use
  it for a constant holder and a known callee. (3) No trial inlining now: what the rules do
  not reach is feedback's age and inlining depth, which a per-caller cache changes neither.
  (4) The lowerings take the form of what stays polymorphic: a shape test that feeds a branch
  emitted as a compare and branch, and the select of the offset where only the slot differs
  (SpiderMonkey's GuardMultipleShapesToOffset), counted first.
- GBEMU's steady-state cost of step 3d, attributed (the two agents', by counts): depth. Its
  run function now compiles in place four levels deep (iterationEndRoutine, audioJIT,
  generateAudioFake nested), so the audio loop's callee computeAudioChannels falls past the
  planner's depth and is called once a sample as a call node of the nested frame: 9,629,416
  calls at 200 runs, all but 7 by the direct sequence, 163 instructions a call outside the
  callee, about 1.57G of the run's 325G (0.48%; step 3d's cost was +0.38%). No way is refused,
  nothing runs template code, and an exited activation is not it (67 of 6,136 samples). Before
  the step the loop function's own code ran the loop with the callee inlined one level down
  (by the samples: its own code 3,107 before and 319 after, the callee's 366 and 6,136; which
  caller reached it then is inferred from the plans). It is the policy's (step 8: Maglev's
  depth and the call counts) and the direct call's (step 7), not a fault.
- The tier's and the back end's modules on 13bfd95f (mine, 638 tests): graph eager, graph

- A call node costs 163 instructions outside the callee (8 of operand stores, 19 the cache's
  probe, 23 of gates, 28 the frame's push and moves, 17 the install, 44 after the return, the
  finish stub's 27 among them); the same from an inlined frame. User callees take the direct

  the 163; the rest is the frame model (a pooled record, the frame side stack, the depth

  Mandreel 1.25%, TypeScript 1.00%, GBEMU 0.60%.

- Decided, mine, in this order (the agent's recommendation in each case):
  A. Constructs, the first stage's and the runtime's, measured with the tier off first: a
     machine-code form of the forwarder the first stage already recognises (the allocation from
     the initializer's learned layout, `initialize` and `apply` proven in line, initialize
     entered directly with the caller's arguments, the helper on any miss), then a layout
     learned from the first constructions for constructors the pass cannot predict, then the
     recogniser extended to an initializer read from a binding. No exemplar has a baseline
     fast path for a forwarding constructor; this moves an existing path of the first stage to
     machine code, and the tier's construct inline and apply reduction stay the end state (the
     design's step 9). Built by the steps 6/7 agent.
  B. A callee that is the graph's own closure, Maglev's form (TryReduceCallForNewClosure,
     maglev-graph-builder.cc:11963-11980): the creation's function and environment node carried
     to the inlined frame's call node, the way taken with no guard and no feedback, the inlined
     body's environment a value, the closure's allocation kept. Mine added: the candidate
     functions come from the compiled function's own creations passed to calls the plan
     inlines, not from the callee site's cache, which is shared by every caller of a
     self-hosted function. Built by step 3e's agent after rule K and D.
  C. The direct call to a known callee: after A and B have numbers.
  D. Every planned way of a site built as a test from the first compile, the last a check or
     the generic call (D7). Built by step 3e's agent after rule K.
  E. The holder record of a prototype way: not now (a constant holder saves nothing without a
     dependency on the holder's property; its one consumer is the arm by load shape).
  F. Guards by code: not now; B takes React's case without them.

Stage 3 step 3f, rule K landed (1982beb6, 2026-11-16; target/p3/stage3/step3f-impl and
step3g-impl, the two agents' reports; my checks merge-check/counts-k.out, counts-k-graph.out,
cycles-k.out and stepk-review): a named access checks only those of its feedback's shapes that
its receiver's proven shapes allow.
- The rule. Where the builder's facts for an object word (State::shapes) exclude some of an
  access's receiver shapes, the access's check takes the rest alone, and a polymorphic load
  drops the arms the facts exclude; one arm left is the monomorphic walk, with no test and no
  join. The facts after the check are what they were (the intersection), so nothing is assumed
  that was not proven. As Maglev intersects an access's feedback maps with the node's known
  maps (maglev-graph-builder.cc:4641-4650) and JSC prunes a polymorphic access's cases by the
  proven structure set (dfg/DFGConstantFoldingPhase.cpp:648-666). Facts that allow none of the
  shapes keep the whole check, which then always exits (Maglev's unconditional deopt,
  :4652-4654): the resumed first stage's read records the shape, the flag's data is the
  access's own count of ways and not the trimmed one, and the site converges in one discard
  (to the new shape, or generic by step L5's rule where a full cache never held it): both
  tested. It takes own reads, method loads, chain walks, polymorphic reads, own stores,
  creations, updates and appends, in the compiled frame and in inlined frames; a String's
  prototype check and a chain's levels are left (no shape facts of their own).
- It is not graph-only: the splice's second stages hold the same facts. The tier's code
  changes in 33 of 1,373 compiles at the default (DeltaBlue 2, TypeScript 31) and 39 of 1,368
  at graph, each in a function the compile's log names (`[opt] known shapes`); template code
  and the tier off are identical.
- Measured (fat LTO; mine, the agent's the same to 0.05%). At 5 runs against the main before:
  DeltaBlue -0.38% at the default and -1.58% at graph, Earley-Boyer -0.62% at graph (a
  collecting benchmark, inside its modes), the rest within their spread (Vue's fixture -0.17%
  at every tier with the tier off too: the binary's); compile cost level (Richards 0.00%: the
  first form allocated in every check's path and cost +0.07%, found by its count and fixed
  before landing). At 200 runs, five alternating rounds on one X925 core: DeltaBlue at graph
  -3.61% instructions and -1.82% cycles, at the default -0.68% and -0.34%; graph against the
  splice on the one binary now -8.22% instructions and -12.06% cycles (-5.44% and -10.73%
  before); Richards, TypeScript, Box2D and Earley-Boyer unmoved. Nothing else moves at 200
  runs (the agent's eight: within 0.04% but Earley-Boyer inside its spread).
- Where DeltaBlue's gain is (the agent's profile and node counts at 200 runs): all of it in

Steps 6 and 7, part A2 landed (c2b91010, 2026-11-16; target/p3/stage3/step67-impl, report.txt
section 4 and a2/): a recognised forwarding constructor's construction in machine code. The
first stage already recognised the body `this.<initializer>.apply(this, arguments)`
(Prototype.js's Class.create) and ran it in Rust from the construct helper; the direct
construct sequence now has a forward form for it.
- What it builds. A construct site's entry for such a constructor gains a forward entry at the
  Rust path's first success (the initializer's call entry and the proofs; a flag in the
  entry's direct bits). The sequence's gate that declines an entry without machine code of its
  own tests that flag, on the declined path alone, and takes the forward form: one helper call
  proves, by identity and with nothing allocated on a miss, that the constructor's live
  prototype object has the recorded shape and holds the recorded initializer as a data
  property (the instance has no own property, so the lookup is that object's), that the
  initializer has its recorded shape with no own `apply`, and that its prototype has the
  recorded shape and holds %Function.prototype.apply% in the recorded slot; it then allocates
  the instance with the initializer's learned layout. Machine code pushes the constructor's
  [[Construct]] frame at the site's position, with the depth of it and of the apply it
  elides, enters the initializer's code as a call with the instance as `this` and the site's
  argument words, and returns the instance whatever the initializer returns; a throw pops
  both frames. A failed proof clears the flag and the construction takes the Rust path, which
  proves and refills. x86-64 keeps the Rust path.
- No exemplar has this at its baseline tier: each materialises the arguments object there
  and gains in its optimizing tier (the constructor inlined, the apply of the frame's
  arguments reduced to a call: js-call-reducer.cc:4945-4983, maglev-graph-builder.cc:
  11730-11756, DFGArgumentsEliminationPhase.cpp:1292-1323, ScalarReplacement.cpp:2406-2440).
  This moves a path the first stage already had to machine code, for the tier off as well;
  the tier's own construct inline and apply reduction stay the end state (the design's step
  9). Decided by me; the owner may want it otherwise.
- Measured (the agent's, fat LTO, against part A's first commit). A forwarded construction
  and one field read: 1,577 -> 883 instructions with the tier off, 1,527 -> 747 with it on (an
  ordinary constructor 558 and 441, unchanged; one that reads a global +0.04). RayTrace at 5
  runs, the tier off / the default / graph: -33.9% / -42.3% / -44.2% instructions and -30.8%
  / -39.2% / -41.8% cycles (two X925 cores alone, their clocks recorded); at 200 runs -36.5%
  / -49.6% / -49.8% and -34.7% / -52.6% / -52.9%. The other workloads within 0.05% where
  steady (DeltaBlue +0.02% to +0.05%: the flag's test on its declined constructs). Template
  code: every `new` site's cold gates gain the test (154 of 4,242 compiles with the tier off
  differ by those blocks alone), the construct stub grows from 1,012 to 1,808 bytes; a
  construct site's entry now carries the forward entry in line at every site, whose memory I
  have asked to be measured (out of line if it shows).
- What it found on the way: the Rust forwarder never counted the initializer's entries (it
  runs a field plan in its place), so the forward form declined for ever at the initializer's
  first checkpoint until the plan counted them; a helper slot of its own would have moved
  every field of the interpreter's state after the helper table in 1,353 compiles, so the
  form shares the construct sequence's allocation helper; the helper's proofs and allocation
  are about 330 of the 883 instructions, and moving them in line is the next step of this
  kind (to about 600).
- Tests: every observable against the tier off and the VM (the stack through both frames at
  the VM's positions, initialize's caller, its `arguments` with extra and missing arguments,
  a throw, a returned object ignored, new.target, the depth inside the initializer and the
  unwind of a RangeError); a 17-phase proofs test that checks every construction against the
  VM and which form ran (the initializer replaced, an accessor, a moved key, an own `apply`,
  another holder, Function.prototype.apply replaced, restored and moved, a stale layout, a
  Proxy prototype); seven mutations caught; the library suite (3,588) and GC stress over the
  new tests; zombie's check, clippy with tests, Windows' check and suite (2,899). Mine on the
  merged tree, running when this was written: fat LTO, the recount at every tier, the 5-run
  sweep, cycles and the whole library suite with the tier off and on.

  and Vue between the two binaries were file-backed pages, the binaries' layout, with anonymous
  memory 12 KiB lower on both, a2/rss-split.out, and I had charged them to the entry in this
  record's first form); and the lowerings' three commits (the

Steps 6 and 7, part A: the counts for A1 and A3, and the decisions (2026-11-18;
target/p3/stage3/step67-impl/report-a1.txt and a1/; the agent's counts on c2b91010 with a probe,
fat LTO, not yet repeated by me; nothing built).
- A1, constructors without a static instance layout, which the direct construct sequence
  leaves to the Op::New helper: 745 instructions a construction more than the sequence's with
  the tier off, 737 with it on. Its bound is 1.2% of DeltaBlue's instructions with the tier off
  and 2.3% at graph at 200 runs (101,287 constructions: Variable's first store holds a jump,
  the constraints' fields are set by a super constructor), 0.3-0.4% of Djot's steady state,
  0.13-0.17% of TypeScript (69,238 of its 94,561 are an empty constructor), nothing elsewhere.
  The engine already learns a layout for such a constructor on the helper's path; a wrong
  learned layout was measured at +16 instructions a construction with the tier off and +20
  with it on, with no exit and nothing relearnt.
- Box2D's 4.33 million constructions through the helper at 200 runs are none of A1's kind:
  every one is a forwarding constructor. A fifth are `F.init.apply(this, arguments)` (3,816
  instructions a construction with the tier off); four fifths a form the first count missed,
  that call followed by `this.constructor === F && this.init.apply(this, arguments)` (4,971).
  The bound is 12.8% of Box2D with the tier off and 25.8% at graph at 200 runs; the "8-14%" and
  "about 21%" of the input's record priced every construction as the first form. Of a
  construction's instructions the helper's Rust path is 27-32%, the arguments object 18-23%,
  apply and the forwarded call 17-24% (a profile's shares times the count).
- Decided by me. A1 is built, the exemplars' form: a chunk takes the layout its instances were
  observed with once its construct learning ends (V8 makes the initial map at the first
  construction and corrects it by slack tracking, objects/js-function.cc:939-975; JSC's
  allocation profile, bytecode/ObjectAllocationProfileInlines.h:67-110; SpiderMonkey's this
  shape, vm/PlainObject.cpp:40-62). The departure, a template for each chunk where theirs is
  for each closure, is what the wrong-layout measurement prices. A design note first. No
  recognizer for Box2D's constructors: the second form runs author code between the facts a
  recognizer would prove, so it is a body and not a pattern.
- The owner's (2026-11-18). The agent's next step for Box2D is a template call form for
  `f.apply(t, arguments)` in a function whose only use of `arguments` is that argument: where
  the method read finds %Function.prototype.apply%, the call takes the frame's arguments and
  no arguments object is made, an extension of the rule that already leaves the object unmade
  where it is read only as `arguments.length` and `arguments[key]`. The exemplars make this
  reduction in their optimizing tiers alone (js-call-reducer.cc:4926-4983,
  maglev-graph-builder.cc:11730-11756, DFGArgumentsEliminationPhase.cpp:1292-1345); JSC keeps
  first-tier operations for it that nothing instantiates now (jit/JITOperations.cpp:4324-4350).
  Put to the owner as the same kind of choice as part A2: "The agent's recommendation is fine
  with me. Don't forget that the optimizing tier will *eventually* be on by default, once it's
  fully completed, so don't count it out completely just because of its current status. But
  the suggestion makes sense either way." So it is built, after A1, on a count of such sites
  outside constructors and a design note; its case is what it does at every tier, it must leave
  the tier's own reduction (the design's step 9) buildable from what the builder sees at the
  site, and it is measured at the three modes with the tier-on rows weighed as the tier-off's.

Stage 3 step 3f, piece D landed (9e525fea, 2026-11-18; target/p3/stage3/step3g-impl,

planned way of a call site is built from the first compile.
- The rule. Each kept way of a site but the last is a test of the callee's identity
  (CalleeMatches) and a branch to its inlined frame, the next way on its mismatch; the last is
  a check (CheckCallee), which ends the chain, unless WrongCallee is flagged at the call past
  its place (as before) or the site's cache is past the plan (megamorphic, or naming a callee
  no kept way expects: `past_plan`), where it too is a test and the generic call follows. As
  JSC's polymorphic call inlining switches on the callee and falls through to the generic call
  where the call's status could take the slow path, to an exit where it could not
  (dfg/DFGByteCodeParser.cpp:2726-2770, 2875-2890), and as Maglev's polymorphic access compares
  each arm and checks the last (maglev-graph-builder.cc:5877-5887); the owner's answer on D7.
  Before, a first compile built way 0 as a check and left ways 1 to 3 unbuilt, so a site whose
  callees were all planned was learnt one way a round of a hundred exits and a discard (step
  4p's rule 5 flags only callees outside every kept way).
- Two places must agree and now say so: the loop prepass reads a site through
  Tree::effect_free and the build through Builder::call. Both take the site's ways with an
  analysed callee, none is dropped while building (a refusal there refuses the compile), and
  both read the last guard by the same two tests; a debug assertion at the call holds them to
  it. The splice is untouched: its tier code is identical in 1,373 of 1,373 compiles, template
  code in 2,174 of 2,174, and with the tier off in 2,588 of 2,590 (the two are one constant
  whose value is an address of the binary, a move-wide one instruction shorter and a pad two
  nops longer; identical with nop runs folded).
- Exits and discards at graph (the agent's logs, main's engine before -> with D). At 5 fixed

  discards 286 -> 249, tier compiles 1,516 -> 1,481; TypeScript's WrongCallee exits 8,763 ->
  1,336 and its discards 94 -> 63. At 200 runs over the fifteen Octane benchmarks: 15,182 ->
  2,707, discards 400 -> 322; DeltaBlue's discards 29 -> 2 and its tier code 1,037,812 ->
  357,012 bytes. All tier code at 200 runs 17.13 MB -> 15.39 MB (-10.2%). GBEMU's 791
  WrongCallee exits are untouched: callees outside every planned way, rule 5's population,

Steps 6 and 7 since part A2 (2026-11-18; target/p3/stage3/step67-impl: report-ool.txt with its
section 8, pin/, report-apply.txt, design-note-apply.txt): the site entry's move out of line is
parked, a defect of part A2 is fixed, and the apply form is counted and designed.
- The forward entry out of line: parked, not landed (branch p3-s3-step67, 97c82028). Part A2
  put its 168-byte forward entry in line in every construct site. The commit moves it to a
  record a site points to and returns the forwarder's two instructions by passing the record's
  address tagged to the helper: the forwarder's construction 882.90 -> 881.90 instructions
  with the tier off and 746.78 -> 745.78 with it on, an ordinary helper construction +10, the
  memory 168 bytes a `new` site back (169 KB on TypeScript by count). I had decided it lands.
  Its cycles decide otherwise: RayTrace at 200 runs, five alternating rounds on one X925 core,
  +0.71% with the tier off, +2.09% at the default and +2.38% at graph against c2b91010
  (spreads 0.2% to 0.4%; the commit before the two-instruction fix +0.57%, +1.63%, +1.70%),
  and by the means of 8 runs a side at randomized addresses +2.08% at graph (a run's
  deviation 0.2% to 0.5%), while its instructions fall 0.04% to 0.20%. The counters say where
  (three runs a side at graph): cycles +70.7M, branch mispredictions -0.4%, first-level data
  cache refills -8.4%, back-end stall cycles +102.9M (+16%) of which memory stalls +1.8M: a
  longer chain of dependent loads that hit the cache. The forward form now loads the site's
  word and then the record where it read a fixed offset of the site; 13.3M constructions at
  about five cycles a load-to-use step is 67M (the agent's arithmetic; no precise sampling).
  Two per cent of the benchmark the entry exists for is not worth 0.08% of its memory. The
  layout is settled when part A2's standing is measured again: the fields the form reads
  first in line, the rest out of line, is the form to try then.
  The rule this adds to the method: instruction counts did not show this cost. A change that
  moves where data lives is measured in cycles as well, before the decision and not after it.
- A defect of part A2, fixed (5636b4f9; found by reading, the question was the previous
  agent's). The forward entry proves `apply` by comparing the live slot's value with the
  address it recorded and held no pin for it. Nothing but Function.prototype's property holds
  %Function.prototype.apply% (no Realm intrinsic; the natives' realm table holds it weakly),
  and a swept cell's address is reused: a program that replaced Function.prototype.apply let
  it die, and a later object at its address, stored in the slot, would pass the comparison, so
  that the forward form skipped a call of that object. No failing program was written for the
  old code; the fix's test shows the address reserved. The site's pin cell now holds a third
  weak pin, apply's, beside the constructor's and the initializer's: set by the forward fill,
  dropped by a fill of the site. No generated code reads the cell, so the entry keeps its
  layout: template code at the default identical in 3,582 of 3,582 compiles and with the tier
  off in every deterministic run; the six construct rows equal or 1.1 instructions lower. The
  cell grows from 24 to 32 bytes a `new` site. RayTrace at 200 runs in cycles against the main
  before: +0.04% with the tier off, +0.24% at the default, +0.25% at graph (spreads 0.02% to
  0.15%), with +0.06% instructions at graph alone (confirmed at randomized addresses), in
  functions the commit does not change; the agent infers the build's inlining and has not
  verified it. Landed as a correctness fix with that row open. Tests: the new one (the entry
  and its three pins before the form first reads them; the dead apply's address still
  allocated after three major collections; a fill drops the pins), also under GC stress; two
  mutations caught; zombie's check, clippy and Windows' check. Mine on the landed tree:
  clippy with tests clean, and the 22 call-feedback and constructor-transfer tests, the new
  one among them, pass with the tier off, with it on and under GC stress.
- The apply form, the count (report-apply.txt; a probe build, every Octane benchmark at 5 and
  200 runs and the fixtures, the tier off and at graph alike). Functions whose only use of
  `arguments` is `<f>.apply(<t>, arguments)`: Box2D has 34, which at 200 runs make 4,329,249
  arguments objects in constructions and 186,176 in plain calls and apply them 7,959,885
  times; RayTrace's one is part A2's forwarder, 13.3M constructions that make no object;
  TypeScript makes 1,192; no other workload makes an arguments object in compiled code but
  Earley-Boyer, whose use is another shape. What one costs (fat LTO, instructions a call, the
  tier off / at graph): through `target.apply(this, arguments)` 5,894 / 5,791, a direct call
  256 / 55, `apply` of an Array literal 1,968 / 1,834, `target.call` 1,610 / 1,465. Why
  it costs so much is read from the code and not measured: the object is a mapped one (a
  sloppy function with simple parameters) and the call template's forwarded path takes only
  unmapped objects and Arrays, so the call should run the builtin; no run showed that it
  does, and the 5,638 extra instructions were not profiled. The bound,
  inferred from these products: about 5% of Box2D's instructions with the tier off and 11% at
  graph at 200 runs.
- The apply form, the design, approved by me under the owner's approval of the form
  (design-note-apply.txt). The rule extends the compiler's existing lazy-arguments rewrite to
  a slot whose only other use is the last argument of a method call of `apply`; a sloppy
  function with simple parameters qualifies only where no parameter is written or captured
  (V8's condition for mapped arguments, js-call-reducer.cc:4891-4900, decided here on the
  bytecode). One new operation, CallApplyFrameArgs: where `apply` is the builtin it calls
  the callee with the frame's actual arguments; otherwise it makes the object and takes
  today's path. The template reaches it through the intrinsic helper, with no new helper
  slot. Every exemplar removes the object in its optimizing tier only (V8 above; JSC's
  DFGVarargsForwardingPhase.cpp:46-389; SpiderMonkey's ScalarReplacement.cpp:1900-2406), and
  their first tiers make it; this is a departure, on the owner's word that every tier's cost
  counts, and it hands the tier the forwarded call those passes build for themselves. Not
  built: SpiderMonkey's baseline form (the object kept, read without the generic apply,
  CacheIR.cpp:13009-13016), which has no counted reach beyond the same functions.
  My conditions, each to be answered and tested: `w.arguments` read by reflection gives a
  fresh object of the actual arguments, as V8 builds one on every read (builtins/
  accessors.cc:429-461), never null where today it gives an object, and the form never reads
  one back; which root holds the frame's argument words until the late read, for every kind
  of caller, under GC stress; every other way to `arguments` or a parameter (eval, `with`, an
  arrow function, a parameter named `arguments`) declined or shown safe; the site's cache
  records the forwarded callee so that the tier's plan applies to it, written into the
  feedback schema; what the tier does with these functions today and after (it declines the
  new operation until step 9), measured; which construct path Box2D's two forms take
  afterwards, where the report and the note disagree; part A2's recognizer against the
  rewrite; x86-64's template compiler handles the operation; Error.stack, `caller` and the
  call depth as today's. Code follows A1.

Steps 6 and 7, part A1 landed (1aa09634, 2026-11-18; target/p3/stage3/step67-impl,
report-a1-impl.txt, design-note-a1.txt and a1-impl/, the agents'; my checks stepa1-review and
merge-check's counts-pa1.out, counts-pa1-graph.out and cycles-pa1.out): a constructor whose
chunk has no static instance layout takes a learned one, and the direct construct sequence then
takes its constructions.
- What it changes. The static pass predicts a layout only from a constructor's straight-line
  `this.<name> = ...` prefix. A constructor with a store behind a branch, fields a called
  super constructor sets, or fields its maker adds after `new` had none, and every one of its
  constructions went through Op::New's helper: 1,302.65 instructions with the tier off against
  558.54 for a constructor with a static layout. The exemplars make a constructor's template
  from its first constructions and not from its body (V8's initial map and slack tracking,
  js-function.cc:939-975, map.h:443-473; JSC's allocation profile,
  ObjectAllocationProfileInlines.h:67-110; SpiderMonkey's this-shape, PlainObject.cpp:40-62).
  Here the construction that ends a chunk's construct learning (the eighth) measures the
  previous instance, and the chunk takes that instance's layout if it is one of the largest
  measured and learnable: default-attribute String-keyed data properties, no indexed, Symbol,
  private or far storage, and no key index of its own (which an instance builds past sixteen
  keys). Otherwise it settles without keys. A settled chunk's entries get the direct
  sequence's gate; an entry filled while the chunk was learning is refreshed by its next
  construction through the helper. It is not the tier's: the default configuration and the
  tier off change too, as designed.
- A layout is a prediction only. An instance allocated with it has no property until a store
  makes one, and a store the layout does not predict detaches it, so property order and every
  result are the stores' own; the tests compare them at every tier for wrong, missing and
  extra keys, a throw, a returned object, a constructor that constructs itself, accessors,
  frozen and deleted properties, another prototype, closures of one literal, and a base class
  reached by `new` and by `super`. No layout is kept between constructions and no Chunk field
  is added: the learned layout lives in the chunk's once-set cell (renamed learned_layout),
  whose three setters own disjoint kinds of chunk, which a debug assertion states.
- Measured, fat LTO against the main before (mine; the agent's agree to 0.03 points in
  instructions). A construction without a static layout 1,302.65 -> 567.54 instructions with
  the tier off and 1,177.60 -> 446.39 with it on (the agent's rows); Box2D's two forwarding
  forms 3,815 -> 3,094 and 4,970 -> 4,248. Instructions at 5 runs, the tier off / the default
  / graph: Box2D -1.91% / -3.01% / -3.09%, DeltaBlue -0.59% / -0.71% / -0.81%, Djot's fixture
  -0.11% / -0.24% / -0.37% and its steady state -0.30% / -0.39% / -0.32%; every other row
  under 0.07% or inside its spread but Earley-Boyer, below. At 200 runs on one X925 core
  alone, five alternating rounds (the requested clock 1.4 to 2.8 GHz, the heat guard not
  tripped): DeltaBlue -1.16% / -2.03% / -2.49% instructions and -1.63% / -2.02% / -3.53%
  cycles; Box2D -2.24% / -4.55% / -4.63% instructions and -2.69% / -6.61% / -7.07% cycles
  (the agent's cycles on another core: -1.46% / -2.34% / -3.88% and -2.74% / -6.69% / -7.21%);
  Richards, a control, level. Box2D's cycles fall more than its instructions: the helper's
  path that A1 removes ran at about 3.5 instructions a cycle where the program runs at 5.5
  (the agent's arithmetic; what made it slow was not profiled).
- Earley-Boyer with the tier off reads +0.28% at 5 runs (+0.16% at graph, level at the
  default). It is the incremental marker's schedule and not A1's work: with
  TRUST_JS_GC_INCREMENTAL=0 the same two binaries differ by +0.02% (0.6M instructions, about
  1.7 a helper construction: the refresh's test). Runs of one binary agree to 0.001% either
  way, so it is a difference of schedule between the binaries and not noise; the method's
  note on the collecting benchmarks covers it.
- Memory (the agent's, anonymous and file-backed apart, fresh copies of both binaries): the
  file-backed part equal on every row; anonymous memory within 164 KiB of the base on Box2D
  (of about 50 MB) and +128 KiB on DeltaBlue at 5 and 200 runs; TypeScript at 5 runs -6.7 MB
  (-1.2%) with the tier off and -1.9 MB at graph; Box2D's live bytes after each of 19 major
  collections at equal allocation counts unchanged.
- Template code. Identical at the default in 3,582 of 3,582 compiles. With the tier off
  TypeScript's differs in 24 of 942: one compile at another place in the order and 23 of equal
  length that differ only in embedded words, 442 of them 32-bit words each exactly 9 lower
  (read as shape numbers in the caches' guards: nine fewer transition shapes exist when these
  functions compile) and five loads whose offset is one slot lower. No inlining decision
  differs and no layout or capacity is embedded. The reading of the words is the agent's and
  not verified; the five loads are not explained.
- Tests and checks: five tests (the direct sequence after the learning ends; the prediction
  whatever was measured; the end of the learning exits no compiled function; one setter for
  each kind of chunk; the bound, sixteen keys learnt and seventeen not), under GC stress too;
  seven mutations caught; the tier's modules with the tier off, on, eager and with forced

  zombie's check, clippy, Windows' check and suite (2,902). Mine: the 5-run verifier sweep 42
  of 42 at both modes on the landed binary; clippy with tests clean and the 12 construct tests

Stage 3 lowerings landed: shape-test fusion, compare-and-branch fusion, folded Object offsets
(14a0bae0, d922672f, 6a49d05d, 2026-11-18; target/p3/stage3/final-builds, report.txt, the
agents'; my checks steplow-review and merge-check's counts-low.out, counts-low-graph.out and
cycles-low.out). Three changes to the tier's ARM64 emission (opt/a64.rs), no change to the
graph, the builder or the first stage; the stack was built over c2b91010 and is landed rebased
over piece D, the apply pin and part A1, with which it shares no file.
- Shape-test fusion (14a0bae0). A ShapeIs whose one use is the Branch right after it in its
  block sets the flags the Branch takes: `cmp`, then a conditional compare a shape more, then
  `b.cond`; the Boolean and its test (`mov #0` / `mov #1`, `cbz`) are not emitted. Two
  instructions fewer where the shape matched, three where not. As Maglev's polymorphic arms
  branch on the map compare itself (BranchIfReferenceEqual in BuildCompareMaps,
  maglev-graph-builder.cc:3750-3804). It changes 140 of 1,522 tier compiles.
- Compare-and-branch fusion (d922672f). The same for an I32Cmp, F64Cmp, TaggedSame,
  IsTypedArray or CalleeMatches whose one use is the Branch after it: `cmp, cset, cbz` becomes
  `cmp, b.cond`; a BoolNot between them emits nothing and inverts the condition; a constant's
  Branch emits nothing. A Number compare keeps the conditions under which an unordered result
  is false (MI and LS for less and less-or-equal) and its negation true. It changes 714 of
  1,522 compiles. A fused CalleeMatches saves two on a match and not the note's three (its
  last compare's branch stays; removing it is worth 0.013% of TypeScript and was left).
- Folded Object offsets (6a49d05d). The Object's offset in its cell is folded into the address
  of a field, shape, flag or kind load: `add x17, x4, #8; ldr x4, [x17, #64]` becomes `ldr x4,
  [x4, #72]`, one instruction fewer at each, two at CheckProto and LoadArrayLength. It changes
  1,404 of 1,522 compiles.

  -1.25% and -1.41% / -1.25% and -1.53%.
