Steps 6 and 7, the apply form landed (ddeed4f6, 2026-11-18; target/p3/stage3/step67-impl,
report-apply-impl.txt, apply/facts.txt and apply/, the agents'; my checks stepapply-review and
merge-check's counts-apf.out, counts-apf-graph.out and cycles-apf.out): `<f>.apply(<t>,
arguments)` in a body whose other uses of `arguments` are only `arguments.length` and
`arguments[k]` calls `f` with the frame's actual arguments and makes no arguments object.
- The rule and the operation. The compiler's lazy-arguments rewrite takes a load of the
  arguments slot that is the last argument of a two-argument method call whose method a
  GetMethod of "apply" pushed, in one straight run of operations, and makes the call
  Op::CallApplyFrameArgs. While `apply` is %Function.prototype.apply% (by its native's address)
  and `f` is callable, the operation calls `f` with `<t>` and the actual arguments: apply's
  own steps on the object the body would have made (ECMA-262 20.2.3.1; CreateListFromArrayLike
  reads each index at the call). Anything else (a replaced, own or getter `apply`, a
  non-callable `f`) makes the exact object and calls `apply` with it, as before. The call's
  site records `f`, the forwarded callee.
- What the facts changed in the design (apply/facts.txt, each shown by a run or marked read).
  Legacy reflection needs nothing: every compiled frame answers `w.arguments` with null today,
  so no object is handed out to read back (the defect that is, the owner's question 11 in
  the handoff note). A sloppy body with simple formals takes the second pass's form, where
  formals live in the activation: the hit reads each mapped index from its formal's binding at
  the call, which needs no condition on writes (V8's and JSC's passes have one,
  js-call-reducer.cc:4891-4900), and a replaced `apply` receives the exact mapped object,
  which formals in frame slots could not alias. The optimizing tier has no refusal for an
  operation it does not know: it runs this one as its template's canonical-frame effect, as
  it runs the other arguments operations, and keeps compiling the 26 of Box2D's 34 such
  functions it compiled before; no line of opt/ changed. Part A2's recognizer reads the
  rewritten forwarder. No splice is planned at such a site and it seeds no second stage: a
  reader must take the site's way as the forwarded callee and never as the call's callee
  operand (feedback-schema.txt 5.10), which the first-stage inline planner had got wrong
  (found by the agent's sweep: three of A2's tests and one of A1's on x86-64 failed).
- Measured on the landed commit against the main before, fat LTO (mine; the agent's on its
  own base agree). Box2D at 200 runs on one X925 core alone, five alternating rounds (the
  requested clock 1.4 to 2.8 GHz, the heat guard not tripped), the tier off / the default /
  graph: instructions -4.64% / -10.14% / -10.38%, cycles -4.49% / -12.72% / -12.81%. At 5
  runs Box2D -3.97% / -6.56% / -6.72%, RayTrace -0.08% / -0.12% / -0.15% (its forwarder's
  first constructions make no object now), every other row level or inside its spread.
  RayTrace at 200 runs level in instructions (+0.02%, +0.03%). A call (the agent's rows,
  instructions, the tier off): through `target.apply(this, arguments)` without formals 3,222
  -> 1,711 and with formals 5,894 -> 2,237; Box2D's two constructor forms 3,094 -> 1,579 and
  4,248 -> 2,673; `target.call` (1,610) and an Array's apply (1,968) equal to the base to the
  hundredth, after a constant +2.00 on both was traced by disassembly to an arm on a shared
  dispatch path and removed.
- One control row that is the binary's and not the form's: DeltaBlue with the tier off, which
  runs no such call and whose template code is identical, reads +0.46% in cycles at equal
  instructions, and +0.29% (standard error 0.05%) by the means of 8 runs a side at randomized
  addresses with instructions equal to a part in a million. Randomized addresses move the
  stack and the heap, not the placement of the Rust code within the binary, which a
  341-line change to bytecode.rs shifts. The apply pin's open row on RayTrace is the same
  kind. Noted for the question of a link order file (the owner's question 6).
- Tests and checks. The agent's: twelve tests (every program at the tree-walker, the VM and
  the JIT from the first call and at the default thresholds, compared with the tree-walker:
  actual, extra and missing arguments; bound, Proxy, native, class, throwing and non-callable
  callees; a replaced, own and getter `apply`; mapped bodies with written and captured
  formals, a replaced apply writing arguments[0], duplicate formals; every other way to
  `arguments`; collections between entry and the call with the words only the callers hold;
  recursion and Error.stack; the tier compiling the form at both inline modes, eager and with
  forced exits); twelve of thirteen mutations caught, the thirteenth changing no result;
  GC stress over the tests; zombie's check, clippy, Windows' check and suite (2,914); the
  verifier clean. Mine on the landed tree: the 5-run verifier sweep 42 of 42 at both modes;
  clippy with tests; the form's tests with the call-feedback, constructor-transfer and
  construct tests, 44 passed with the tier off, on, eager, with forced exits, and eager and
  with forced exits at graph; the tier's modules 664 passed with forced exits at the default,
  and 663 with one failed with forced exits at graph, below.
- The one failure is the intermittent test A1's record names, for the second time:
  opt::offthread_tests::a_loop_limit_discard_drops_a_reoptimization_pending_meanwhile
  ("nothing installed", offthread_tests.rs:521), in a parallel run of the modules under a
  machine load of 17. Alone it passes 25 of 25 at that configuration on this commit and 25 of
  25 on the main before, alternating, under the same load. Both sightings were at graph in
  full parallel runs, on trees that hold A1; the runs of trees before A1 did not show it,
  which is too few runs to mean anything. It asserts that a pending reoptimization is
  dropped by a loop-limit discard: either the test assumes a timing, or a compile can be
  installed after its discard. The tier's line is to find out which.
- Open with this commit. Template code reaches the operation through the generic helper,
  which leaves a call about 100 instructions above `target.call` and lets the compiled tiers
  reach the stack's RangeError 12% earlier in a recursion through such a call (the same text,
  Error.stack and `caller`; the VM reaches it 10% later): the intrinsic route, the next
  commit, takes both back. Box2D's peak anonymous memory at graph at 200 runs is 52.9 MB in 7
  of 9 runs against the base's 50.5 MB in 9 of 9, and lower with the tier off: not
  attributed. A strict body's `return f.apply(this, arguments)` compiles to a tail call,
  which the rule does not take: to be counted. The later stages of the agent's own sweep
  (the tier's modules in seven configurations, the whole suite with the tier off, on and
  under each switch) were running when it stopped and are read by its successor.

Stage 3 step 3g landed: the Splay retention failure at graph, and the Rust entries' stale
words (0d94f320, d2d6e985, 73282547, 6de9dc2c, 2026-11-18; target/p3/stage3/step3g-impl,
report-splay.txt and report-entries.txt, the agents'; my checks stepsplay-review). It closes
the defect the flip's rehearsal found ("Found by the flip's rehearsal", above).
- The cause was not graph's and not the tier's. A loop the tree-walker moves into the VM and
  the VM then moves into template code runs on a borrowed VM frame, whose operand buffer comes
  from the interpreter's pool. The pool clears a buffer by its length only, and the entry
  registered the buffer's whole capacity with the collector, so words an earlier user of the
  buffer left were roots for the frame's whole native run. In Splay's test at graph that
  buffer had capacity 24 and its words 9 to 12 named removed nodes (an earlier user had grown
  it; which one was not traced); at the default and with the tier off it had capacity 12 and
  its stale words named long-lived objects, which is why those passed with the same defect
  present. Found by marking every root word alone: one word of that range kept 65, 737, 1,809
  and 3,281 cells over four samples.
- The fix (0d94f320), two parts. A Rust entry's operand words are scanned up to its
  activation's live top where the collector knows it, as an active record's are since stage
  2: the top a direct call left in its callee's record, or the top its poll passed; an
  inlined frame's slot window above that top is scanned apart, as a record's is; elsewhere
  the whole range, as before. And a borrowed frame zeroes its buffer past the entry depth at
  its first transfer, as JSC sanitizes its stack, which removes an earlier user's words in
  every wait and on x86-64, where the collector never knows the top.
- The Rust entries' stale words (6de9dc2c), the same defect in the entries that run compiled
  code on a pooled buffer of their own (jit::run, which serves Reflect.apply, natives'
  callbacks such as sort's comparator and replace's replacer with the tier off, and a
  script's own entry; and the entry for frames too large for a record): nearly every such
  entry held an earlier user's words inside the words it registers (Splay about 500 of 507;
  every one of 119,993 in a callback script). They zero those words where they take the
  buffer, in line up to 16 words: 15 to 27 instructions an entry, +0.28% on the callback
  script and +0.006% on Mandreel with the tier off, level on x86-64 (DeltaBlue +0.005%), no
  cycle difference beyond the runs' own deviation. The design that would make both clears
  unnecessary is to publish the run's operand top on every call into Rust; not built.
- What review changed. The first form scanned an entry to its top and nothing else. My
  question whether anything live can lie above that top found that it can: the optimizing
  tier's code runs on a Rust entry when a function is called through Reflect.apply, and an
  inlined frame's generic node keeps its slot window above the top its end poll passes
  (1,240 such traces in a diagnostic run); the form as first written would have let the
  collector miss it. The window is scanned now and a test holds it. The per-entry cost was
  an allocation and a hash set per entry per collection, and is one pass per trace now (a
  2,000-deep recursion through a native callback: +3.42% instructions in the first form,
  +1.17% in the landed one).
- Measured (the agent's, fat LTO against the commit before). Splay at graph -1.80%
  instructions at 5 runs and -0.63% at 200, level at the default and with the tier off; graph
  against the default is -1.30% and -0.47% where the rehearsal read +0.46% and +0.19%, so
  Splay's rows above zero there were this retention. Anonymous memory within 8 KiB at every
  mode but Splay at 200 runs with the tier off, 2.8 MB lower (-1.03%).
- Tests and checks. Ten tests of the fix (a pooled word's object is collected while the
  frame waits on a direct call, on a native that collects, and at its polls; the top operand
  word and the slots are roots; the window on a Rust entry) and three of the pooled entries'
  clear, at each tier an entry can run in; nine mutations of the fix, of which eight are
  caught by assertions (one of them the heap verifier's) and one, which can only over-scan,
  is accepted uncaught; the clears' mutations caught. GC
  stress over the borrowed-frame, rooting and collector tests with the tier off, on and at
  graph: 45 passed and none failed at each, without two tests (below). The whole library
  suite at graph, which the Splay test had failed since step 3e: 3,618 passed and none
  failed on the four commits' tree. difftest 138 agree at the three modes. zombie's check,
  clippy, Windows' check and suite (2,911, the stale-word tests' legs run there). The tier's
  modules with forced exits under GC stress were still running when this was written. Mine
  on main, where the four commits meet the apply form and the lowerings: clippy with tests
  clean, and the collector's, borrowed-frame, rooting, apply-form and inlining tests, 150
  passed and none failed with the tier off, on, at graph, and at graph eager with forced
  exits; the Splay retention test passes in each.
- The graph counterparts of the four splice-pinned tests that the rehearsal found missing
  (d2d6e985, tests only): each passes with the environment at any mode and under GC stress
  at graph.
- Found on the way, each recorded and none fixed here. The Splay retention test fails under
  TRUST_JS_GC_STRESS in its VM leg, on the commit before the fix as on it, number for number:
  one native-stack word in the tree-walker's loop-step frame (eval.rs:1280) keeps removed
  nodes while collections run at nearly every safepoint; an ordinary run holds at most one
  such word from its last due collection until the loop ends. The test's comment says it is
  not a test for stress (73282547). A deep-recursion test under a collection at every
  allocation is quadratic and is left out of the stress sets (it passes at a sparser
  interval). jit::proxy_trap_tests::reflect_calls_agree_across_tiers counts hits against a
  threshold while the call-cache epoch is process-wide, so a parallel run can fail it (seen
  once on x86-64, 246 of a needed 250). A crashed child of the rooting stress test reads as
  "different test inventory", because that assertion runs before the exit status's.

The intermittent off-thread test, explained and closed (7e974625, tests only, 2026-11-18;
target/p3/stage3/step3g-impl/report-offthread.txt and otd/). opt::offthread_tests::
a_loop_limit_discard_drops_a_reoptimization_pending_meanwhile had failed twice in parallel
runs of the tier's modules at graph ("nothing installed") and never in 320 runs alone. With
an assertion at the hold's release that names what is pending, it failed 6 times in 1,000
alone at graph, each time with two compiles pending: the reoptimization the test is about, and
the first optimizing compile of its helper function `noop`, queued by the calls the fallback
made under the hold. The poll after the hold installed that second compile when the compile
thread finished inside the test's last loop. Whether `noop` had reached its compile before
depended on when f's own code, which inlines it, was installed: a timing the test assumed. It
was the test's and not an install after a discard. The test now warms `noop` to its tier
code before the clear, keeps the assertion at the release, and names the chunks in both
messages; 0 failures in 1,000 runs alone at graph and 200 at each mode.

Steps 6 and 7, the apply form's route landed (8bfb6fab, 2026-11-18; target/p3/stage3/
step67-impl, report-apply-route.txt and route/, the agent's; my checks steproute-review and
merge-check's counts-rt.out, counts-rt-graph.out and cycles-rt.out): template code reaches
Op::CallApplyFrameArgs through the intrinsic helper, as the approved note had it, where the
first commit used the generic one.
- What it is. A new intrinsic code; an arm in ARM64's and x86-64's template compilers that
  calls the intrinsic helper directly with no callee probe (the site's way is the forwarded
  callee, so a probe could not class `apply`; the helper tests it); one arm in the helper's
  dispatch. No helper slot, nothing in opt/: the tier's canonical-frame effect shares the
  arm. The operation's semantics, the VM's arm and every test of the first commit stay.
- The others' rows are untouched, which took two builds. The first form added 5.0
  instructions to every intrinsic that takes the helper's general path (`target.call`, an
  Array's apply): its inner closure captured two more values, so it saved a register and
  loaded a pair more on every use. The landed form reads the code, the count and the pc from
  the packed operand inside the closure and captures no more words than before. On my
  binaries, the main before -> the route, instructions a call: `target.call` 1,610.36 ->
  1,610.36 with the tier off and 1,456.64 -> 1,456.64 with it on; an Array's apply 1,968.17
  -> 1,968.17 and 1,826.42 -> 1,826.42; the forwarder's construction, the loop and a direct
  call equal.
- What it gains (mine). A forwarded call without formals 1,711.02 -> 1,634.66 instructions
  with the tier off and 1,601.99 -> 1,525.63 with it on (24 above `target.call` with the tier
  off, 69 at the tier's modes, where the tier takes more off a call it sees through than off
  this effect); with formals 2,236.79 -> 2,165.32. Box2D at 200 runs on one X925 core alone,
  five alternating rounds, the tier off / the default / graph: instructions -0.40% / -0.87%
  / -0.89%, cycles -0.55% / -0.08% / -0.19%; at 5 runs -0.33% / -0.56% / -0.57%. With the
  first commit the form is worth -5.0% / -10.9% / -11.2% of Box2D's instructions at 200 runs.
- The recursion depth. A recursion through such a call reaches the stack's RangeError at
  320,636 levels in template code where the main before the apply form reached 191,236 and
  the first commit 168,380: two thirds deeper than before the form, not the 12% shallower
  the first commit left. It is the engine's own exhaustion check that raises it, from the
  intrinsic helper, with all 64 execution-stack segments taken, and never a guard page; the
  text, Error.stack and `caller` are the tree-walker's at every tier (the agent's probe and
  tests, also in a build with debug assertions).
- Tests and checks. The agent's: four new tests (a counter that fails if a template takes the
  generic route; a site past the operand's 16-bit pc; the call's level against the
  tree-walker; the end poll), eight mutations of the route caught, thirteen configurations
  and GC stress; zombie's check, clippy, Windows' check and suite, where the x86-64 arm
  runs. Mine on the landed tree: the call-cost rows above; the 5-run verifier sweep 42 of 42
  at both modes; clippy with tests; the form's tests with the call-feedback,
  constructor-transfer, construct and borrowed-frame tests, 71 passed in each of six
  configurations; the tier's modules 666 passed and none failed with forced exits at the
  default and at graph. The first commit's own sweep, read since its record: the tier's
  modules 559 passed in each of seven configurations, the whole library suite 3,618 passed
  with the tier off and with it on; the suite under each of the two switches was still
  running.
- Closed with it. Box2D's peak memory at graph (2 MB more in most runs) is not the
  collector's: the same number of major collections, equal live data after each, fewer minor
  collections, a lower page peak; the difference is one more 2 MB transparent huge page at
  the peak in the allocator's arena (the agent's figures). TypeScript's template code with
  the tier off compiles in one of two orders that depend on addresses; the main before
  shows both by itself.
- Open. A strict body's `return f.apply(this, arguments)` compiles to a tail call, which the
  rule does not take. It runs in no measured workload (counted: none in the fixtures and
  Octane at 5 and 200 runs); one such call costs 2,950 instructions against 1,226 for the
  same body with its result in a variable. No work now; JSC forwards the tail case too
  (TailCallForwardVarargs, DFGVarargsForwardingPhase.cpp:380-383), and module and class
  code is strict, so a workload that has it would ask for it.

A correction to step 3g's record: Splay's rows were the marker's schedule (2026-11-18;
target/p3/stage3/steproute-review/splay). That record gives, as the retention fix's measure,
Splay at graph -1.80% instructions at 5 runs and concludes that the rehearsal's Splay rows
above zero "were this retention". Both are wrong, and I had passed the first on to the
owner. My recount of the route showed Splay 1.64% and 1.91% lower at the default and at graph
for a commit that runs nothing in Splay, with the tier's log equal line for line. The
collector's log differs: the first major collection ends at 168,025 objects after 191
marking slices on one binary and at 184,155 after 224 on the other. With
TRUST_JS_GC_INCREMENTAL=0 the two binaries count 1,291,590,452 and 1,291,593,221. The same
recount of the fix against its base (the piece D line's own binaries): at graph
1,092,938,683 -> 1,073,230,628 with the marker, the -1.80% of the record, and 1,305,481,133
-> 1,305,478,271 without it; at the default equal both ways. So the fix changes no work in
Octane's Splay, whose loops do not run on the borrowed frame the fix is about; what it
changes is what its tests show, the objects a collection keeps in that case. And the
incremental marker's schedule is a property of the binary worth up to 1.9% of Splay with
the tier on: the rehearsal's +0.46% and +0.19% at graph against the default say nothing
about graph. The method's note on the collecting benchmarks now carries the numbers and
the rule that such a row is charged to a change only when the recount without the marker
shows it too. Why the schedule differs between binaries at equal work was not looked into.

- The forwarder's construction, instructions (the tier off / the tier on, both modes alike):
  882.87 / 738.73 with the forward form; 1,503.00 / 1,288.88 with the apply form alone;
  3,092.36 / 2,878.11 with neither. The apply form halves what the general path cost, and

The lowerings' next piece, counted before anything is built (2026-11-18; target/p3/stage3/
select-impl, report.txt, table.txt and table-detail.txt, the lowerings' agent's; a census engine
over 218ee9e7 that counts every shape test by the position that matched, every shape check, the
record-pointer loads and the named sites at different own slots, each by whether a check of the
same word dominates it; every Octane benchmark at 5 and 200 runs, the fixtures and their
steady-state copies, at both inline modes; the cost of today's paths read from micros'

workload's instructions at graph at 200 runs.
- The arm that tests two shapes (the fusion's leftover): only TypeScript runs such arms, and
  they match their second shape 25.8M times against 2.3M for the first, so Maglev's compare and
  branch a shape would cost it 0.002%. Not built.
- The select form (one check of the shape set, the offset selected by the shape, one access):
  Splay -0.19%, GBEMU -0.15%, RayTrace -0.10%, DeltaBlue -0.06%, nothing elsewhere. Above
  those workloads' spreads, under 0.2% everywhere. Decided after the next item is measured.
- What the count found instead, and what is built first: a shape check takes the tagged word,
  so after every call the second access to the same object tests the word's tag and extracts
  its cell again, though a value's tag cannot change. Predicted for a check that takes the
  known cell (Maglev's CheckMaps omits its Smi test for a known heap object,
  maglev-reducer.h:785-790, maglev-ir.cc:2631): Richards -1.67%, GBEMU -0.82%, Box2D -0.78%,
  Crypto -0.58%, DeltaBlue -0.46%, TypeScript -0.42%, RayTrace -0.35%, Earley-Boyer -0.31%.
  A design note, then the code; its measured rows against these are also the test of the
  count's cost model.
- Two more, counted and not yet understood: the record pointer loaded again where one load of
  the same object's dominates (Mandreel -1.37%, all of it the global object's at global reads,
  which overlaps the global-read piece below; Richards -0.53%, GBEMU -0.42%), and shape, kind
  and flag loads repeated inside one stretch without effects (Richards -1.17%, Crypto -0.96%,
  DeltaBlue -0.86%). Why the tier's facts do not already cover them is looked into before
  anything is built for either.
- The kind test beside the tag test would about double the first item's gain, but needs an
  object's kind to be immutable once JavaScript can see it, which value.rs does not promise
  today. Left out.
- For the global-read piece (Mandreel's heap views; design step "F4"), a first finding of the
  same agent: of Mandreel's 6,456M reads of global-object properties in the tier's code at 200
  runs, 4,030M sit in loops with no effect at all and still run their whole chain of checks
  and loads every iteration (122 instructions an iteration for three reads in a micro); 1,806M
  sit in loops with a call. The first kind needs no dependency and no owner decision; the
  second is owner question 7. The count, a ceiling from a diagnostic build, and the design
  note follow.

Stage 3 step 3g, piece B landed (5dd88e0d, with a test of its own ed4ef848; 2026-11-19;
target/p3/stage3/step3g-impl, report-b.txt and report-b3.txt, b-note.txt and b-points.txt, the
agents'; the coordinator's review step3g-impl/review-b-coordinator.txt and checks
target/p3/stage3/stepb-review). A closure that the compiled function creates and passes to an
inlined callee that calls it is a known callee there: under TRUST_JS_OPT_INLINE=graph the call
is built as an inlined frame with no guard ("by literal"), as Maglev reduces a call whose
target is a creation node (TryReduceCallForNewClosure, maglev-graph-builder.cc:11963-11980).
React's reducer passes fresh arrows to the self-hosted map and filter, whose calls of them
were generic calls (4.12M of them at 163 instructions in the steady-state copy).
- The form. The builder puts a fact on the node that created the closure (its function
  literal and the environment it captured); the fact reaches an inlined callee's formal bound
  to that node and nothing else, and a call there whose callee node carries it is that
  literal's frame, taken before any identity way planned at the call. The frame's free names
  walk from the creation's environment; its generic operations read the environment from the
  closure. The closure is a fresh object each time, so the stack and Function#caller find it
  through a per-interpreter table with one word per function frame, which the frame stores at
  its entry and every way out clears (its returns, an exit, a throw out of the code). The
  table is the way to find the closure and never what keeps it alive: the allocator gives
  the frame's callee a home across every node of its frame, as JSC keeps a closure call's
  callee recoverable in the machine frame and Maglev in each call's frame state.
- Measured (fat LTO against main's engine, the coordinator's builds target/p2/lto/e3099886 and
  661a2ba5; counts-b3*.out, cycles-b3.out). Instructions at graph: React's steady state
  2,210,289,458 -> 1,347,248,398 (-39.05%), the fixture 263,512,005 -> 211,795,546 (-19.63%);
  at the splice both equal (+0.00%), and graph against the splice on the new engine -38.87%
  and -19.59%. Cycles on one X925 core, five rounds at randomized addresses: the steady state
  -42.1% at graph, the fixture -17.9%, the splice and two controls (DeltaBlue at 200, zlib)
  flat. Every other workload at graph within 0.1% but TypeScript (+0.85%, and +1.04% with the
  incremental marker off), which is not this change: see the next record. With the tier off
  and at the splice the rows are equal to a few hundredths of a per cent but RegExp (-0.11%
  to -0.12% in all three modes) and PdfJS with the tier off (-0.21%), not attributed; the
  agent's masked dumps have the templates' and the tier's code identical there (2,174 and
  1,373 compiles at the splice, 2,589 with the tier off). The verifier sweep, 5 runs at both
  modes: 42 of 42.
- Reach. Of 21 workloads only React has such sites, and three of its five arrows take the
  form; the other two are in call sites the planner skips for its optimized-body budget.
  Counted, not built: admitting them (the budget doubled) gives the steady state -0.69% and
  costs the fixture +0.35%, and the same budget grows the other workloads' tier code by 1% to
  56%. The budget here charges the caller's own size; Maglev's does not
  (maglev-inlining.cc:100-118). A planner question for every workload, not this line's next
  piece.
- Cost. An activation of a code that has such a frame computes its table index once (12
  instructions); a frame costs about 10 (the test, the store, a clear at each return); a
  throw out of such a code 15 more on that path; the table is 32 KB an interpreter that
  compiled one. Counted from the emitter.
- What the review found in the first form (c7188012), all fixed before landing. (1) A
  miscompile: a named function expression whose name can be observed has a record of its own
  between the closure and the creation's environment, so every free name in its frame was
  read one record short ("me is not a function", and wrong values silently). The frame now
  reads the name as its callee node and walks the others from one record further out, as the
  three engines take a function's own name from the callee; test builds trap at the frame's
  entry where the closure's environment is not what the compile assumed. (2) No rule kept the
  closure alive while the frame ran a helper that collects and then reads the stack; what
  covered it was a read on the fallback path that happens to follow the frame in code order.
  The allocator's rule above replaces the accident. (3) A throw with no handler left the
  table entry set; the throw path clears it now, the clear at the activation's entry is gone,
  and debug builds assert at every collection that an entry is set only while its frame
  runs. The coordinator's guess at what had held the closure (a stale operand word) was wrong.
- Tests: opt::literal_tests (22), each rule with its mutation (the agents' tables in
  report-b3.txt: 20 caught, three equivalent and said why); the coordinator's adversarial
  programs (stepb-review/adv: recursion through the frame, throws, a reassigned and an aliased
  formal, parameter scopes, `this`, generators, async and class literals, array methods with
  holes, named function expressions, script, eval and generator bodies) equal the tier off at
  both modes and under GC stress; the by-literal tests with their neighbours in six
  configurations (164 each) and under stress; the tier's modules in six (686 each, the
  agent's run); the other architecture's check, clippy and suite (2,930). The whole library
  suite with the tier off and on runs after this landing as its confirmation, and at graph
  in the agent's run; a failure takes the two commits back off local main.
- Open. The profile note of graph against the splice (Crypto +0.66% and GBEMU +0.22%
  instructions at 200 runs, Mandreel's cycles) is not written. Splay at graph against the
  splice with the marker off read +0.50% at 5 runs on the first form (same compiles, exits
  and discards): not attributed. Both belong to the flip's table, which is taken again now.

TypeScript's two modes (2026-11-19; target/p3/stage3/stepb-review/ts and
target/p3/stage3/tsmode-impl, the agent's; benchmarks/README.md has the method that follows from
it). Found by the row above: Octane's TypeScript at 5 runs sits at one of two levels about
1.1% of its instructions apart, with the incremental marker off as well, and which one a run
draws depends on the binary and the address layout (main's engine the lower in 10 of 10
randomized runs, piece B's in 2 of 10, an engine of three days earlier the higher in 8 of 8).
- Cause. Each run of the benchmark builds a compiler. In the lower mode something the
  conservative scan finds keeps the previous run's alive, the heap looks larger, and the
  collector runs less often (8 major collections against 12); in the higher mode that garbage
  is collected. The root was not found in the time given: the retained closure is a property
  of an object that 820 others have as their prototype, reached from a cell the agent's walk
  could not decode (tsmode-impl has the neutral tracing driver and the walk).
- Its shadow in the tier, which is how it was seen: a reoptimization takes its earlier ways
  again "each while its callee lives", through a weak reference, so one nested way of
  TypeFlow.typeCheckName is prepared or not with the same draw. The plan is worth at most
  0.1% of the difference; the rest is the collector's work.
- The method: compare the mutator's instructions from a sampled profile (every sample whose
  call chain passes through a collection is the collector's). Two builds of equal generated
  code that drew opposite modes, 1.05% apart in total, agree to 0.02% on it.
- What the exemplars do at such a site, and what is being counted: all three inline a
  function's code behind a check of the code, not of the one closure, so a dead or replaced
  closure changes nothing (V8's feedback cell target, maglev-graph-builder.cc:11447-11520;
  JSC's closure call by executable, CallLinkStatus.cpp:247-263; SpiderMonkey's
  GuardFunctionScript, CacheIR.cpp:6607-6623). This engine guards the identity only, and its
  planner declines a site that met several closures of one function. Piece B is the special
  case where the closure is made in the compiled function. How many calls sit at such sites
  in each workload is being counted; it decides whether a code-guarded way is the inliner's
  next piece. A smaller rule (the reoptimization declining such a way whether its callee
  lives or not) waits on that count. A few other sites meet a dead callee the same way
  (GBEMU 1, PdfJS 3, TypeScript 10), and the planner drops a whole site for one dead callee
  (bytecode.rs:11375): recorded, not changed.
- Counted since (the same agent; tsmode-impl/report.txt sections 6 and 7, creach-join.txt). The
  code-guarded way has little reach on these workloads in the one-function form V8 has: 0.02%
  to 0.08% of the Octane benchmarks at 200 runs (Earley-Boyer, PdfJS, Box2D; nine have no such
  site), Vue's steady state 0.17%, Djot's 0.87%, by calls at 150 instructions each, not
  measured a site. React's such calls are piece B's. GBEMU's 0.75% is one dispatch site that
  calls five functions, the largest over the inliner's size limit, and would need polymorphic
  closure ways. Recorded, not built; Djot or the polymorphic form would reopen it, and it
  would reuse piece B's frame with the environment loaded from the callee and a guard of the
  code. The smaller rule is approved and written up to be built (section 7: at a
  reoptimization a kept way is not taken where its site's cache holds another closure of its
  function or the site's code hint is set, whether its callee lives or not; a two-engine test;
  its measure is that the tier's masked logs no longer differ between randomized runs on
  TypeScript, GBEMU and PdfJS). Not built yet: nobody is on it. After it a dead way whose site
  met no other closure of its function still follows liveness, and so does the planner's
  whole-site skip.
- Piece B's confirmation after landing (2026-11-19): the whole library suite on the landed
  tree with the tier off 3,675 passed, with TRUST_JS_OPT=1 3,675, none failed
  (stepb-review/b-suites.log); at graph 3,674, none failed, and the tier's modules 686 in each
  of six configurations (the agent's run, step3g-impl/bsuite3.out, one test older). Under GC
  stress 18 of the 20 by-literal tests passed in a six-hour window and none failed; one of the
  two unfinished passed in the coordinator's stress run, the other
  (no_entry_outlives_its_by_literal_frame) has never finished in that mode.

The owner's decisions of 2026-11-19 (owner question 7, made decidable by
target/p3/stage3/f4-impl/design-note.txt, and the flip, on the table above).
- The tier may depend on a global property staying unchanged: a state per property that the
  runtime moves one way (constant until its first change, then never folded again), a list of
  the codes that folded it, and their invalidation when it changes, as V8, JSC and
  SpiderMonkey do. Ceiling beyond the loop hoist: -6.8 points of Mandreel, about -5% of
  Richards. The same mechanism is to serve stable shapes through calls later (75% of
  Richards', 97% of DeltaBlue's and 66% of RayTrace's repeated shape checks sit in loops that
  call).
- It may also depend on a typed array's buffer not being detached or resized, in V8's form
  (one engine-wide state), built after the first dependency is landed and measured: a ceiling
  of -4.5 points more of Mandreel and nothing elsewhere; taken out if Mandreel does not gain.
- Running code learns of an invalidation by a test after calls, where the tier already tests
  the global object's epoch (0.2 to 0.8 points of the gain given back); V8's return-address
  exit is decided later on numbers, on the same runtime side.
- The flip: graph becomes the default after the register allocator's rule B lands, on the
  table taken again on that main; GBEMU's and Splay's remaining instruction gaps are recorded
  with their causes and their two follow-on pieces, not waited for.

A defect in the default configuration, found and fixed (936676c1, 2026-11-19;
target/p3/stage3/f4p2-impl/refloc and l1/adv, the agent's; the coordinator's programs
target/p3/stage3/stepb-review/refloc). Found by the agent building the global dependencies
while it read every path that writes a global. A top-level assignment to a global (and one in
a function with a direct eval) is a Reference; the template JIT's in-line ResolveNameRef
refreshed that Reference's shape and slot from the name's resolution site but not the word
that locates the slot, on ARM64 and x86-64 alike. The site is shared by every assignment of
the name in the chunk, each with its own Reference, so after the global's slot moved (the
global deleted and added again, or an earlier property removed) one assignment's helper
refilled the site for the new slot and the other assignment then wrote through its stale
location: into another global's slot.
- The program: `a = 1; x = 2; b = 3; for (...) { x = i; if (i == 1000) { delete x; x = 0 }
  x = i + 0.5 }` printed b as 1999 at the default tier and 3 in the VM and the tree-walker.
  It is on every engine back to the phase-2 exit and not on October's: it came in with the
  caches' location word (d79b2a13), and it is on origin.
- The fix: the in-line refresh copies the location word beside the other two (two
  instructions a ResolveNameRef, both templates). Every other place that fills or copies a
  Reference or a name cache was read and carries all three words.
- Tests: seven programs at the three tiers against each other with the neighbour global
  intact, the read side among them; with the copy removed six fail on each architecture (the
  seventh is a control that takes no in-line path). The coordinator's three further programs,
  two of which fail on main's engine (one corrupts three globals), agree at every tier on the
  fixed engine, with the tier on and at graph as well.
- The measurements of the two added instructions (template code otherwise identical, the
  name-store micro, CodeLoad and the fixtures) were still being taken when it landed; they are
  added below when they are in.
- The fix's measurements, in since (f4p2-impl/refloc: identity-report.txt, measure.out,
  counts.out, counts-deferred.out, zombie-v1.out, zombie-v2.out; fat LTO, the tier off). Template
  code is identical but for the two instructions: 2,663 of 3,062 compiles by masked hash, and the
  rest are the workloads whose compiles differ run to run or by addresses alone, identical once
  normalized, but two of zlib's (its code runs under a direct eval, so its global assignments
  are References), which differ by exactly the inserted pairs. A script-body `g = i; h += 1`
  costs 615.0 -> 619.0 instructions an iteration; a function's name store, which is no
  Reference, 82.29 -> 82.29. CodeLoad at 5 runs +0.0003% (+0.005% with collections deferred),
  Vue's fixture -0.03% with every compile identical. The other architecture's whole suite:
  2,937 passed, none failed. Two of the coordinator's programs joined the tests (e3b256a5: two
  moves in one loop across three names; the read side in a function with a direct eval).

The repeated tag test landed (143d39aa, 990b6cbd; 2026-11-19; target/p3/stage3/ctag-impl,
report.txt and design-note.txt with its addenda, the agent's; the count it rests on,
select-impl/report.txt; the coordinator's checks target/p3/stage3/stepctag-review and
stack-review). A value that a check has proven an object's stays one: its tag cannot change,
whatever a call does. The builder keeps that as a bit of the value's forms, kept where every
predecessor of a join holds it, as Maglev's node type survives side effects and merges at
joins (maglev-known-node-aspects.h:247-252, 281-283) and JSC's abstract value keeps SpecCell
through a clobber; a shape check of such a value tests no tag and takes its cell again
(Maglev's CheckMaps with kOmitHeapObjectCheck, maglev-ir.cc:2631), and the inliner's callee
guard does not test a receiver so proven (second commit). The verifier proves on the graph
that every check so relieved has a proof on every path to it.
- Measured against main's engine (fat LTO; the coordinator's builds, stack-review; instructions
  at graph at 200 runs, the first commit | both): Richards -1.67% | -3.89%, DeltaBlue -0.45% |
  -2.71%, Box2D -0.73% | -0.97%, GBEMU -0.83% | -0.83%, Crypto -0.58% | -0.81%, RayTrace -0.35%
  | -0.49%, Earley-Boyer -0.38% with the marker off; the splice alike; the tier off equal.
  Cycles for both, five rounds at randomized addresses on a loaded machine: Richards -2.1%,
  DeltaBlue -1.4%, Box2D -0.6%, Crypto -0.3%, GBEMU -0.3%. Each row is the count's prediction
  to within a quarter, most within a twentieth: the count's cost model holds.
- Three forms of the fact were built and measured (the agent's table): a separate set (5-run
  compile cost +0.15% on Richards), the bit (+0.03%), and "the value has a cell form" (no
  cost, but it loses the joins whose arms check the value again: GBEMU -0.71% against -0.82%).
  The bit landed, as the exemplars' merge at a join. The checked cell is taken again from the
  word and not kept: keeping it is better only on GBEMU (-1.40%) and Crypto and worse on four
  others, because the kept value takes a register from something hotter.
- Tests: the fact's and the guards' with their 23 mutations (all caught; three needed new
  tests); the coordinator's programs (primitives reaching a read through a join; Number and
  String receivers through strict and sloppy methods; null and undefined) equal at every mode.
- Open: a per-site choice between keeping and retaking the cell is the allocator's (GBEMU
  0.6%, Crypto 0.09% at most); a phi of proven values (0.04% at most); the kind test beside
  the tag test, which would about double the gain, waits on an object's kind being immutable
  once JavaScript can see it.

F4 part 1 landed: a global read leaves a loop that runs no effect (62c19047 to 8edf64c4, eleven
commits; 2026-11-19; target/p3/stage3/f4-impl, design-note.txt sections 0 to 3 and
report-part1.txt, the agents'; the coordinator's programs target/p3/stage3/stepb-review/f4adv
and checks stack-review). The tier loaded a global-object property, checked its kind and loaded
a typed array's view and data again at every read, also inside a loop that runs nothing able
to change them: the loop rule of step 0c named locals and closure variables as loop invariants
and not globals. Mandreel's two hottest loops read four heap views that way (4,030M of its
6,456M global reads in the tier's code sit in loops with no effect). The read's own nodes are
now made once at the loop's entry edge and the header keeps the value, as Ion's and the DFG's
LICM move a global's load out of a loop that writes none (jit/LICM.cpp:154-172;
DFGLICMPhase.cpp:96-118, 388-413).
- Measured (fat LTO; the coordinator's builds over the tag test): Mandreel at 200 runs -14.13%
  instructions and -15.1% cycles at graph, the splice alike; at 5 runs -0.95%; Crypto -0.18%
  instructions and +0.10% cycles (its hot function's later loops sit 48 bytes further on;
  measured, the cause inferred); nothing else beyond its spread; a micro's loop over three
  global typed arrays 122 -> 26 instructions an iteration.
- A MISCOMPILE in its first form, found by the coordinator's programs and by nothing of the
  piece's own (22 tests, the sweep at graph): a function inlined into the loop that stores the
  global (through an alias of the global object, by name, a script-level lexical) was not
  among the loop's stores, because the loop's analysis runs on bytecode before the graph is
  built and listed the compiled function's own operations only; the header then kept the
  entry's value. Every test of the piece had stored in the compiled function itself. The
  rule now: what a loop that keeps heap facts may write is the union over its own operations
  and every frame the build may inline into it, at every depth, from a summary each inlined
  chunk carries; and the verifier states it on the built graph (a loop whose header kept
  facts holds, in any frame, no effect and no store that could end one; its classification
  of nodes has no default arm). A census over every workload found no loop that loses a fact
  to the rule: the class never occurred in them. The older facts of step 0c were safe by two
  properties nobody had written down (the planner inlines no callee that stores a free name;
  a frame that calls is generic); neither is relied on any more.
- The entry checks' reason. A hoisted copy of the read's own check keeps the read's reason
  and teacher, so that after the global object's layout changes once the recompile hoists
  again; but only where the read ran since the compile: a read on a path the loop no longer
  takes is marked not to be hoisted, alone. That is Ion's distinction (an LICM bailout that a
  baseline fallback confirms is the guard's own; otherwise LICM is off for the script,
  BaselineBailouts.cpp:2061-2084), narrowed from the script to the read. The coordinator's
  first decision (always the read's reason) cost a blind read one exit cycle per layout
  change (56,100 exits over 300 changes against none); its own condition found that.
- DeltaBlue read +0.23% at graph at 200 runs with the union rule and is not its doing: the
  generated code is the same word for word, and with collections deferred the row is +0.004%.
  It is the conservative scan's draw between two builds, as TypeScript's modes are
  (benchmarks/README.md has the control since).
- Checks of the two pieces together, on the stack they landed as: the coordinator's 53
  programs at graph, at the splice and under GC stress; the 5-run verifier sweep (the rule
  examined 258 loops whose headers kept facts); the tier's modules in six configurations
  (726 each); the whole library suite with the tier off and on (3,714 each); none failed.

The Splay retention fix's confirmation under GC stress, ended (2026-11-19 12:21;
target/p3/stage3/step3g-impl/stress-i and stepsplay-review/stress-rest.log). The tier's modules
(opt:: with jit::opt_a64, the traced and inline-context tests, 651 tests) on the fix's test
binary with TRUST_JS_GC_STRESS=1 and an exit forced every 100 checks: at graph all 651 passed,
398 in the first six-hour window and the other 253 run by name without a limit (15 hours:
three of the inliner's tests take about 70 minutes each in that configuration, and the
by-literal ones longer); at the splice 589 passed and none failed in two six-hour windows, and
the last 62 were not reached. With it the deoptimizer's call windows of step 3g's record
(report-splay.txt 4(b)) are shown at graph, where they were marked inferred. A cost worth
recording: a test build verifies the heap at every collection, so a test that allocates in a
loop of thousands of turns runs for an hour or more under GC stress, and the tests of
by-literal frames are the slowest (one has never finished in six hours); the stress leg of a
change's check should name the tests it runs.

The register allocator's rule B landed (d6722c0e; 2026-11-19; target/p3/stage3/behind-impl,
proposal.txt, rule-b-text.txt and report-rule-b.txt, the agents'; the coordinator's checks
target/p3/stage3/regb-review). It came out of the question where graph was behind the splice.
Crypto's +0.66% was one reload an iteration of its hottest loops: an inlined frame's callee
and receiver are read by nothing in the loop but its exit states, the allocator counted a
state's read as a read, and with every register held it spilled a value the loop does read.
The same pattern, with no inlining involved, sat in zlib's and Mandreel's loops in both modes.
- The rule. The allocator gives a value one home for its whole range, so the victim is the
  held value whose whole range is cheapest in memory (Ion's spill weight: its register reads
  and its definition's store, each weighed by loop depth, over the range's length), where a
  state's read weighs nothing (TurboFan's frame-state inputs and Ion's snapshot uses claim no
  register either), and a value that the loop around the choice has already read in a
  register this turn is never the victim (Maglev's rule for loops). The commit's message has
  it exactly as coded, with the citations.
- Measured against main's engine (fat LTO; the coordinator's builds target/p2/lto/8edf64c4
  and 8aa28538; instructions at 200 fixed runs, graph | splice, each row the same with
  collections deferred): zlib -3.31% | -3.31%, Mandreel -2.91% | -2.90%, Crypto -1.44% |
  -0.75%, Richards -1.01% | -1.87%, Earley-Boyer -0.38% | -0.26%, DeltaBlue -0.28% | -0.07%,
  Box2D -0.24% | -0.20%; the rest within 0.17%, none slower beyond 0.07%. Cycles on one X925
  core, five rounds: zlib and Mandreel at 5 runs -5.4% | -5.3% and -5.1% | -5.3%, Crypto -5.1%
  | -3.9%, Richards -1.2% | -3.3%, DeltaBlue -1.1% | -1.2%, React's steady state -4.2% at
  graph. The gain in cycles is several times the gain in instructions: a reload is a load
  that waits on a store.
- The forms tried, each measured on the whole table (the agents' p1 to p5): the next register
  read alone, and the weight alone, cost Richards +1.3% to +1.4% instructions and +6% cycles
  at graph (values read early in the scheduler's loop turn and then held for exits became
  victims, and with one home their earlier reads turned into loads); the loop term alone cost
  the Vue fixture +0.41% and a third of the gain; the term over every enclosing loop cost
  Crypto +5.4%. The recorded limit: a static weight sees loop depth and not how often a block
  runs; Richards still pays two stores and two loads a task turn for two values read on
  conditional paths.
- Tests. Twenty-three existing tests of the allocator's step R1 had setups that pinned their
  victims by the old rule (the whole library suite at graph and with the tier on was the net
  that found the seven outside the allocator's own module). Each is set up again under the
  weight and catches every mutation it caught before, the ported mutations first shown
  faithful on the parent. One of R1's mutations had been caught by nothing since an earlier
  change made its test vacuous; the rewritten test catches it. The coordinator's 75
  adversarial programs agree at four modes and under GC stress at graph and at the splice
  (an allocator change moves every value's home, and the collector finds values by their
  homes); the 5-run verifier sweep is clean.
- Not built, on this evidence: the guarded way's callee as a constant of its frame (V8's and
  JSC's form). After rule B its remaining reach is at most 0.005% of any workload, and it
  would need a new rooting rule (running code keeping its embedded callees alive).
- The flip's rows on this engine (graph against the splice, instructions at 200 runs):
  DeltaBlue -16.2%, Richards -9.6%, Box2D -2.2%, TypeScript -1.2% (not read by the mutator's
  instructions here), Earley-Boyer -1.0%, RayTrace -0.4%, Mandreel -0.4%, Crypto -0.22%
  (it was +0.66%), PdfJS -0.1%, zlib, RegExp, Navier-Stokes and CodeLoad level; behind: GBEMU
  +0.17% and Splay +0.11% with the marker off. Both remaining gaps are the context switches
  graph makes around operations it has not lowered in inlined frames (the agents' attribution:
  Splay's are stores that mix replacing and creating a property, which are generic in both
  modes; GBEMU's are spread over property reads). The follow-on pieces by predicted gain at
  graph: the lazy switch for property reads in inlined frames (DeltaBlue 1.15%, Vue's steady
  state 0.67%, TypeScript 0.43%), the lowered mixed store (Splay 0.82%, the one that also
  gains at the splice), the intrinsic call as a node (DeltaBlue 0.32%).
- The owner's decision of the same day stands on this: the flip follows rule B's landing.

The quiet loop's facts, first commit, landed (the carry; 2026-11-19; target/p3/stage3/
loopfacts-impl, report.txt, and select-impl/design-note-loopfacts.txt and cause-note.txt, the
agents'; the coordinator's quick check target/p3/stage3/carry-review). A loop header kept no
shape, field or prototype-link fact, so a loop that cannot change a shape checked its receivers
again every iteration (the count behind it: select-impl/loopfacts). A loop that keeps heap
facts now keeps, through its header, those facts of the objects its operations take: the
values of the slots it reads and does not write, the frame's own `this` where the loop reads
it, and what their kept fields and links hold. A creation by any frame in the loop drops the
shape facts and a store at a location drops that field, by the union rule of F4 part 1, which
the verifier states on the graph (it now refuses a creation where shapes were kept). Maglev
keeps the known maps and loaded properties its loop's effects leave (CloneForLoopHeader,
maglev-known-node-aspects.cc:647-735); Ion's and the DFG's LICM move a shape guard out of a
loop that writes none.
- Measured against main with rule B (fat LTO, the coordinator's builds; instructions at 200
  runs with collections deferred, graph | splice): Box2D -0.63% | -0.60%, Crypto -0.50% |
  -0.46%, Richards -0.49% | -0.44%, GBEMU -0.14% | -0.14%; at 5 runs Crypto -0.37%, Box2D
  -0.36%, GBEMU -0.16%; the tier off equal. The agent's cycles: Box2D -0.76%, Crypto -0.35%,
  Richards -0.21%; GBEMU +0.45% at fewer instructions, in the engine's native code and not
  the tier's (the typed-array buffer accessor and fmod carry it; the cause, the binary's
  layout, is inferred). TypeScript at 5 runs reads +0.7% plain and -0.03% with collections
  deferred: the collector's draw.
- Decided on the way. Only the facts of the objects the loop takes are kept: keeping every
  fact the loop leaves intact, as Maglev does, gives Richards a further -0.20% at 200 runs
  and costs it +0.15% at 5, because this builder's states are copied at joins; recorded for
  the piece on facts that joins drop. Inside such loops every check this removes had already
  lost its tag test, so it takes over part of what the tag test's count predicted.
- Checked: 18 mutations caught; the agents' 16 adversarial programs and the coordinator's
  (72 in all) agree at four modes; the verifier sweep; the tier's modules in six
  configurations (the agent's run, before the owner's word on quick checks).
- Next of this line, planned and measured before any code (loopfacts-impl/NEXT.txt): the
  first check of a loop-invariant object moved to the loop's entry edge (Crypto's other 70%,
  about -0.5% more predicted). Its blind case costs one exit cycle a loop and no more. Where
  the rest of the repeated shape checks are: in loops that call (Richards 75%, DeltaBlue 97%,
  RayTrace 66%), which need the dependencies the owner approved, and outside any loop
  (TypeScript 70%, Box2D 23%), where a join drops the fact.

F4 part 2, layer 1 landed: each realm's global object keeps its properties' states (ed6213c0;
2026-11-19; target/p3/stage3/f4p2-impl, design-note.txt with its "AFTER REVIEW" section and
report-layer1.txt, the agents'; the coordinator's check target/p3/stage3/f4p2-review). The
first of four layers of the dependencies the owner approved that morning (question 7). It adds
cost and no gain by itself; it lands alone, as the review asked (R-4), so that its cost in the
default configuration is on record apart from what the tier later gains on it.
- What it is. A one-way state for each named property of a realm's global object: untracked,
  constant (stored once), not constant (SpiderMonkey's ObjectFuse rule; V8's property cell
  types and JSC's watchpoint states hold the same information). Every path that can change a
  global's property reports to it (the note's writers W1 to W11), and every cache or
  generated-code fill that would store to a global's slot in line refuses a slot that is not
  yet "not constant" (fills C1 to C6), so that the second store of a property is always seen.
  A watched global's shape is its own and never shared (rules U1 to U4), as the exemplars'
  global is an object whose map no other object has: a store way filled through one realm's
  global can never hit another's.
- The first form (19504331) cost too much and was sent back: the fixtures +0.07% to +0.15%
  with the tier off, a VM store to a global +250 to +800 instructions. The rework: a
  property's state is read without a lock (atomic words, buffers never freed while the fuse
  lives); a fill reaches the active realm's fuse through the realm, the report through a
  thread-local, with the registry only for another realm's global; a site whose cache
  already holds the current shape and slot asks nothing. The larger part of the first form's
  cost was not the locks: closure and literal instantiation recomputed a copy's shape at every
  copy (rule U3's path), found with SPE.
- Measured, the coordinator's builds (fat LTO, target/p2/lto/ed6213c0 against main's
  b7514ddc; 5 fixed runs, collections deferred), the tier off: Richards +0.046% (a fixed
  cost: the engine's setup, +10K instructions on an empty script, and its 22 globals' first
  stores), React's fixture and steady state +0.034% and +0.036% (ten randomized runs a side
  agree: +0.036%, standard error 0.000), CodeLoad +0.030%, Djot +0.02%, DeltaBlue and Crypto
  +0.01%, GBEMU -0.07%, the rest within 0.01%; Octane's mean +0.005%. Vue read +0.09% at
  fixed addresses and +0.015% and -0.023% (standard errors 0.021 and 0.029) in ten randomized
  runs a side: nothing. The tier on (graph): the same pattern, Octane's mean +0.009%.
  The agent's store micro, instructions an iteration for a property that is not constant:
  compiled code unchanged (name 85, alias 61, update 79, script 372); the VM 0, -1, +22, +26;
  the tree-walker +47, +51, +94 and, through `o.x = v` on the global, +169.
- Checked: the coordinator's 66 adversarial programs (eight new ones for a global's store
  paths, each agreeing with Node first: deletes and re-additions, attribute changes, accessor
  and back, implicit globals made in a loop, eval's declarations, copies from the global, one
  store site meeting the global and plain objects, a global made non-extensible, `with`) agree
  at four modes and with main's engine; the layer's tests (43) with the tier off and on; the
  verifier sweep; the whole library suite with the tier off, 3,766 passed, none failed, 10 ignored (this layer changes
  every program's store path); the agent's 18 mutations, all caught, and zombie's check and
  clippy.
- Left open, the next agent's first work (briefs/f4-part2-resume.txt):
  1. Rule U2's test moves from every property addition to the transition lookup's miss path
     (a watched map's shape is unique, so its lookup never hits); the two tests in the
     named-data copy that can never be true become debug assertions (React's +0.036%).
  2. The tree-walker's generic store reports twice; the census's counters leave the release
     store path; a lookup by text on the watched global costs about 150 instructions more
     there, cause not found (suspected: its additions take no shared layout).
  3. The coordinator's review asks one hardening (R-5): the thread-local's raw pointer to the
     active realm's fuse is cleared by the fuse's own drop only; the engine's teardown clears
     it too, before its heap goes.
  4. One fixture copy compiles one more function with the layer (Djot's steady state):
     unexplained.
- Next: layer 2 (the dependents' lists, a validity word a code tests, the exit reason), layer
  3 (the tier folds a constant global's read: the rows), layer 4 (a typed array's data and
  length through a folded global); then the dependency on a buffer not being detached, V8's
  engine-wide form.

The graph line after the flip: what inlined frames pay for operations the tier does not
lower, counted and decided (2026-11-19; target/p3/stage3/gnext-impl: design-note.txt,
exemplars.txt, count-table.txt, template-reads.txt, the agent's; no engine code yet).
- The cost. An operation of an inlined callee that the tier does not lower runs the callee's
  first-stage template with the callee's chunk and call site written first and the caller's
  restored after (16 instructions; 21 with `this`, 25 with the slot window). At graph that
  eager switch is 0.86% of Splay's instructions, 0.85% of DeltaBlue's, 0.64% of Vue's steady
  state, 0.41% of TypeScript's, 0.30% of Djot's steady state, 0.23% of GBEMU's, 0.20% of
  RayTrace's (200 fixed runs; a probe build's counts).
- The exemplars pay nothing like it on a fast path. Their generic operation takes its inputs
  explicitly (Maglev's LoadNamedGeneric calls the LoadIC builtin with context and receiver,
  maglev-ir.cc:5295-5310; the DFG's generic GetById is a data IC on the base register,
  DFGSpeculativeJIT64.cpp:161-194), and the inlined frame's identity is found only when
  something asks: from the return address in V8 and SpiderMonkey (frames.cc:3441-3462;
  JSJitFrameIter.cpp:319-323), from one store made before a call out in JSC
  (DFGSpeculativeJIT.h:1078-1097; an IC's fast path stores nothing,
  JITInlineCacheGenerator.cpp:193-205).
- Decided, piece 1: the lazy switch for every kind whose template has a machine-code path,
  with the receiver of the kinds that read `this` or a slot passed as an operand word (a form
  of the template only the tier asks for), and the frame's context written only around the
  template's helper calls: JSC's form, which steps B2 and B2b already build for calls and a
  few kinds. Predicted from the per-site helper counts (a site's template reaches its helper
  on nearly none of its executions or on all of them): DeltaBlue -0.85%, Splay -0.83%, Vue's
  steady state -0.31%, Djot's -0.24%, TypeScript -0.24%, GBEMU -0.22%, RayTrace -0.20%; the
  rule as it stood (this and the window kept eager) would leave a third of Splay's and Vue's
  behind. The kinds whose template is a helper call alone are not admitted.
- Decided, piece 2: the polymorphic named store (Splay's mixed store widened: own data ways at
  different slots and creation ways in one site, up to four shapes; Maglev's polymorphic
  access with transitioning arms, maglev-graph-builder.cc:5775 and :4380-4382; JSC's
  MultiPutByOffset, DFGByteCodeParser.cpp:7994-7997). It gains in both modes: 56M executions
  in GBEMU, 17M in Box2D, 1.6M in Splay at 200 runs; a micro reads the generic store at 41 to
  67 instructions an execution over a lowered one at the splice and 65 to 91 at graph. The
  agent's estimate from it: Splay about -3%, Box2D -2%, GBEMU -0.8% (inferred).
- After those, by reach, each with its own note: GetMethodElem as the element load it is
  (GBEMU 188M executions in compiled frames), InstanceOf (Earley-Boyer 100M), Array.prototype
  method loads (key-checked ways: PdfJS 27M, TypeScript 11M), absent arms, `prototype`,
  accessors as calls, growing element stores, and the intrinsic call's section switch as
  intrinsics lowered to nodes (DeltaBlue 0.34%, Vue's steady state 0.27%, Box2D 0.19%).
- What a lazy switch cannot help: sites whose template calls its helper every time
  (TypeScript's keyed reads with non-index keys, 67M at 200 runs; Mandreel's megamorphic named
  read, 7.8M). All three exemplars probe an engine-wide megamorphic cache in line (Maglev's
  LoadIC_Megamorphic, the DFG's compileGetByIdMegamorphic, Warp's MegamorphicLoadSlot). Not
  counted yet in what a helper call costs there: a line of its own, not started.
- Also found, not started (inferred, to count): sites that look megamorphic because their
  caches keep warm-up or stale ways (GBEMU's this.x reads: 14.8M of 18.1M executions are one
  object's earlier shapes; DeltaBlue's refused method loads keep a stale walk). Maglev derives
  an access from the current maps and drops deprecated ones (maglev-graph-builder.cc:
  4693-4708).
- The owner's open question from step 3c (JSC's constant stores around a helper call, or
  V8's and SpiderMonkey's return-address tables) now has its bound: after piece 1 the stores
  cost at most 0.29% of any workload (Vue's fixture), on paths whose helper call costs far
  more. Recommendation: keep JSC's form; ask again when the megamorphic paths no longer call
  a helper every time.

The quiet loop's facts: the blocks the builder builds, and the first shape check moved to the
loop's entry, landed (e96aa0eb and 632b3cc2; 2026-11-19; target/p3/stage3/lf3-impl, report.txt,
the agent's; the coordinator's check target/p3/stage3/lf3-review).
- How the first came about. The planned move alone measured Crypto -0.22% at 200 runs against
  a count that predicted -0.66%. The loop analysis (Flow::new) counted the operations of blocks
  that never ran as effects: such a block has no feedback, so nothing in it is lowered. The
  builder never builds those blocks; each is its NeverRan exit. Crypto's two hottest loops
  (montReduce, bnpSquareTo) were therefore "not quiet" for code the compiled function does not
  contain, and so were Mandreel's loops with a call on a rare path, which is the case F4 part
  1's hoist was written for.
- The rule (e96aa0eb). One predicate, Rules::exits_unbuilt (the block never ran and holds no
  NeverRan flag), is read by the builder, the loop analysis, the summaries of an inlined
  callee's writes and the inliner, each with its frame's own feedback: a block the builder
  makes an exit ends none of its loop's facts. JSC's ForceOSRExit for code that never ran
  leaves the rest of its block unreachable before LICM collects the loop's writes
  (dfg/DFGByteCodeParser.cpp:1330), Warp's bailout for a cold IC ends its block
  (jit/WarpBuilder.cpp:4185-4190), Maglev's unconditional deopt builds nothing after it
  (maglev-graph-builder.cc:1678). It is its own commit because it widens three landed pieces
  (the loop's facts, the typed entry checks, the global-read hoist) in every program. When the
  cold block does run, the exit, the discard and the recompile are main's own (four programs,
  both modes, equal counts); the recompile builds the block and its loop keeps no fact the
  block ends.
- The move (632b3cc2). In a loop that keeps heap facts, the first shape check of a word the
  loop does not write (a slot it reads, `this`, an entry-loaded global) whose entry edges hold
  no fact is made at the entry edge, with the moved-site records step L1c gave typed checks:
  a site that runs whenever the loop is entered is taught WrongShape at its own pc, a blind
  one flags HoistingFailed at the header. Ion's and the DFG's LICM move a shape guard out of a
  loop that writes none. Always wrong, measured: 100 exits, 1 discard, 2 compiles against the
  unmoved 0, 0, 1 for a site on a path taken only in warm-up; a shape no record saw at every
  entry costs what the site's own check costs.
- Measured, the coordinator's builds against main's engine (fat LTO, target/p2/lto/ed6213c0;
  graph; 200 fixed runs, collections deferred; the first commit | the move over it | the
  pair): Mandreel -10.62% | 0.00% | -10.62%, Crypto -1.48% | -0.29% | -1.76%, Box2D -0.01% |
  -0.09% | -0.10%, GBEMU 0.00% | -0.08% | -0.08%, Richards 0.00% | 0.00% | 0.00%. At 5 runs
  the first commit: Mandreel -5.09%, Crypto -1.26%, Octane's mean -0.43%; the move: Crypto
  -0.22%. The splice does not gain (Mandreel -0.04%, Crypto -0.05% at 5 runs): its spliced
  second stage has no block that "never ran". The tier off equal.
- Slower, accepted: Richards at 5 runs +0.015% at graph and +0.024% at the splice for the
  first commit (the agent's 1-run rows: +0.03% to +0.05% for the pair), a fixed cost of the
  compile (the analysis asks the predicate per block; the builder's states carry the facts at
  headers) that 200 runs do not show (+0.002%). The exit asked Richards within its spread
  (0.012%) at 1 and 5 runs; it is over it by that much and lands on the pair's gains.
  The agent's rows against an older parent showed Djot's fixture +0.09% and +0.17%: against
  main's engine it is within 0.01%, the layout draw the flip's record describes.
- Checked: the coordinator's 89 adversarial programs (six new ones in which a cold block
  first runs in a loop's middle iteration after the compile: reassigning the globals the loop
  reads, growing the object whose shape it carries, storing and deleting its carried fields,
  calling and throwing, the same inside an inlined callee and on `this`; each agreeing with
  Node first) agree on both commits at five modes (bytecode, the tier off, graph, the splice,
  graph eager); the verifier sweep at 5 runs; the agent's mutations (4 of 4 and 6 of 6: a
  prepass that skips a block the builder builds is refused by the verifier's kept_heap), its
  tests at graph, at the splice and eager, and zombie's check.
- Out of this form's reach in Crypto, each with its count (the report's section 1): a check
  behind an inner loop's header (3.75M executions; a header that also keeps its enclosing
  quiet loop's facts), an inlined frame's site on the caller's invariant argument (7.5M; a
  prepass that sees a callee's sites on a parameter bound to the caller's slot), and the shape
  of a word loaded in the loop (`this.m`, 4.99M; the field's own fact). c-rec's recount was
  not done.

Validation after the landings of 2026-11-19 (main 27c54229: rule B, the loop's facts with the
never-ran rule and the moved check, the flip, layer 1 of the global dependencies; run in the

- Test262, whole (annexB, built-ins, harness, intl402, language, staging), the tier on and

- What these cost, for planning: a release runner built from an export in under a minute,

  landing's quick check. The whole library suite is the expensive check (18 to 25 minutes a
