Property operations that call a helper on every execution: counted, and what was found in it
(2026-11-19; target/p3/stage3/mega-impl: design-note.txt, exemplars.txt, count-table.txt,
stubcheck/, the agent's; the coordinator's micro target/p3/stage3/getter-impl/micro; no engine
code yet).
- The count (a probe build's executions, perf on main's fat LTO for the shares; graph | the
  tier off, share of the workload's instructions in the four property helpers and their
  stubs): GBEMU 45.1% | 27.2%, TypeScript 23.0% | 22.9%, Vue's steady state 21.9% | 17.2%
  (its fixture 19.2% | 17.2%), Djot's steady state 3.8% | 4.1%, PdfJS 3.1% | 4.7%, Box2D 2.4%
  | 1.2%, Mandreel 1.6% | 0.3%, React 0. These are costs of the default configuration as much
  as of the tier.
- FOUND: a built-in accessor getter costs about 830 instructions a read in every tier. The
  coordinator's micro (instructions an iteration, the tier off | graph): a typed array's
  length 834 | 833, its byteLength 821 | 820, a Map's size 918 | 916, ta.buffer.byteLength
  1,785 | 1,790, beside an array's length 107 | 17, a string's 39 | 17, a plain object's own
  `length` 52 | 18. The read takes the generic [[Get]] each time; no site caches it and the
  tier does not lower it. GBEMU reads a typed array's length 217M times in 200 runs: that is
  all of its 45% | 27%. Mandreel's 1.6% is the same, and so is Earley-Boyer's
  `arguments.length` (0.3%). The exemplars cache an accessor read at the site and call the
  getter directly, and make the typed array's getters field loads (SpiderMonkey in the IC
  stub, CacheIR.cpp:2494 and :11917-11957; JSC's IntrinsicGetter case and the DFG's
  GetArrayLength, InlineCacheCompiler.cpp:4388, DFGByteCodeParser.cpp:6207-6225; TurboFan
  reduces the getter, js-call-reducer.cc:8749). A line of its own from 15:43
  (briefs/getter.txt, evidence getter-impl): the facts first (why a JS getter has a direct
  path in the template's stub and a native one has none; what a host's accessor on a
  prototype costs an embedder), then the site's way for an accessor on the chain, the typed
  array's getters in line, and the tier's nodes.
- TypeScript's 23%: 14.9% is named stores that create a property at megamorphic sites (127M
  at about 1,140 instructions each) or on maps of more than 8 entries (100M; the template's
  in-line creation refuses those maps); string-keyed reads 3.2%; megamorphic named reads 2.9%
  (108M, each a stub-cache hit found by Rust code at about 560 instructions; the cache's hit
  rate is 99.3%); string-keyed stores 1.6%, which have no cache at all.
- Vue's 22%: 11% is the template's get_prop stub calling user getters directly (71% of the
  stub's samples) and running Proxy get traps (28%), with two tables' slot-probe loops its
  hottest instructions (verified by annotation; not yet counted by call); 5.5% is absent
  named reads on Arrays (`__v_isRef` and its kin: 0.53M at 988 instructions each, which the
  cache's fill refuses to remember across an Array level); 3.1% string-keyed reads.
- The exemplars' form for the megamorphic part: one engine-wide cache keyed by receiver shape
  and unique name, probed by machine code for named and keyed reads, stores with transitions
  and `in` (V8's StubCache and its builtins, ic/stub-cache.h:38-93,
  ic/accessor-assembler.cc:3244-3315; JSC's MegamorphicCache probed in line by the DFG and by
  the baseline handler after 8 cases, runtime/MegamorphicCache.h:35-320; SpiderMonkey's
  MegamorphicCache and MegamorphicSetPropCache behind a runtime-wide trampoline,
  vm/Caches.h:156-366, jit/CodeGenerator.cpp:4916-4970). This engine has the cache
  (Interp::stub_cache) and only Rust probes it.
- Decided by the coordinator (briefs/mega-build.txt), building from 15:49: first the Array's
  absent read cached for a name that cannot be an index (one fill rule; Vue's steady state
  -5.1% predicted), then the cache with its key inside the entry, probed by a process-wide
  shared stub that the template's slow path and the tier's generic node call before the Rust
  helper: reads, then stores with creation entries, then string-keyed sites and `in`.
  Predicted (inferred from micro costs): TypeScript -14.9% at graph and -11.4% with the tier
  off, Djot's steady state -2.5%, Vue's -1.5%. After those, each on its own note: creations
  on large maps in line (TypeScript -3.3%, Box2D -1.9%), a string's length and character in
  the template (PdfJS -1.7%), stale ways retired.
- With the owner, the recommendation built on meanwhile (the handoff's questions 12 to 14):
  string constants carry their key from the chunk's making (all three engines make literals
  unique names); a prototype hit in the cache keeps the per-hop shape walk, a departure,
  because a shape here does not fix its prototype where V8's map, JSC's structure and
  SpiderMonkey's shape do; stale ways retired by V8's in-place overwrite and SpiderMonkey's
  reset at a major collection.
- Stale ways, counted: negligible in the template (it refills at each miss; its helper calls
  are at sites with more than four live shapes), real for the tier, which refuses to lower a
  site whose record keeps dead shapes (GBEMU 17.3M inlined generic loads at sites that are
  monomorphic today; DeltaBlue's stale walks).

F4 part 2, layer 1's residuals landed (4464ea9b; 2026-11-19; target/p3/stage3/f4p2-impl,
report-partA.txt, the agent's; the coordinator's check target/p3/stage3/parta-review).
- Rule U2 (a watched global's every shape is a fresh serial, no transition entry read or
  written for it) keeps its meaning and loses its cost: the test for "watched" runs only
  where a transition lookup misses, which is where a watched map always arrives, and a hit
  asserts in debug builds that the map is not watched. The exemplars' globals: V8's is a
  dictionary-mode object with a map of its own (factory.cc:3524-3533; additions change no
  map, js-objects.cc:2991-3008); JSC's becomes a cacheable dictionary at its creation
  (JSGlobalObject.cpp:1019, JSObject.cpp:4138-4143); SpiderMonkey's is not created a
  dictionary, its shape unique because the base shape holds class and realm
  (Shape.h:238-247).
- The named-data copy is back to its code before layer 1, with debug assertions for the two
  facts that made its watched tests dead.
- Corrected from layer 1's record: the tree-walker's generic store did not report twice
  (103,013 reports for 103,000 stores); the cost was the census's counters, which now count
  only under TRUST_JS_OPT_LOG. And the lookup that read 150 instructions slower on the watched
  global was a draw of one build pair, not a cost: the key index hashes an atom's address, so
  its probe length differs between binaries; between the coordinator's builds the two read
  the same.
- R-5, the review's hardening: an engine's teardown clears the thread's pointer to its
  global's fuse before its heap goes. Found on the way: with two engines alternating on a
  thread, the engine that was not last installed took the registry's lock on every reported
  store; an entry that switches the thread's Agent now installs that engine's fuse. A first
  form that installed at every entry cost Djot's steady state +0.16% (every native call's
  return passes there) and was replaced. Left, correct and slow: an engine's code run inside
  a host's raw-access guard entered while another engine was active reports through the
  registry for the guard's length; the form that ends it is SpiderMonkey's, the fuses kept
  with the heap (Watchtower.cpp:760), which the next agent builds if its reading holds.
- Measured, the coordinator's builds (fat LTO, target/p2/lto/4464ea9b against 632b3cc2; 5
  fixed runs, collections deferred; the tier off): React's fixture and steady state -0.021%
  and -0.028% (layer 1 had cost them +0.034% and +0.036%), Vue +0.009% and -0.019%, Djot
  +0.016% and +0.022%, Richards +0.016%, RegExp +0.047% (an addition to the global costs
  about 50 instructions more: the miss path's lookups come before the test), the rest within
  0.011%; Octane's mean +0.007%. Layer 1 and its residuals together are level on the fixtures.
- Checked: 53 adversarial programs at six modes (the tree-walker among them) and against
  main's engine; the agent's seven mutations, its 74 nearest tests with the tier off, at
  graph and at the splice, zombie's check, and a whole-library run with the tier off on the
  commit's first form (3,779 passed; the one failure is the loop-cache test of the next
  record, which fails on main without this commit).

A test broken by the loop's facts, found a landing later and fixed forward (ce0e88f8;
2026-11-19).
- opt::tier_tests::loop_cache::objects_cached_through_the_inner_loop_survive_collections_in_
  the_outer_loops_call failed on main from 632b3cc2 (the first shape check moved to a quiet
  loop's entry) until this commit. No miscompile: its results equalled the VM's in all six
  configurations. Its setup's assertion, that the allocation made a loop cache, no longer
  held: the inner loop read twelve objects' fields, and with the check at the entry edge and
  the field loads reading the cell carried from there the loop reads no object. Measured on
  main, caches made in each configuration: the old loop 0; a loop that stores to the fields 0
  (the store goes through the carried cell); identity tests of the objects 0 (folded); a loop
  that stores each object into an array's element 8; one that selects between them 7.
- The test now stores the objects into an array in its inner loop and asserts at graph (the
  new program makes no cache in the configurations that follow the environment under
  TRUST_JS_OPT_INLINE=splice; why the spliced second stage needs none is not traced). The
  allocator mutations it caught stay caught by the other loop-cache tests.
- How it got through: the pair landed on its agent's nearest tests (the builder's modules at
  graph, the new tests at graph, at the splice and eager), the coordinator's adversarial
  programs, counts and the verifier sweep. None of them runs this module with the tier's
  switches left to the test. Test262 and difftest, run after the landing, do not see a test's
  setup. A whole-library run of the next change with the tier off found it.
- The check that would have caught it costs ten minutes: the tier's modules (opt::,
  jit::opt_a64) in ONE configuration, TRUST_JS_OPT=1 with the inline mode left to the
  default. The coordinator's landing check of a tier change runs it from here on, beside the
  counts; the whole library suite stays for changes to every program's default path.

The graph line's piece 1 landed: the lazy context switch with explicit inputs (844667b7;
2026-11-19; target/p3/stage3/gnext-impl, report-p1.txt and design-note.txt, the agent's; the
coordinator's check target/p3/stage3/g1-review).
- The rule. An inlined frame's generic node of an admitted kind writes before its template
  only its operand words; a kind whose template read its receiver from a fixed place (`this`,
  a slot of the window) reads it from an operand word in the form the tier asks for; the
  frame's chunk, call site, `this` word and the window's description are written only around
  the template's helper calls and restored there. JSC's form (a store before a call out and
  nowhere else, DFGSpeculativeJIT.h:1078-1097), with the receiver an input as Maglev's and
  the DFG's generic nodes take it. The tier off's templates are unchanged (every receiver
  load goes through one function, jit.rs emit_receiver_load). Admitted: GetProp, GetMethod,
  GetPropThis, GetPropLocal, SetPropDrop, SetPropThisDrop, SetPropLocalDrop,
  SetElemLocalDrop, GetMethodElem, InstanceOf, Add, ToStr, MakeObject, MakeArray,
  MakeArrayLiteral. Not admitted: the kinds whose generic sites reach the helper on every
  execution (GetElem, GetElemLocal, SetElemDrop, the relationals) and those whose template
  is a helper call alone.
- Measured, the coordinator's builds against main's engine (fat LTO, target/p2/lto/6516b34a
  against 632b3cc2; graph; 200 fixed runs, collections deferred), with the count's
  prediction: DeltaBlue -0.86% (-0.85%), Splay -1.99% (the agent's row with the marker off
  and collections not deferred: -0.82%, predicted -0.83%), GBEMU -0.22% (-0.22%), RayTrace
  -0.20% (-0.20%), Crypto -0.09% (-0.09%), Box2D -0.04% (-0.06%). At 5 runs: Splay -0.66%,
  Vue's fixture -0.29% and steady state -0.23%, Djot's steady state -0.21%, RayTrace -0.10%,
  Octane's mean -0.09%. The splice and the tier off equal within the draws of two binaries
  (the agent's code comparison: the tier off's and the splice's generated code differ only
  in how many instructions a heap address takes to materialize). The agent's GBEMU row at 5
  runs, +0.04% against an older parent, reads -0.07% against main's engine.
- Checked: the coordinator's 87 adversarial programs at five modes and again under GC stress
  at graph (the piece changes what a helper path tells the collector): none differs; the
  tier's modules with the tier on on the landing tree, 766 passed and none failed; the
  verifier sweep; the agent's eight mutations, its new tests of each helper path from an
  inlined frame (a getter, a setter, a Proxy receiver, a null receiver's error and stack,
  valueOf, Symbol.hasInstance), zombie's check.
